# ROADMAP.md: Engine-Builder

**One-liner:** a browser engine and editor for the games RCJ Labs makes. You put in records, missions, scripts, worlds and screens, and the engine does the rest. It runs on a PC, tablet or phone and ships as a PWA or a Play TWA.

**CURRENT MILESTONE: M7.3.** Side-view rooms: floors with room slots, stairs and route-finding, in the 2D spaces M7.1 made. Phase 6 waits only on your phone: open [`engine.rcjlabs.com/games/kaldvik/`](https://engine.rcjlabs.com/games/kaldvik/), set Quality to Low and then Medium in its settings, and walk the shore, the steading and the howe for 30 fps (see Phase 6's Done when). Phase 4 waits only on your phone: open [`engine.rcjlabs.com/games/space-wrecked/`](https://engine.rcjlabs.com/games/space-wrecked/) held sideways, play from the hangar to Launch and a few jumps, then install it and open it in flight mode (see M4.12 below). Phase 5 waits only on your phone too: its frames a second at each tier, the trail, the glen and the moor walked, and a hut snapped together in the Scene panel (see Phase 5's Done when). M7.2 (Tilemaps) is done: a tileset is an engine record type (tiles with their characters and rules, frames for edges), and a tilemap is a component of a 2D space: layers of characters, autotiled by a 4-bit mask, with walls, the hull, doors and hatches on the edges in Space-Wrecked's own map text. Sight and cover are the facts `world.sees` and `world.cover`, and the walkers the sim sends route four ways round the walls, all worked out in the pure core from pack format 18. The Scene panel paints tiles and edges, shows each cell's rules and imports a map as text. The demo is Space-Wrecked's example ship as a boarding deck, imported from its own text and drawn with a starter tileset made in code, untested for feel until you see it on your phone ([specs/tilemaps.md](docs/specs/tilemaps.md#as-built-m72)). M7.1 (2D scenes) is done: a 2D space is a space with `view: "2d"`, drawn in whole art pixels at the largest whole scale its fit allows, on Canvas 2D rather than three.js (D83's As built). Sprites come from grid sheets with named clips, parallax is a component, figures walk in straight lines to the sim's marks, and a use is a button over its sprite. stage2d.js draws it in play and exports, and the Scene panel edits it in a 2D view of its own. The demo is a keep at night from a starter sprite kit made in code, untested for feel until you see it on your phone ([specs/2d-scenes.md](docs/specs/2d-scenes.md#as-built-m71)). M6.10 (Proof #2) is done: Kaldvík, a steading on Landnam's coast, deployed at `/games/kaldvik/`: the shore's terrain and three interiors, five townsfolk on their days, three quests, outlaws to fight and two keepers to trade with. An `essential` actor goes down at no health and gets up at full health 10 seconds later, with no drops and no body (E090, the fighting kit's slice version 2). Its bot brings the silver home in most runs and reaches every stage of every quest; it is walked and fought through Rapier and Recast in Node, and its export is played by keys to the ending and by touch at 390 and sideways ([specs/kaldvik.md](docs/specs/kaldvik.md#as-built-m610)). M6.9 (Templates) is done: New project offers the valley hamlet ("Open-world RPG", third person: Osric's job against three bandits, Wenna's shop and her radiant supplies job, a cave a door away) and the lighthouse ("First-person adventure": the logbook in ink, the keeper's chest, the locked store, the lamp). Each is a lazy chunk with its models and navmeshes inlined. Bots play walked games through a headless stage (`walk`, or `walk+fight`) in `eb sim` and Run; `quests.can-start` guards a job's offer; a guide step can open a space at an object. Each validates clean, plays to its golden runs and reaches every stage and its ending, and was made in the editor, walked to its ending by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and exported as one file ([specs/templates.md](docs/specs/templates.md#as-built-m69)). M6.8 (Barter and containers) is done: an actor carries a `purse`, kept by the economy kit once it changes (slice v2); a shop names its `actor`, who sells from their bag, buys into it with their purse and refuses what they can't pay for or carry (`cant-pay`, `no-room`), their bag and purse restocked every few days (W156 for one who never has anything to sell); any object's interact can hold items (`holds`, E089), opened in the inventory kit's container mode and taken from and put into a line at a time (slice v3), and a fallen body opens the same mode on its bag; the pack is format 16 once anything holds; the stock Container screen, and a merchant's purse on the Shop screen. The post played headless and by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and made a trading post with forms at 1280 and 390, is its gate ([specs/barter.md](docs/specs/barter.md)). M6.7 (Radiant aliases) is done: a space and any object carry tags, an object's kept through prefabs and overrides; a quest's slot of type `space` finds a space by its tags, and an `object` slot, or an actor slot with In or Tags, finds a thing in it, leaving out what was taken or has fallen; Nearest takes the fewest doors from the player's space (`world.doors`), a repeat tries its last run's pick last, and a space slot tries its next match when the slot In it can't be filled (E088, W127, E086's slots); the pack is format 15 once anything has a tag. The hollows played by keys and by touch, and tagged and its Supplies made with forms at 1280 and 390, is its gate ([specs/radiant.md](docs/specs/radiant.md)). M6.6 (Quest markers and maps) is done: an objective names its `target`, an actor or a space and an object in it, or has it read from the count that completes it (E086, W155); the quests kit tracks one quest, the last started unless the journal's Track picks another, kept by a save (slice v3); the walk's compass strip and a marker over each target on screen point at the tracked quest's open objectives, a target in another space by the door toward it on the fewest doors; a setting hides them; Bake map in the Scene panel draws a space from above as a player sees it, roofs left out indoors, kept beside the space with a hash of what it drew (E087, W154) and packed as format 14; and the Map screen pins the player, the targets and the doors on it. The glen followed by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and its map baked and a target set with forms at 1280 and 390, is its gate ([specs/markers.md](docs/specs/markers.md)). M6.5 (Animation) is done: every rigged model is on VRM's humanoid rig, a skinned model mapped by its joints' names and normalised once as it is imported, its rest a T-pose on the world's axes and the hips' drift taken out of its clips and kept as their pace (W152 for a skin off the rig); the starter character, generated in code and dedicated CC0, is the figure, the player and the stock animator's model, carried by walk.js; an `animator` record is a state machine in two layers, the whole body and an upper body over it, its transitions conditions over the stage's `anim.*` facts (E083 to E085, W153); the walk steps each body's animator by the motion's time within 60 m and lays its clips on three's mixer, a swing's states fit to the phases the fight times; the editor edits an animator as an outline with a preview on the starter character; the camp read frame by frame by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and an animator made in the ford with a foreign rig's walk at 1280 and 390, then seen on Ada as her talk opens, is its gate ([specs/animation.md](docs/specs/animation.md)). M6.4 (Combat) is done: the fighting kit, with project.json's `fighting` naming the player's actor and the combat record (E082, packs from format 13), makes an item's `weapon` group worn through the gear kit a melee or ranged weapon; the stage finds every hit on the walk's fixed steps, a swing's reach and arc or an arrow under gravity, each line a Rapier ray, and the sim takes `max(1, damage + the wielder's damage − the target's armour)` off health with no roll; the player aims softly toward the foe within 30° in front, by F, a click with the pointer locked, a pad's right trigger or a touch Attack button above Jump; a behaviour's `attack` step engages an NPC (the world module at version 5), at most two at once while the rest hold at 5 m, closing, leaning back to wind up and striking, archers shooting from where they stand; a fallen NPC lies as a body Interact searches, its drops rolled into its bag; a fallen player stops the game and the shell's Fallen screen loads the arrival checkpoint kept beside each slot; the editor has the Fighting kit's box, a Fighting form in the Project panel and Attack the player in the outline (W150, W151); the camp, three bandits and a hero, fought headless through the session and through Rapier and Recast, in the Play panel by keys and the mouse, by a pad and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and a bandit armed with forms alone, is its gate ([specs/combat.md](docs/specs/combat.md)). M6.3 (AI) is done: the game clock runs while a space is walked, at project.json's `clock.rate` (20x when left out, 0 stopping it; E080 when the calendar can't run it, W149 for one left out that can't), stopping under screens and the pause, with Wait still jumping it, packs carrying it from format 12; an actor's `behaviour` is a record, a tree of `first`, `in-order`, guards by condition, hours, days and cooldown, and the leaves travel, sleep, work, patrol, sandbox, follow, wait, face and effects, which the ai kit's module re-checks after every event and whose goals the crowd walks, the world module at version 4 adding `follow` and `stand`; NPCs see the player (a range, a cone, a ray through Rapier) and hear their running, landing and Interact, and an awareness from 0 to 100 climbs and decays through unaware, suspicious and alerted; the editor edits a behaviour as an outline of steps with a form each (E081, W148); the village, a smith's day and a guard's watch, played headless over a whole game day and in the Play panel by keys and by touch, and the watch made in the outline alone, is its gate ([specs/ai.md](docs/specs/ai.md)). M6.2 (Navigation) is done: Bake navigation in the Scene panel bakes a space's navmesh in a worker with the walk's own Recast and keeps it as `spaces/<id>.nav`, W147 once the solids it was baked from have changed and E078 for a file that isn't one; a `walk-to` effect gives a placed actor a goal, an object of its space (E079 when it can't walk there), the stage's Detour crowd walks it there round solids, NPCs and the player, and its arrival is `world/npc-arrived`, the world module's slice at version 3 keeping each goal and where each stood; nav.js loads beside walk.js only for a space with a navmesh, packs carry it from format 11, and a tap on the ground walks the player there; the square, a carter and twelve villagers walked headless through Recast and in the Play panel by keys and by touch, is its gate ([specs/navigation.md](docs/specs/navigation.md)). M6.1 (NPCs) is done: an actor placed in a space is an NPC, each in one place across the spaces (E077), drawn as its model or else as the engine's figure from a height and three colours, standing on a capsule; an actor with a talk is offered Talk by Interact, turns to face the player as it opens and back once the walk goes on, and the walk stands still under it; each actor carries a bag of its own (the inventory kit's slice at version 2, a version 1 save still loading) and with the factions kit belongs to a faction whose band `factions.band-of` reads; and the ford, a smith, a ferry-keeper, a warden drawn as the kit's pillar and a drover in the way, is played headless and walked by keys, a pad and touch at 360, 390 and sideways, while an NPC is made in the editor alone, seen as a figure and talked to ([specs/npcs.md](docs/specs/npcs.md)). M5.10 (Streaming and snapping) is done: the walk cuts one space into 64 m cells by its top-level objects' footprints, builds the 3×3 around the player whole (drawn 4 ms a frame, solid, its zones heard and its uses offered) and keeps it until it leaves the 5×5, and draws what is farther, to the tier's view distance (Low 250 m, Medium 400 m, High 600 m), as its models alone at their coarsest level, instanced, with fog closing there; in the Scene panel a moved box latches touching or flush onto another's within 12 px, the toolbar's Grid step takes the other axes, and Drop stands the chosen on what is under them; and the moor, a kilometre of terrain with about 1,350 starter kit pieces, is walked headless, by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, its rings built and taken down, the cairn's zone heard once built and the chest taken, while a hut is latched together from the starter kit to the millimetre by mouse and by touch ([specs/streaming.md](docs/specs/streaming.md)). M5.9 (Worldspaces) is done: a game moves its player between spaces with `enter-space`, an effect a zone, a use, a talk, an event or a quest stage can run, with a loading screen between; an `interact` component gives an object a prompt, used by E, the pad's X or a tap on the prompt beside Jump, sending `world/use`, which the sim checks and runs, a take kept; the world module's slice keeps the space the player is in, where they stood and which way they faced, told by the stage as the shell pauses, as the page hides and as they walk, and what was taken; a load puts the player back there, the taken gone; packs go to format 10 where a world needs it; a Space widget with no space of its own draws the player's; the kits' checks hear what zones and uses hold; and the glen, its gate, is walked by keys, a pad and touch at 360, 390 and sideways: the rope and the lamp taken, each door each way, the gate's zone setting the quest's stage, and a save in the bothy loaded where the player stood ([specs/worldspaces.md](docs/specs/worldspaces.md)). M5.8 (Movement) is done: a game's spaces walk in play. A pack of format 9 carries the spaces, their trigger zones resolved through prefabs and overrides, and the terrain and models they draw from; a screen's Space widget draws one with the game's HUD over it, in the Play panel, on the player page and in every export; Rapier on the page walks a capsule over the terrain and against colliders and models, pushing what `moves`, in third person with a camera pulled in short of walls or in first person; keys and mouse, a pad's two sticks, and by touch a floating stick, a look drag and a jump button; a zone entered sends `world/trigger`, which the sim checks and runs, so the game stays pure; `walk.js` (three, Rapier and the stage, 2.54 MB at gzip) loads the first time a space draws, the editor's Play panel walking with the same file an export ships; and the trail, its gate, is walked by machine by keys, mouse, pad and touch at 360, 390 and sideways and exported as one file and as a web build, each walked to the cairn offline. The trail's web build is at [`engine.rcjlabs.com/games/trail/`](https://engine.rcjlabs.com/games/trail/) for your phone ([specs/movement.md](docs/specs/movement.md)). M5.7 (Performance) is done: the Scene panel's view draws at Low, Medium or High, each setting the pixel ratio, antialiasing, the sun's shadows, a physical sky or its gradient, and how far and how thick foliage and weather are drawn, a lower tier drawing a subset of the same plants and drops; Quality picks Auto or a tier, kept per device, Auto starting from the device's hints (a software renderer Low, a phone Medium or Low by its GPU, a computer High with 8 cores) and stepping down when frames drawn back to back run long, never up; Stats shows the tier and why, the frame times, the draw calls and the triangles; `pnpm budgets` fails CI over a size budget in `budgets.json` (the editor's start, the 3D view's chunks, the precache and Space-Wrecked's export) and the gate over a count budget; and an e2e reads each tier back from three on the grove's glade, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, finds Auto stepping down on a computer it reads as fast, and finds the tier and Stats after a reload. The frame rate each tier must hold waits for your phone's reading (D69) ([specs/performance.md](docs/specs/performance.md)). M5.6 (Foliage) is done: a foliage type is a record of the engine's own `foliage` type, the model it scatters, plants per 100 m², sizes, turn, lean, the slopes and heights it grows on and how far it is drawn, with Start from and four presets (trees, bushes, grass and rocks); a terrain lists up to four, their density painted into a PNG beside the space, checked as E058, E067, E073, E074 and W144 by `eb validate` and the Problems panel; the terrain's row gains Plant and Clear, with the type they paint and Fill (everywhere, nowhere, or where a paint layer is); the inspector lists the terrain's foliage with Open and Remove, and adds a record or makes one from a preset; the view scatters every plant from the density by a fixed seed, so painting more adds plants and moves none, and draws each chunk's as instanced meshes at the model's level for its distance and not at all beyond the type's reach; the starter kit gains a tuft of grass; and an e2e makes trees and grass from the kit, plants, clears and fills them, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, reads the plants through the view's hook near and past the grass's 60 m, and finds it all after a reload ([specs/foliage.md](docs/specs/foliage.md)). M5.5 (Assets) is done: a model and a material are records of the engine's own `model` and `material` types, checked as E070 to E072 and W146 by `eb validate` and the Problems panel from the glTF's JSON alone; the Assets panel imports a GLB, or a glTF with its files, in Draco, Meshopt or with KTX2 textures, packs its geometry with Meshopt in a worker and makes its record with a fitted box; above a model's form, each of its materials picks a material record, made from one of eight presets, LOD levels are listed, and Fit collider fits a box, sphere, capsule or hull; a space places a model record, or any record whose type names a model, and draws it with its materials, its levels cut by meshoptimizer's simplifier as it is drawn and its collider as a wire when chosen; a starter kit of twenty pieces, made by a script here and dedicated by RCJLabs to CC0, comes in with one button; and an e2e imports a Draco, a Meshopt and a KTX2-textured model, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, makes a material, a LOD level and a collider, places them, reads back every triangle near and a tenth far, adds the kit and finds it all after a reload ([specs/models.md](docs/specs/models.md)). M5.4 (Environments) is done: an environment is a record of the engine's own `environment` type, its hour, sky, sunlight, sky light, fog and weather, that a space names, checked as E058, E068 and E069 by `eb validate` and the Problems panel; Records makes one, with Start from and seven presets (desert, coast, forest, snow, cave, sci-fi interior and town); a space's inspector picks one, makes one from a preset, and sets its hour, fog and weather; the view draws three's physical sky, a gradient or a colour, the sun's light and the sky's from the hour, fog, and rain or snow that fall every frame and stand still with reduced motion; and an e2e makes one from each preset, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, reads the view back through its hook and the canvas's pixels, moves the hour from noon to dawn, and finds it all after a reload ([specs/environments.md](docs/specs/environments.md)). M5.3 (Terrain) is done: a space can have one terrain, a square heightmap of 65 to 513 vertices a side, its heights a 16-bit greyscale PNG and its four paint layers an RGBA PNG beside the space; water is the engine's sixth component kind, on a plane; checked as E058, E067, W144 and W145 by `eb validate` and the Problems panel from the PNGs' headers alone; the Scene panel's Terrain row adds one and holds Raise, Lower, Smooth, Flatten and Paint with their layer, radius and strength, each stroke one step of undo and put back if a second finger lands; the inspector holds its form, a new low or high keeping each height in metres; the view draws it in chunks whose detail falls with distance, each edge laid on a coarser neighbour's so no seam cracks, and water see-through; and an e2e makes, sculpts, paints and waters one, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, sees finer chunks near than far with no gap at a seam, and finds it all after a reload ([specs/terrain.md](docs/specs/terrain.md)). Scattered foliage waits for M5.6, after Assets (D65). M5.2 (Scenes) is done: a space's objects carry components, the engine's light, collider, trigger zone, spawn point and sound, and a project's own kinds as type files in `components/`, made in the type designer; an object can place any record and change its fields where it stands; a prefab's placement can change any field of any object inside it, by id, prefabs inside prefabs included; each overridden field says so, with Revert; checked as E058 and E062 to E066, W142 and W143 by `eb validate` and the Problems panel; a placed record draws as a marker with its name, each component as a mark of its kind; a record's form lists where it stands; renames follow; and an e2e makes all of it with forms, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and finds it after a reload ([specs/components.md](docs/specs/components.md)). M5.1 (Viewport) is done: a project's 3D places are `spaces/<id>.json` and its reusable groups `prefabs/<id>.json`, placed by reference and checked as E058 to E061 by `eb validate` and the Problems panel; the Scene panel draws them with three.js's WebGPURenderer, on WebGL2 where there's no WebGPU, and only when something changes; a gizmo moves, turns and scales what is chosen, snapped, its handles 44 px to a finger, each drag one step of undo; the Outliner chooses and renames, docked above the Inspector on desktop and a tab of the sheet on a phone; Group into prefab makes a prefab of what is chosen; and an e2e builds a space with all of it, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and finds it all after a reload ([specs/viewport.md](docs/specs/viewport.md)). M4.12 (Proof #1: Space-Wrecked acts 1–2) is done by machine and deployed: the import writes all 455 records with no errors, the captain settles a world in all 500 golden seeds with every act 1–2 quest stage reached, every action on offer as it plays is a button its screens draw, and the web export is within its 540,000-byte budget. M4.11 (Templates) is done: New project starts from Empty or one of two original games, the salvage coast (an Expedition run: a harbour, a seeded coast of wrecks, storms and raiders, a debt and three endings) and the last ferry (a Story/choice: four passengers in ink scenes whose choices set flags, three endings), each bundled offline and taking the author's name; `eb new` writes the same files; a project's `guide.json` shows Make it yours steps in the Project panel, each opening the place it names, checked as E057 and W140; each template validates clean, matches 500 golden runs with every quest stage and ending reached, and is made, followed, played to an ending and exported as one file by machine on desktop and by touch on phones; and renaming a record now follows every reference the record index finds, in the kits' records, effects and keys, and the guide's steps. M4.10 (Export) is done: a project's `export.json` names what each target ships, set up and edited as a form in the Project panel; the hut exports to the web as a build that installs, plays offline after its first visit and offers its next build on the title, its service worker's caches named by its scope and build so games on one origin keep theirs; to one HTML file that plays from the device's files with its saves; to an itch.io zip; and to a repository, Publish's zip or `eb publish` with your own git, whose workflows deploy it to Pages and check the live build, and build the Android app around it on its own domain; an edition leaves out records, types, scenes and assets, E055 names a kept record that still names one, and a leak check reads every built file for what was left out, with the full build as its control; the same bytes from the editor and from `eb export`; and each target played by machine from the editor's own export, on desktop and by touch on phones. M4.9 (Play-in-Editor) is done: the Play panel's debug tools change a game only by its own effects, run through the sim as debug steps that a player's game refuses and a debugged slot can't save out; the run is recorded from its start, stepped back by playing it again and forward along it, kept as named states, and replayed on the project after an edit, stopping at the first step that plays out differently and naming where; time moves a day at a time by the calendar's own wait, so a skipped day fires what a played one does; quests say why they haven't moved on; and the hut is checked by machine on desktop and by touch on phones. M4.8 (Audio) is done: sounds are records of layered ZzFX voices, rendered by the engine's own port the same in every browser and in Node, made in Records with Play, presets, Vary and Paste ZzFX; ambience is synthesised from data, noise and drones looped without a seam with sounds now and then, and music is stems from audio files packed into the game file; each layer and stem plays while its condition holds, the topmost screen naming them; the feedback lines' sounds and buzzes come with each step, one buzz a step; the shell's settings gain Sound, Music, Ambience, Effects and Vibration, nothing is made before a press, and Sound at 0 makes nothing at all; and the hut, its audio made in the editor with forms, is heard by machine through the page's hook on desktop and by touch on phones. M4.7 (Encounters) is done: encounters are a kit, ticked in the Project panel; a fight is an effect, a choice of an event or a talk, fought by the roster front first or by actors it names, with a stance a choice of its own; worked out round by round, exactly, into a table out of 10,000 that is both what the choice's button shows and what its one roll reads, so the odds shown are the odds rolled by construction, which the bots check over 300 seeds of each fixture; above an encounter's form, its fight as the sim works it, its party picked; and the drift's hostile ships fought, made with forms and flown through by touch, beside a pit in Ludus's style where a gladiator from the roster fights. M4.6 (World map) is done: maps are a kit, ticked in the Project panel; layered maps made from a seed by rules written as data (counts, weights and layer windows), checked over 300 seeds with the first seed that breaks a rule a tap away, and maps drawn by hand with roads, each drive taking the quickest; travel an action whose cost, time and effects show on its button by the sim's own preview; a map widget that runs up a phone and across anything wider; the World panel, which makes both, a drawn map's places dragged; and a slice of Space-Wrecked's sector run made there and flown to its exit, by its bots and by touch through the map widget, beside a valley in Dirtbag's style. M4.5 (UI designer) is done: a screen is designed as it looks, as an outline of its widgets, a form for the one selected and a device frame at 360×740, 390×844, tablet or desktop, upright or turned, which draws the Play panel's moment from a game of the designer's own, samples filling what it lacks; widgets added, moved by buttons or a handle's drag, wrapped and deleted, each one step of undo, the JSON a second view of the same file; the theme designed by colour pickers with each pair's contrast shown as it is picked; and the hut's own screen and journal, set aside, made again from blank in the designer alone, as the fixture's JSON, on desktop and by touch on phones. M4.4 (Stock screens) is done: New screen offers templates written from the project, every number a live fact: a HUD over the actions on offer, a talk, an event, a shop, a hall, the inventory, the journal, a codex of records each shown once a condition the author writes holds, credits packed live from the assets' provenance log, and an ending whose buttons go back to the shell's title or New game; the hut, its own screens set aside, is made again from them in the editor and played to its end on them alone. M4.3 (Play shell) is done: a game is played inside a shell the engine draws in the game's theme, its words set in `ui/shell.json`: a title with Continue, New and a Daily that is the same game for everyone on the same day; the slot in play saved after every step, the next step waiting until the save lands; each slot saved out as a file or a code and brought back in, a damaged save falling back to its backup and set aside; pause, settings for text size and motion, a crash screen that gives the save as stored, and an update prompt on the title only; on the player page and in the editor's Play panel, each with saves of its own. M4.2 (Runtime UI II: input and feedback) is done: a game's feedback is data, `ui/feedback.json`, lines on the game's events worded in the worker after each step, shown as toasts, kept in a log widget the author places, and spoken once by a polite region; arrows and a gamepad move focus by Chooser's spatial rule; swipes turn tabs and close a screen a button opened, and on the player page the browser's back does too; the hut played to its end by keyboard alone, by a faked gamepad alone, and by touch on phones. M4.1 (Runtime UI I: screens) is done: a game's screens are data, `ui/<id>.json` widget trees and `ui/theme.json`, checked as the project compiles against the game's facts, actions and modes, each problem at its line; drawn by Preact from a JSON view the game's worker sends after each step, every button one the game offers; played in the editor's Play panel and, exported as a game file, at `/play/`, offline; the hut played to its end through its own screens, by touch at 360 and 390 px and in landscape. Phase 3 is done: its gate, the hut, an original adventure at an alpine hut, is built only in the editor by an e2e on every push, from an empty project to an export that `eb` finds clean and its bot plays to every quest done (D50). M3.11 (ink scenes) is done: scenes written in ink as `scenes/<id>.ink`, compiled into talks by our own parser of chooser's subset, in the editor and by `eb` alike, their conditions naming the game's facts through EXTERNALs and their effects written as `# fx:` tags; talks whose lines can pass and branch; the talk's checks over every scene at its lines, with a lint that walks every path; and in the editor, scenes written in CodeMirror with their problems at their lines and the talk's map beside. M3.10 (Aliases and timed jobs) is done: a quest's slots, filled by rule as it starts (a named record, or among those matching a condition the first, one at random, or the most or least by a fact) and named `@slot` in its conditions and effects and `{slot}` in its texts; a quest that fails, or goes to a slot's stage, when a slot's record is gone; quests that repeat, their runs counted; and timed jobs, rolled as their stage is reached and kept as a timer of the time module's, so nothing a player does after can roll them again; with the checks for slots that can't be (E044) or can never be filled (W127), forms, slots in the pickers, and jobs and gone lines on the quest map. M3.9 (Quests) is done: the quests kit (quests of stages, each with a log line, effects and objectives that check themselves, a condition or a count of events, an objective's Leads to before its stage's Then; failed by a stage, a condition or a deadline in days; started by an effect, with the game, or once a condition holds; a journal of each stage's line and the live objectives with their counts, read from the sim), with its record, forms and effects in the editor, a map of each quest above its form, and each quest's end state in the bots' reports. M3.8 (Dialogue trees) is done: the dialogue kit (speakers, and talks of lines and options, each option an event's choice that leads to another line, and its outcomes to lines of their own; talks opened while one is open wait their turn; the open talk and its line are saved), with its records, forms and effect in the editor, and above each talk a map of where its lines lead, whose boxes open their lines. ink scenes are M3.11 (D46, D47). M3.7 (Choice events) is done: the events kit (choice events whose choices cost, need or roll something, shown locked with what they lack and with their odds hidden unless an event shows them; decks that draw by weight, with rules on what can come; consequences that land days, choices or a number flag's rise later, checked again as they come due), with its records, forms and effects in the editor, and a list of effects as a kind of field in type files. M3.6 (Kits II: roster) is done: the roster kit (members who join and leave, kept as gone with why; recruits rolled from templates and offered by halls, worked out from the seed so only hires are saved; ranks on a stat, each giving a status; bonds between pairs, in bands), with its records, forms and effects in the editor, and records that modules make in play in the kernel. M3.5 (Kits II: factions and time) is done: the factions kit (standing with each faction in its own range and bands, which set a share of shops' prices, and which drifts back a point every few days) and the time kit (the calendar in `project.json`, edited in the Project panel, and rows that run as each day, week, month, season and year starts), with their records, forms and effects in the editor. M3.4 (Kits I: stats) is done: the stats kit (actors' stats worked out from a base, adds and percents and rounded once, damage kept apart, statuses that stack and run out over days or ticks) and the gear kit (slots, and items worn in them out of the party's bag), with their records, forms and effects in the editor. M3.3 (Kits I: economy) is done: kits switched on in `project.json`, the inventory kit (items, the party's bag, loot tables) and the economy kit (resources, and shops priced by one quote), with their records, forms and effects in the editor and fields a project adds to a kit's types. M3.2 (Scripts) is done: a project's game is TypeScript in its `scripts/` folder, checked against the purity rules, run by `eb` with no flags, and written, checked as you type and run in the editor, which asks once before running the scripts of a project from elsewhere. M3.1 (Trigger sheets) is done: events and listeners in the kernel, flags and trigger sheets as records, their checks and lint, and the Logic panel. Phase 2's milestones are done and its automatic gates pass. The editor is live over HTTPS at engine.rcjlabs.com, and the deploy check confirms it. Phase 2's own gate is yours to try on your phone: create a project with 20 records across 3 types, one of them designed there, reload it and export a zip. Phase 1 waits only on your phone: open [`engine.rcjlabs.com/spikes/3d/`](https://engine.rcjlabs.com/spikes/3d/), tap **Measure all tiers**, and send the reading. Phase 0 waits only on your phone check: install the editor from engine.rcjlabs.com and reopen it offline.
