    - Effects: start-quest, set-stage, fail-quest. Facts: state, stage, reached, objective, count, tally, each worded by the ids it names ("pay of debt is done"). Events: started, staged, objective-done, done, failed, refused.
  - **Checks:** E035 for a stage or an objective id twice or one a condition can't write, a Then or Leads to naming no stage, an objective with both or neither of Done when and Count, a failing stage with fields it never reads, and stages leading round with nothing to wait on; E022 and E023 for a count's event, as for a row's When; E043 for a condition or a set-stage naming a stage or an objective its quest lacks; W120 for days without a time module; W124 for a stage nothing brings the quest to; W125 for a quest nothing starts; W126 for a stage the quest can never end from.
  - **The bots:** `eb sim` and the editor's Run report each quest's state at the runs' end after the game's own metrics, so a quest no run finishes, or one left under way, shows without the game listing it.
  - **The editor:** Quests in the kit boxes; the quest in Records; its three effects with forms and in words on the cards ("move debt to its stage square"); a quest's change, or a change to a record naming one, checking them all again. Above each quest's form, its map: a row for each number of steps from the first stage, each stage a box saying where its objectives and Then lead and how it can end ("“Pay the miner” → square", "Then → the quest is done", "Fails after 5 days"), the stages the first doesn't reach in a last row, and a tap on a box opening its stage's Id.
  - **The gate:** in the editor, Quests ticked with Time; a flag; a quest made with forms: a first stage with an objective done by the flag, one counted that names neither Done when nor Count until it does (E035 at the objective), one reading the count, and a deadline; a failing stage nothing brings the quest to, shown at its id (W124) and in the map's last row, until an objective leads there; the map's rows read and a box tapped opening its stage; the warning that nothing starts it (W125) until it starts with the game; a row on a day's start setting the flag and the quest's stage, whose If names a stage the quest lacks (E043) until fixed; exported and checked clean by `eb validate`. Also the yard fixture, a slice of Space-Wrecked's first act (the miner's debt due in six days, paid, refused or let lapse; the drone bounty counted haul by haul; a name made in three steps; the chief's trust, which waits on the bounty and fails with the debt), run in the editor to the same lines `eb sim` prints, the quests' states among them, and a test over 30 seeds' runs with its own bot that every quest is done in some run, every one that can fail fails in some run, and every journal entry says what the state holds at every step.
  - [`docs/specs/quests.md`](docs/specs/quests.md) is the spec; the editor's part is in [`docs/specs/editor-kits.md`](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#the-quests-kit-m39); the survey behind it is [`docs/research/missions.md`](docs/research/missions.md).
  - **Found while building it:**
    - a stage's effect that refused refused the action that moved the quest: in the yard, a penalty of 20 credits on refusing the miner meant a party with less could not refuse him at all. The effects are one attempt now, as the events kit's consequences are;
    - a stage that fails, whose effects raise an event, was checked by the kit hearing that event before reaching it was done, and read as done: the yard's day lost to bruises finished the debt instead of failing it. A failing stage fails on any check now;
    - a tap that left a field lost its click on phones when the field showed a problem as it was left, pushing "Add to Objectives" down: M3.8's wait for the pointer to lift was too short, as on touch the focus moves after the finger lifts. The problem waits for the press's click now;
    - the quest's journal was first to list a stage reached twice twice; it lists each once, in the order last reached, so a looping quest's save can't grow.
  - **153 planted bugs**, at the unit and the UI level. The first run caught 128, one of them, the move limit taken out, by spinning its test's worker until it was stopped, as a CI job would time out. Twenty of the 25 survivors got a check, now seen failing:
    - in the kit: the objectives done kept out of order; one move a check, which the kit's own events still finish, but 64 events deep on a quest of 70 stages; no check as a quest starts or is set to a stage, for an active quest and a waiting one, which its own events also make, but only after the effects that follow it; a start that is unknown taken as true; a stage with no log journaled by its id; `reached` true only at the current stage; the start's look at waiting quests in a game whose start raises no event; an objective's facts reading the first stage alone; quest metrics with the kit off, which would ask a project's own quest type for a fact it has no module for;
    - in the checks and the graph: days counted as a way to end on a stage done at once; a Then from a stage that waits taken as a loop with nothing to wait on; the last stage of an id read, where the kit reads the first; W120 with no game's events given, when a game's own time module may be there; an unreached stage that can't end called W126 rather than W124; no stages, no days and a count to none let through;
    - in the editor: the map's rows in the order the stages are written; a deadline shown on a stage done at once.
    - Three were code that could never matter: the stage that fails failing the quest in `enter`, twice over, and `finish` checking for a quest the check had just found active. Every check fails a quest at a stage that fails, and a check follows every stage reached, so the code is gone.
    - Two change nothing a player or an author can see: `startWaiting` skipping a quest already started, which only saves reading its start, as `startQuest` refuses it anyway; and a box with no padding or floor, which its id and its exits still hold to 44 px, as in M3.8.
- **M3.10 Aliases and timed jobs** (D47, D48, your calls). Done:
  - **Slots** (Bethesda's aliases): records a quest picks as it starts, a named record or one among those of a type that match its Where, made ones such as the roster's recruits among them: the first by id, one at random from the kit's own stream, or the most or least by a fact (your call: by rule). In the quest's fields `@man` stands for the slot's record wherever a record's id goes, in conditions, effects and counts; `{man}` in its texts shows the record's name, read as the journal is. A quest whose slots can't be filled doesn't start: `start-quest` refuses, so a choice that would start it says why.
  - **Gone** (your call): while a quest runs, a slot keeps its record only while its Keeps while holds; when it stops, the quest goes to the slot's stage or fails, saying who is gone, after success and before the stage's own failing. A slot that goes holds a record away, from every other such slot, until its quest ends (`quests.away`).
  - **Repeats** (your call): a quest that repeats runs again by `start-quest`, `set-stage`, or a Starts when that has stopped holding and holds again, its slots filled afresh, its entry made anew and its past runs counted (`quests.times`), so the save holds one run.
  - **Jobs** (your call): a stage with a length in the calendar's units and outcomes with chances, rolled with `roll`'s table as it is reached and kept as the time module's timer `quests-<quest>`, so another draw or another order of actions can't roll it again, and time passed while a game is closed is the time module's. The journal shows its odds and the time left, never its outcome. Leaving its stage calls it off.
  - **The kit's slice is version 2,** with a migration, and a save of the dock made by version 1 that loads with its quests as they were and plays on.
  - **Checks:** E044, new, for `@x` naming no slot of its quest, in Starts when, anywhere outside a quest, where its slot's type isn't wanted, or in a Where or By naming a slot after its own, and for a named record that isn't there; E035 for each slot and job rule; E045, new, for a job without a time module; W127, new, for a slot no record can ever fill; W128, new, for `{x}` naming no slot.
  - **The editor:** a quest's Repeats, Slots and a stage's Job in its forms; inside a quest, a record field's picker offers first the slots holding a record of its type; the quest map leads a job by its outcomes' chances, puts a gone stage a step from the first, and says above its rows where the quest goes when each slot's record is gone. Run adds a repeating quest's runs done and failed.
  - **The gate:** in the editor, Stats, Time, Roster and Quests ticked; a quest made with forms, its slot picked at random among the roster, sent, kept while on it and gone to a stage; an objective naming a slot the quest lacks (E044) until fixed; a job whose chances add up to 90 (E035) until they add up to 100; the slot picked for an effect's actor; a second slot of a type nothing has (W127) until taken out; the map's gone line and the job's outcomes read; exported and checked clean by `eb validate`. Also the school fixture, a slice of Ludus (a booking at the games that repeats all season for a gladiator picked at random from the roster, bought ones among them, sent and lost if he dies in drill, his bout a job; and the rival house's challenge to the school's most famous man), run in the editor to the same lines `eb sim` prints, and a test over 30 seeds that each quest is done, failed and lost to a gone record in some run, that made gladiators fill slots, that the bouts end within three standard deviations of their odds, and that at every step the journal agrees with the state, the booked man is away and a job's timer is pending only at its stage.
  - [`docs/specs/quests.md`](docs/specs/quests.md#slots) has slots, repeats and jobs; the editor's part is in [`docs/specs/editor-kits.md`](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#the-quests-kit-m39); the survey behind it is [`docs/research/aliases.md`](docs/research/aliases.md).
  - **126 planted bugs**, at the unit and the UI level. The first run caught 114. Eleven of the 12 survivors got a check, now seen failing: a field of a project's own taking two types given a slot of neither; W128 in an objective's text; a job leading back to its own stage, which waits on time and isn't a loop; a slot that can be lost as a way to end (W126); `@slot` as a map's key, as Pay a cost's resources are, and `@constructor` naming no slot; a named record not there; a Keeps while that can't be answered; gone before the stage's own failing; a job stage whose effects move the quest on; the picker's slots by type. One was code that could never matter, canEnd's test for a job, which canBeDone already makes, and it is gone.
- **M3.11 ink scenes** (D46, D49, your calls). Done:
  - **ink compiled into talks** (your calls: our own parser of chooser's subset, `scenes/<id>.ink` files, facts named through EXTERNALs). `@eb/ink`, a small pure package, reads a scene in the browser and in `eb` alike: knots, once-only choices, gathers and their labels, one-line and block conditionals, `{condition: this|that}` inside a line (three at most), diverts to knots, labels and `END`, and the speaker, `fx`, `cost`, `needs`, `beat`, `name` and `show-odds` tags. A condition names a fact through its EXTERNAL, `flag_on` for `flag.on`; an effect is a tag, `# fx: set-flag(flag: asked, to: true)`, checked by its schema. Anything outside the subset is E046 at its line, saying what to write instead; an EXTERNAL naming no fact is E012, its arguments wrong E013.
  - **A line of the talk is one speaker's run of lines** (the session's call), so a talk taps on between speakers; a choice point that shows no choice ends the talk, as ink's story ends there; a choice is once-only on a line its talk can come back to, so a scene opened again offers its choices again.
  - **Talks gain lines that pass and branch:** a line may leave out its text, and goes on by its first branch whose If holds, before its Then; ink's blocks compile into them, and a talk's form can write them too.
  - **Checks:** the talk's checks over every scene, each at the scene's line; E035 for lines that pass to each other in a loop; W129, new, the lint that walks every path from a start with the conditions that held on it, for a branch, an option or a line the ways to it rule out; W130, new, for a line the talk can never end from; W131, new, for a file under `scenes/` that is no scene.
  - **The editor:** Scenes in the Logic panel; a scene edited in the inspector with CodeMirror and ink's colours, its problems in the gutter and listed, each a way to its line, and beside them the map of the talk it makes, a box opening the scene at its line; the scene's talk among the talks in Records, opened by its row, its picker, Used by and the Problems panel, all opening the scene.
  - **The gate:** in the editor, Dialogue ticked; a flag; a scene made in the Logic panel, compiling as it stands, its map drawn; ink the engine doesn't read (`VAR`) at its line, with no map, and the Problems panel opening the scene there; the scene written with a speaker that isn't there (E005) at the scene's line until the speaker is made; the map's branch and choices read, and a box opening its line; the scene among the talks in Records, its row opening it; nothing opening it (W123) until a sheet's row does; exported and checked clean by `eb validate`, its talk's lines those `eb build` makes. On phones at 360, 390 and 740 px: a scene made, written and gone to by touch, a problem's Line button and a map's box opening their lines. Also the gatehouse fixture, a slice of Chooser of the Slain written fresh (three dusk scenes in ink and a game written as scripts), played by `eb sim` to its golden runs and over 30 seeds with no offer refused; and a test that plays every way through each of its scenes by inkjs, a dev dependency, and by the talk, holding them to the same lines, speakers, effects and choices.
  - [`docs/specs/ink.md`](docs/specs/ink.md) is the subset and its compile; the kit's lines that pass and branch, and the new checks, are in [`docs/specs/dialogue.md`](docs/specs/dialogue.md); the editor's part is in [`docs/specs/editor-kits.md`](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#ink-scenes-m311); the survey behind it, with the reading over chooser's 61 scenes, is [`docs/research/ink.md`](docs/research/ink.md).
  - **Found on the way:**
    - the comparison with inkjs found that a choice point showing no choice ends ink's story, where the first lowering went on to the gather after it;
    - every ink line with a cost was W122, its options all once-only: an option is once now only where its line can come back to itself;
    - the walk of every path, on a loop that passed the same branch again, knew one more condition each time round and ran to its limit; it keeps each condition once now;
    - a scene with the dialogue kit off made a record of no type, and with it a second problem; an EXTERNAL that named no fact was reported again at each use, as not declared; a condition with an `or` was bracketed even alone, and one starting with a bracket wasn't bracketed under a `not`, which then read wrongly; the fourth `{condition: this|that}` was reported at its paragraph's first line.
  - **122 planted bugs**, at the unit and the UI level: 90 first, 23 in the editor, and 9 with the fixes above. Of the 110 first ones that applied, 86 were caught; three more named text the formatter had since moved, and once moved, all three were caught. 22 of the 24 survivors got a check, now seen failing:
    - in the ink compiler: `or` as a word; numbers with a point; a comparison turned round, `<` and `>`; values given to arguments in the EXTERNAL's order; braces in a tag; `\` before a sign; one `|` at most; runs of white space made one; `- else:` last; a choice deeper than those above it; `DONE` as `END`; three `{condition: this|that}` at most; a knot's own label before a knot of that name;
    - in the kit: a branch taken on an If that is neither true nor false; whether going on ends the talk, read from its branches;
    - in the checks: a branch naming no line as a way to end; the dialogue kit's facts forgotten as a line shows; the starts before a start known not to hold; a condition that can never hold left to W102; a branch with no line allowed by the talk's type;
    - in the editor: a scene that isn't UTF-8; the scene editor showing the catalog's problems, seen now with the kit turned off.
    - Two were code rewritten: `topColon`'s count of brackets, which no valid condition needs, is gone; and brackets round an `or`, put on in two places, are put on once now where conditions join, a rewrite that found the `not` bug above. Of the 9 planted with the fixes, 8 were caught at once; the ninth, a lone `or` bracketed in a line, got a check.
- **Phase 3's gate: the hut** (D50, your calls). Done:
  - **An original adventure at an alpine hut** (your calls): a season of ten days keeping a hut above a valley village, made only in the editor. Its game is a short script: carry a load up, rest, open the store, visit someone, and close at the season's end. The rest is records and a sheet:
    - the guides' guild and the village, as factions;
    - the store, priced by the village's standing;
    - Greta's and Anselm's talks, made with forms;
    - an evening at the hut, written in ink;
    - a storm, as a choice event whose search is rolled;
    - three quests: stocking the hut in steps, the ridge as a timed job that Anselm's talk starts, and loads carried up, counted.
  - **One e2e builds it all** (your call), on every push (`e2e/hut.spec.ts`):
    - it starts from an empty project and works through Records' forms, the Logic panel, the script editor and the scene editor, comparing each file with the hut fixture as it lands;
    - exported, the zip is the fixture file for file;
    - `eb validate` finds nothing, `eb sim` plays it to the fixture's golden runs, and its bot finishes every quest in some of 100 runs.
  - **The fixture** (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/hut`) is what the e2e builds. A test plays it at random over 60 seeds and reaches every talk line, option, storm choice and quest ending.
  - **Its checks were seen failing:**
    - a fixture record that differs from what the editor writes fails the e2e at that record;
    - a ridge that can never top out fails the check that every quest is done in some run.
- **Ideas, not yet in a milestone:**
  - From M3.1 ([triggers.md](docs/specs/triggers.md#open-questions), [editor-logic.md](docs/specs/editor-logic.md#open-questions)): a "game starts" event; previews that hear events; a warning when a once row's id changes; flags a module says its code reads; long sheets whose rows fold; rows moved by dragging. (A game's events and effects in the pickers came with M3.2's game.ts.)
  - From M3.3 ([kits.md](docs/specs/kits.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): rules on what a shop pays, not only on what it charges; what the party sells put on the shelf to buy back; bags of other actors, and containers (Phase 6); `eb` exporting the kits, so a game.ts can place a kit's module among its own; an example project per kit; relabelling a kit's field. And, from the readings: a turn in a shop quotes every shelf item and every item carried, about 76 µs a turn for the hangar against 42 µs for salvage run.
  - From M3.4 ([stats.md](docs/specs/stats.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): effects over time on every actor (venom, regeneration), which a row naming one actor can't do; who may wear what ("strength 6"), which conditions can't say of the actor wearing it; two-handed items; turns as a duration, with Phase 6's combat; the designer naming the gear kit for its fields on items. And, from the readings: each value read works its parts out afresh, 193 µs a turn for the arena against 69 µs for the hangar; values kept for a state would be worked out once. A golden file's content hash counts a script's spaces, so formatting a game's scripts means taking its goldens again.
  - From M3.5 ([factions.md](docs/specs/factions.md#open-questions), [time.md](docs/specs/time.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): relations, allies and rivals a change spreads to; drift below a point a day; setting a standing rather than changing it; prices by band when selling, and a job's pay by band (with M3.9's missions); schedule's form; a season that starts on a month in the calendar's form; the calendar's form with Time off. And, from the readings: an edit to a faction checks every record naming it, 40 ms for 53 of 2,179 records; an edit to the calendar checks every record, 76 ms.
  - From M3.6 ([roster.md](docs/specs/roster.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): rows naming the member an event is about (aliases, with M3.9's missions); a fee set by a hall's faction; a hall's offers shown on screen and in the editor; kin made together, and positions in a line; bonds that decay; a template's names typed in bulk. `constructor` is a valid record id, and the kits before the roster read their slices' maps as a plain object does: a faction named constructor reads Object.prototype's, and its standing throws as outside its domain. Reading by own keys, as the roster does, or refusing the id, would end it. And, from the readings: a handler's context, its mode control among it, is made afresh for every effect, and every beat and slice is checked as JSON; together over a third of the crew's turn.
  - From M3.7 ([events.md](docs/specs/events.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): odds moved by stats; a timed choice; a delay drawn from a range; a lint for a consequence's id that nothing books; an event played in the editor, its choices locked, costed and with their odds as the preview gives them, and a deck's chances; an event's text filled in from state. A record renamed in a kit's fields, a sheet's rows and effects too, by the paths the index's used-by already holds for every reference. And, from the readings: an event's edit checks every record naming any event, 99 ms for 210 of 2,219 records. The CLI's tests that play a fixture's golden runs through `eb sim` take most of vitest's 5 s here: the steading's file took 4.8 to 5.3 s on its own, the same on main (3 runs each, 2026-10-19), and its test timed out once in a full run; a timeout of their own would end that.
  - From M3.8 ([dialogue.md](docs/specs/dialogue.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): barks, single lines drawn from a pool without coming again soon; a picker of a talk's own lines for a Then or a Leads to, in place of a typed id; the map's arrows drawn on a wide screen; a talk played in the editor, as an event would be. And, from the readings: a talk's edit checks every record naming any talk, 101 ms for 200 of 2,212 records, as an event's does.
  - From M3.9 ([quests.md](docs/specs/quests.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): have and need for conditions, "30 of 50" read from the fact a Done when compares; optional objectives; a warning for fields a quest never reads (a Then from a stage whose objectives all lead elsewhere, Fails when or Days on a stage with no objectives, which is done at once); a picker of a quest's own stages for a Then, a Leads to or a `set-stage`, in place of a typed id; a quest played in the editor, its journal as the preview gives it. And, from the readings: the kit hears every event and reads every waiting quest's start and every active quest's objectives on each, 11% of the yard's sweep; a quest's edit checks every record naming any quest, 117 ms for 200 of 2,210 records, as a talk's does.
  - From M3.10 ([quests.md](docs/specs/quests.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions), [aliases.md](docs/research/aliases.md#open-questions)): the player picking who fills a slot, with odds moved by stats (D48); a cooldown between a repeat's runs; other records naming a quest's slot, a talk about the booked man; a slot's type picked, and its slots offered as a condition is typed; a roll among a job outcome's stage's effects sealed with the job. And, from the readings: the bench's quests have no slots, so what slots cost a quest's edit is not measured yet.
  - From M3.11 ([ink.md](docs/specs/ink.md#open-questions), [research/ink.md](docs/research/ink.md#open-questions), [editor-kits.md](docs/specs/editor-kits.md#open-questions)): conditions comparing two facts, which one of chooser's 61 scenes needs; sticky choices and stitches; a warning for a line whose numbered id an edit would move, a saved game or a condition naming it; a run of lines that only go on shown as one passage, the stage's (Phase 4); `# draft` as a talk's field; a read-only form of a scene's talk. And, from the readings: a scene's edit checks every record naming any talk, as a talk's does, 262 ms for 200 of 2,212 records whose talks run to 32 lines, against 101 ms for talks of 8; most of it the checks every record gets, and the ink compile 4%.
  - From M3.2 ([scripts.md](docs/specs/scripts.md#open-questions), [editor-scripts.md](docs/specs/editor-scripts.md#open-questions)): scripts in a sandbox of their own origin; renaming a script with its imports; type errors as you type, once TypeScript 7.1 has a JavaScript API; a precache that leaves CodeMirror and the worker to first use; a throw's line seen right in Firefox and Safari; CodeMirror with a real phone's keyboard. And, from the index's reading: an edit to a record naming a flag checks every flag-related record again, 60 ms for 300 in Node; a lint indexed by flag would check only the flags the edit names.

**Done when:** a text/menu adventure built only in the editor passes bot reachability headless in CI, with every quest completable. It has one quest from each mission family, 2 dialogue trees, 1 ink scene, a shop and factions. [auto]

**Done** (2026-10-19): the hut passes. The storm is the choice event, the store is the shop, and the guild and the village are the factions. Stocking the hut is the quest of steps, the ridge the timed job, and the loads the counted objective; Greta and Anselm are the two talks, and the evening is the ink scene. See Phase 3's gate above.

## Phase 4: UI, menus, play, export → Proof #1

**Goal:** a whole game from the editor to a phone, with title screen, menus, HUD and saves.

- **M4.1 Runtime UI I: screens** (D51, your calls). Done:
  - **Screens are data** (your call: JSON widget trees). `ui/<id>.json` is a screen: its title, the modes it shows, whether it is modal, and its top, main and bottom, each a widget. `ui/theme.json` is six colours and the type. Nine widgets: stack, panel, grid, text, image, button, progress, list and tabs, each with `shows-when` and layout by breakpoint. Templates show facts as conditions write them, `{time.day}`; a view's own fields, `{talk.text}`; and in a list, an entry's, `{.price}`.
  - **Checked as the project compiles,** against the game once its scripts have run:
    - E047 for a screen's or the theme's shape;
    - E048 for a fact or a field it can't read;
    - E049 for an action, a screen, a mode or a kit the game lacks;
    - E050 for a theme short of AA, 4.5:1;
    - W132 for a file under `ui/` that is no screen, and W133 for a mode no screen shows; an image of a file `assets/` lacks is E018.

    Each problem is at its line in the file, in the editor and from `eb validate` alike. Pack format 4 carries the screens and the theme; a project without screens packs as before.
  - **Drawn by Preact with signals** (your call), in `@eb/ui`, from a view the game's worker sends after each step: the game's modes, the actions on offer, the values of the facts and conditions the screens name, and the talk, event, shop, bag and journal as the kits' own previews and facts give them. The stack follows the modes, a modal screen over the one below; a mode no screen shows plays as a list of what is on offer. A button is enabled only while its action is on offer, and the session refuses anything else before the sim sees it. Layout follows the game's own box, the safe area kept clear, and the theme's colours are CSS variables.
  - **Played** in the editor's Play panel, in a copy of the script worker of its own, with the screens listed beside it, New screen and New theme, each opening in the inspector as JSON with its problems at their lines. **Export game** in the Project panel writes the pack as one `.game.json` file, which the player page at `/play/` opens and plays, with nothing sent anywhere. It is a page of the editor's own build, so it plays offline once opened.
  - **The gate** (your call): the hut gained a theme and six screens (the hut, a talk, the storm, the store, the journal, the closed hut), which the Phase 3 spec now builds in the editor too, its export still the fixture file for file. The hut is played to its end through its screens on desktop, and by touch at 360 and 390 px and in landscape, every screen with nothing past the edges, no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds; exported as a game file and played at `/play/`, online and offline; and in Node over six seeds, pressing only what the screens draw, every mode on a screen of its own. Play waits while the project has errors, and a screen's problem opens it at its line.
  - [`docs/specs/screens.md`](docs/specs/screens.md) is the format, the checks, the session and where it plays; the survey is [`docs/research/runtime-ui.md`](docs/research/runtime-ui.md).
  - **Found on the way:**
    - the bag's list belongs to the inventory kit, not the economy kit, as first written;
    - a fact the game lacks would have thrown in the kernel at every step: the session reads it as no value, the compiler having said why;
    - the Play worker first bundled the compiler a second time, 577 kB; Play runs in a copy of the script worker now;
    - the player's file input counted as a 1×1 target: it is made only when its button is pressed;
    - the hut's store buys nothing back, which the store screen shows as a sale with no price that can't be pressed. That is the hut's content, yours to change.
  - **76 planted bugs:** 70 first, at the unit level and through the built page, of which 62 were caught. Of the 8 survivors, two were code that did nothing, and went: a sort `readUi` had already done, and a test for errors `compileProject` already makes. Six got checks, and each, planted again, was caught: a three-digit colour; the dark end of the contrast formula; a breakpoint's layout over the widget's own; buttons held while a step is on its way, and screens a button opened closed when the modes change, both moved into plain functions; and Play waiting on errors.
- **M4.2 Runtime UI II: input and feedback** (D51, D52, your calls). Done:
  - **Feedback is data** (your call): `ui/feedback.json` words the beats a game wants said, each line on an event the game declares, matching some of its arguments, with holes for them (`{.item}`, a record's id shown as its name), to a toast, the log or both. The worker fills the words in after each step, so the page still never reads the sim's state. Checked as the project compiles: E051 for an event the game doesn't declare, E052 for an argument it lacks or a value it can't take, E048 for a fact in the words. Pack format 5 carries it; a pack without it keeps its format. The editor's New feedback starts the file with lines for the kits that are on.
  - **Toasts, a log and an announcer:**
    - one toast at a time, timed by its length as Dirtbag's are, which taps go through and screen readers don't hear;
    - a `log` widget the author places (your call), its lines kept for the play and not saved, newest first, read back but not announced;
    - one polite region that speaks each step's lines once, emptied then filled as Spliceworld's is, so the same words twice are still read.
  - **Keys and a gamepad:**
    - arrows and the pad move focus by Chooser's spatial rule on the top screen;
    - A presses, and Escape and B are Back;
    - LB and RB turn tabs;
    - each screen gives focus back to the control it last had when it returns to the top;
    - the pad draws focus rings until a key or a pointer is used.
  - **Gestures** (your call): Space-Wrecked's recognizer, with a swipe. A swipe on tabs turns them, a swipe right closes a screen a button opened, and on the player page the browser's back (Android's back gesture) does too, without leaving the game. W134 warns of a screen only a button opens with nothing on it that closes it.
  - **The gate** (your call): the hut, with its own feedback, played to its end at `/play/` by keyboard alone and by a faked gamepad alone, and on phones at 360 and 390 px and in landscape by touch with its swipes and the browser's back; each step's words checked as toasts, in its logs and in the announcer, the same words twice still spoken; axe and the 44 px checks on each screen. The Phase 3 spec builds the feedback in the editor too, and the hut's export is still the fixture file for file.
  - [`docs/specs/input.md`](docs/specs/input.md) is the design; the survey's second look is in [`docs/research/runtime-ui.md`](docs/research/runtime-ui.md).
  - **Found on the way:**
    - `touch-action` counts only up to the nearest element that scrolls, so the screens' `pan-y` didn't reach inside their main region, and the browser cancelled every swipe there as a pan: the main region says `pan-y` too;
    - a swallow for the click a swipe would make outlived a swipe that made none and ate the next quick tap. Planted out, nothing failed: a touch that moves past the browser's tap slop makes no click. It went;
    - the browser's back sometimes left the game, about one run in ten: Back's history read its depth from `history.state`, which `history.go` changes only later, and added its entries after the page painted, so a back pressed as a screen showed found none. It counts its own entries now, before the paint, and a spec slows `history.go` to show the first;
    - a tap straight after the specs' fast swipes was sometimes lost, which looks like Chromium's handling of a fling; the specs' swipe holds still for 150 ms before it lifts, as a finger does. Whether a real phone loses a tap after a quick swipe isn't known;
    - `GameScreens` grew past 160 lines, so the screen view moved to `Screen.tsx` and input to `input/`.
  - **Untested for feel:** the toasts' times, the swipe's 48 px and 800 ms, and the stick's dead zones are the numbers of the games they came from, not tried on a phone or with a pad. No real screen reader has heard the announcer: the specs check the region's text, not speech.
  - **77 planted bugs:** 47 at the unit level, of which 44 were caught. The three survivors got checks and, planted again, were caught: the editor's feedback when it is the only file in `ui/`, the stick's hysteresis, and the furthest stick of every pad. 30 through the built page, of which 24 were caught. Of the six survivors, three were code that did nothing, and went: the swallow above, a key that remade the game between games (both pages unmount it), and a memory of where focus woke, which the screen already gives back as it returns to the top. The other three, planted again, were caught: the Play panel's steps saying nothing and the scroll at a screen's edge, which got checks, and New feedback renamed, first planted as "New feedbacks", which the spec's match still found. Back's history, rewritten after the race above, was planted again four ways, each caught: no entries added, entries kept for screens that are gone, entries added after paint (by five runs of the phone spec), and the count read from `history.state`, which the slow-history spec passed at first, as it didn't wait for the late step back to land; now it waits, and checks the race happened.
- **M4.3 Play shell** (D53, your calls). Done:
  - **Engine-drawn in the game's theme** (your call), on the player page and in the editor's Play panel. A game sets its title, tagline, slots (1 to 5, 3 by default), Daily and a slot's line in `ui/shell.json`, checked as the project compiles: E047 for its shape and braces, E048 for a fact the slot's line can't read. Pack format 6 carries it; a pack without one keeps its format. The editor's New shell writes one, which opens in the inspector with its problems at their lines.
  - **The title:** Continue loads the numbered slot played last; New starts in a slot, asking again over a full one and naming what it holds; Daily, when the game turns it on (your call), is the same game for everyone on the same local day (seed `daily:<n>`, `n` the days from 2026-01-01), in a slot of its own that it goes on from that day.
  - **Saves every step** (your call): the worker makes the save after each step, as the game's view comes back, and the page writes it through `@eb/platform`; the next step waits until it lands. A write that fails says "Not saved" in the bar, and play goes on. The worker never touches storage: loading sends the slot's text to the worker, so backups and quarantine work as M1.8 built them. A save that won't load falls back to its backup and says so.
  - **Saves screen:** each slot loaded, saved out as its file, copied as an `EB1` code (shown to copy by hand where the clipboard is refused), or deleted on a second press, never the slot in play; a save brought in by code or file, asking again over a full slot, another game's refused by name; and the saves set aside, each saved out and deleted on a second press.
  - **Pause** by Escape or B with nothing open, a pad's Start or the bar's button, the game inert under it; **Settings** for text size and motion, kept per game and edition, and the controls listed; a **crash screen** in plain DOM on the player page that covers it and offers the save exactly as stored; and the **update prompt** on the title only.
  - The Play panel keeps its saves under edition `playtest`, apart from the player page's `play`.
  - **The gate** (your call): the hut, with its own `shell.json`, at `/play/`: started in a slot, played and reloaded, and continued to the same state; its code copied and brought into a fresh browser to the same state; a damaged save falling back to its backup and set aside; a crash giving its save as stored; its Daily the same save, byte for byte, in two browsers on the same day; pause by Escape, settings kept across a reload; a held write holding the step, and a failed one saying so. On phones at 360 and 390 px and in landscape, by touch, every shell screen fits with no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds, and a game paused, reloaded and continued comes back the same. In Node, the hut's saves taken at each step load back to the same views. The Play panel's spec plays the hut to its end and finds its playtest slot alone.
  - [`docs/specs/shell.md`](docs/specs/shell.md) is the design; the survey's third look is in [`docs/research/runtime-ui.md`](docs/research/runtime-ui.md).
  - **Found on the way:**
    - after a step, focus came back to a button still disabled: the view landed while the shell was busy. Busy now ends in the same render as the view;
    - the shell's box first carried the game's class, `.eb-game`, so the page had two games; it has its own class;
    - pause first covered the game with an opaque box; the game shows under it, inert;
    - the Saves screen listed nothing in play: slots were read once, at the title. They are read again whenever a screen that lists them opens;
    - the update prompt has no spec: Chromium fetches a service worker's script outside Playwright's routing, so a spec can't make a new version arrive. The editor's own update toast is in the same place;
    - a save costs more than the step it follows: see Readings.
  - **Untested for feel:** the second press's 4 seconds, the text sizes 1.2 and 1.4, and the Daily's number on the title. No real phone has run the shell.
  - **48 planted bugs:** 24 at the unit level, of which 21 were caught. The three survivors got checks and, planted again, were caught: the Daily's day counted in UTC (the container runs in UTC; a test now sets the time zone), a slot line's facts not read (the hut's screens already named them; a test now uses one they don't), and the pad's Start not mapped. 24 through the built page, of which 23 were caught. The survivor, a step going on before its save lands, passed because a write's data is in the store before it completes; the spec now holds the completion and checks the view waits, and, planted again, it was caught.
- **M4.4 Stock screens** (D54, your calls). Done:
  - **Templates New screen offers** ([specs/stock-screens.md](docs/specs/stock-screens.md)), each written into `ui/` as JSON the author owns, from the project as it stands, every number a live fact: HUD, Dialogue, Event, Shop, Hall, Inventory, Journal, Codex, Credits and Ending, each offered while the kits it reads are on. Event and Hall weren't on the list: with them every kit's mode has a stock screen, and the gate's hut has a storm. A template's `shows` is the mode it is for, unless a screen already shows it; the ending's, the modes that allow no action, which the game's catalog now lists.
  - **A codex of records shown once known** (your call): a `records` list gives every record of a type, its item naming the entry as `@entry` in facts the worker reads for each record, and `known` holds an entry back until it holds, drawn by the list's `unknown`, which shows nothing of it. Checked: a codex names a type the project has (E049), its known and item read the type's facts and fields (E048), and `@entry` is named nowhere else (E048).
  - **Credits packed live from the provenance log** (your call): pack format 7 carries each asset's licence, author, source and tool, only with screens to show them, so a project without screens keeps its pack and hash. A `credits` list shows them.
  - **A button back to the shell** (your call): `{"shell": "title"}` and `{"shell": "new"}`, disabled where no shell plays the game. The ending template places both.
  - **The gate** (your call): in the editor, the hut, its own screens set aside, is given every stock screen its kits allow by New screen, and a CC-BY image by the Assets panel. Its export's screens and credits are those the templates write in Node; played at `/play/` to its end through them alone, the codex holds rope back until it is bought, the credits name the image's author and licence, and Back to title reaches the shell's title. On phones at 360 and 390 px and in landscape, each stock screen fits by touch, no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds, and the season is played to its ending. In unit tests, every template written into the hut, the crew (roster, stats) and the arena (stats, gear) compiles clean.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - packing credits for every project with assets changed the ludus fixture's pack and its golden hash, though it has no screens to show them: credits travel only with `ui/` now;
    - a codex with no type, or one the project lacks, reported its item's `@entry` a second time: an untyped codex leaves `@entry` to the list's own E049;
    - a guard in the templates for a kit's mode the game lacked could never hold, since a kit's modes are the game's while it is on: it went;
    - the HUD's buttons use the engine's words for each action ("Carry", "Visit greta"), where the hut's own screen says "Carry a load up": a game that wants its own words puts its own buttons there;
    - an option's reason, cost and odds read as a dash where there are none, so the templates leave them for the author to place.
  - **Untested for feel:** the stock screens' words and layout, which no player has seen; the HUD's first three stats, which is a guess at what a HUD wants.
  - **36 planted bugs:** 28 at the unit level, of which 26 were caught. The two survivors got checks and, planted again, were caught: `@entry` left unbound read an item named "@entry", whose count was 0 as rope's was, so a codex of resources now reads each entry's own amount; and a codex tab that lost its known still compiled clean, so the hut's codex is checked tab by tab. 8 through the built page, each caught. Before planting, six that would have gone unseen got checks: the HUD taking a mode already shown, the HUD made before the pages, records named by their ids, the roster's starting members ignored, the modes other screens show not read, and a logged file credited that isn't there.
- **M4.5 UI designer** (D55, your calls). Done:
  - **An outline, a form and a frame** (your call), stacked in the inspector's column and the phone's sheet. The outline is the screen's slots, widgets and tabs as rows, each marked with its problems, whether it shows now and an override at the frame's size. Its bar adds a widget (into a slot, inside a container, or after), and moves one Up, Down, In and Out, duplicates it, wraps it in a stack, adds a tab and deletes; a handle, ⋮⋮, drags a widget among its siblings, by mouse or finger, and only the handle drags. The form is the record form's own fields over the screen file, the layout fields writing `at` for the frame's breakpoint alone once "Change layout at … only" is ticked. Each change is one step of the editor's undo, its keys written in the fixtures' order.
  - **Device frames** at 360×740, 390×844, tablet (820×1180) and desktop (1280×800), each turned to landscape, the breakpoint the device's own; fitted by one scale, after Dirtbag's fit, into the panel's width and 60% of the window's height. The frame is inert: a tap selects the deepest widget under it, ringed in the editor's colours.
  - **The frame draws the Play panel's moment, with samples in the gaps** (your call): a game of the designer's own, a copy of the Play worker, compiled without `ui/`, so a screen half made stops nothing, and again only when a change names a file outside `ui/`; loaded to the Play panel's last save, or started from seed 1, and asked by a new `look` request for the screen's facts and conditions, taking no step. What the moment lacks reads as a sample, `‹name›`; with no game, everything does, and the line above the frame says which it draws and why.
  - **Screens and the theme designed, their JSON kept** (your call): Design and JSON are two views of the same file and undo; a screen opens designed, and a problem's line opens its JSON. The theme gets colour pickers, each text pair's contrast shown as it is picked against 4.5:1, as E050 computes it, and a sample screen in the theme. The shell and feedback stay JSON.
  - **The gate** (your call): in the editor on desktop, the hut's own screen and its journal, set aside, are made again from blank screens in the designer alone, typing no JSON, and each file comes out as the fixture's JSON, key for key. Every device frame, upright and turned, draws the hut with nothing past its edges, and the hut plays on them in the Play panel, the frame then drawing the Play panel's moment, a day on. At 360 and 390 px the same two screens are made by touch in the inspector's sheet, a widget picked by a tap on the frame, no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds. On a phone held sideways, 844×390, the hut's screen is designed with its frame turned to landscape and a widget dragged by a finger, in reach. A drag by mouse on desktop and the theme's contrast as a colour is picked have specs of their own.
  - [`docs/specs/designer.md`](docs/specs/designer.md) is the design, as built; the survey's fifth look is in [`docs/research/runtime-ui.md`](docs/research/runtime-ui.md).
  - **Found on the way:**
    - a half-typed argument name, its key undefined, made the renderer throw as it matched a button to the actions on offer: `fill.ts` now matches only keys the offer has, and a render test holds it;
    - the frame dropped a list's own fields (`empty`, `type`, `known`, `shows-when`, `at`): the code that makes a half-made file drawable spread a function, not the widget's fields. Found writing the spec as built; a test now keeps every kind's fields, seen failing on the bug;
    - the script worker answered `look` as no request of Play's, so the frame drew samples: its list of Play's requests lacked it;
    - the specs' `.eb-game` matched the frame's game too: the frame carries `eb-designed`, and the specs' game leaves it out;
    - the blank template wrote `shows: []`, which means the same as no `shows`, so a journal made from it, opened only by a button, kept a key the fixture's lacks: the blank has none, and the screen's form drops the key when its last mode is unticked;
    - a journal's Close and the hut's bottom row are a button inside a row stack, which Add can't make around a button already there: Wrap in stack does;
    - the moment's own rules, ui/ left out and a compile or load only when needed, had no unit test: they have one, each rule seen failing;
    - one full e2e run failed on a toast axe caught mid-fade, as low contrast: the axe check waits up to 2 s for what is running to an end, seen failing with the fade slowed to 1.5 s;
    - on desktop at 900 px tall, the outline sits below a frame of up to 60% of the window, so a drag spec has to scroll to it first; whether that is the right split on a laptop is untested for feel.
  - **Untested for feel:** the frame's 60% of the window, the outline's rows and marks, the handle's drag by a real finger, the sample marks, and the device list. No real phone has run the designer.
  - **52 planted bugs:** 35 at the unit level, of which 34 were caught. The survivor put Duplicate's copy before the widget, not after: the two are the same and the selection lands on the second either way, so no check can tell them apart. 17 through the built page, each caught: the frame's boxes and its fit, the moment always the start, `look` refused, JSON the first view, a problem's line not opening the JSON, "only here" writing every size, an argument's name not written, the Play panel's save not kept, the frame not looked at again when it moves, the handle under 44 px, a drop never made, the handle taking touch scrolling, the pointer not held, the frame not inert, the contrast always passing, and the row itself dragging. Before planting, four things that would have gone unseen got checks: the moment's rules in `lookAt`, a phone held sideways, a drag by a finger, and the frame taking no focus; two of the plants above, the touch scrolling and the frame not inert, were run again to see they failed at those checks.
- **M4.6 World map** (D56, your calls). Done:
  - **A map kit** (your call), ticked in the Project panel: `node-kind` and `map` records; travel the action `maps/travel`, one price (`travelOf`) read by the action, its options and the widget, its verdict the events kit's; arriving runs the kind's effects, then the node's; `enter-map` and `reveal-map`; facts for conditions (`maps.on`, `maps.at`, `maps.at-end`, `maps.layer`, `maps.entered`, `maps.visited`). A map is saved whole as it is made, so a change to its rules never moves a node under a save.
  - **Layered maps made by rules** (your call): a start, layers of one to four nodes and an end; links between neighbouring layers each way (space-wrecked's), an extra by chance that crosses none, directed or both ways; kinds placed by counts within layer windows, apart or avoidable, then rolled from a weighted mix; names from each kind's list, shuffled once a map. Valid by construction, never retried: a rule some seeds can't keep is found over seeds 1 to 300 (W135), the first such seed named. Maps drawn by hand: nodes at places, roads with time, cost and one way, a move taking the quickest road (dirtbag's `road()`). Checks: E035, E053, W119, W120, W135, W136.
  - **A map widget that follows the screen** (your call): bottom to top in a column that scrolls to the party on a phone, left to right and fitted otherwise; across a layer, nodes share the width, each a button at least 44 px; a tap picks one, its moves under the map saying what they cost, take and do by the sim's own preview ("Jump · 1 Supplies · heat 0 → 2") and why one is shut. The view is enter-map's preview of the map the party is on. A Map stock screen shows each mode that travels, the HUD of a game that starts on its map; the designer knows the widget and draws a sample map.
  - **The World panel:** maps and kinds; New layered map, New drawn map, New kind; above a layered map's form, the map a seed makes, Next seed, and the check over 300 seeds rule by rule in words, Open seed showing the first that breaks one; above a drawn map's, its places dragged by mouse or finger, one step of undo each, a tap opening the row.
  - **The gate** (your call): the drift, a slice of Space-Wrecked's sector run, is brought in without its sector and its way in; the kind is made with New kind and the sector's rules in the World panel with forms, each file landing as the fixture holds it; every rule is kept in 300 of 300 seeds; a rule broken on purpose names its first bad seed, which opens, and is mended; the pilot's 20 games in the Logic panel read as `eb sim` prints them; a move shut by its condition, Warp, is added with forms and drawn shut, saying why; and a player flies it to the exit through the map widget, the first jump's button reading "Jump · 1 Supplies · heat 0 → 2", the party's node picked after every move. The valley, in Dirtbag's style, is drawn there with forms, a place dragged and undone, and its drives run as `eb sim` runs them, each taking the quickest road. On phones, 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390, the drift is flown to its exit by touch, the map and an event each in reach with no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds, and the valley's van is dragged by a finger and undone. `driftScreens.test.ts` plays the drift through its stock screens in the worker's host, the map's enabled moves the travels on offer at every step.
  - [`docs/specs/maps.md`](docs/specs/maps.md) is the design, as built; [`docs/research/world-maps.md`](docs/research/world-maps.md) the survey.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - the check over 300 seeds threw mid-draw on a rule half typed (at least above at most), before the index had checked it, and Preact left the map's form drawn four times over in the inspector: the preview's check gives no rows for rules that make no map, with a test;
    - a layered map's nodes overlapped in a 290 px inspector, three to a layer, each up to 80 px wide on a board inset both ways: across a layer, nodes now share the full width, each no wider than its share, and the e2e checks no two overlap;
    - a game starting on its map would have had a HUD showing nothing (W134): the game's catalog lists the modes that travel, the Map template shows them, and the HUD isn't offered there;
    - a planted bug showed a road both ways beside a one-way road between the same two places went one way only, and that of two roads the same way the first written was taken, not the quicker: roads are now marked one-way each, and the quicker is taken, with tests;
    - the e2e's Play helper read Run its scripts before the panel drew it, and skipped it: it now waits for Play;
    - a full e2e run once saw the roster spec's kit checkbox not change under load; it passed four of four alone. Not this milestone's code; to watch;
    - a planted-bug pass found no check that the party's node is scrolled into the phone's column, nor that nodes keep apart: `expectMapLaidOut` checks both, and that links are drawn.
  - **Untested for feel:** the board's 76 px rows and 100 px columns, node buttons of at most 80 px with two lines of name, the picked node's moves under the map, the drag's 6 px slop, and the preview's check at every edit on a phone. No real phone has run a map.
  - **83 planted bugs:** 67 at the unit level, 57 of the first 64 caught. Each survivor got a test and was then caught: a kind placed outside its layer window; the cheaper of two roads as quick; a one-way road beside a road both ways, whose test found the bug above; two of a kind kept apart in one layer, which the generator never makes, so the check is tested on a map made by hand; E053's words for a layered map; "1 hour"; and a change to nothing said. The road fix's own three were caught, one after a further test (two roads the same way, the slow one written first). 16 through the built page, 12 caught at first. Moves not on offer left enabled, the picked node not reset as the party arrives, and nothing picked until a tap each got a check, then were caught: a move shut by its condition, Warp, added with forms and drawn shut with why; "You are here" at the start and after each move. A node's width cap dropped survives the e2e, whose sectors are never wide enough for it to bite, and fails the layout's unit test.
- **M4.7 Encounters** (D57, your calls). Done:
  - **An encounters kit, needing Stats** ([specs/encounters.md](docs/specs/encounters.md)), ticked in the Project panel: `combat` records name the stats a fight reads (health, hit, dodge, damage, armour) and its bounds (a hit's chance kept within 5 to 95%, 20 rounds); `encounter` records name the combat, the foes and their counts, who fights for the player, when the foes break (100% of their health by default), what a win, a draw and a loss do, and what falling does (a status, and leaving the roster). Facts: `encounters.fought`, `won` and `lost`, by encounter; the roster kit gains `roster.place`.
  - **Rounds worked out exactly** (your call): two lines, each striking the other's front, what is left over falling on the next; every pair of health lost worked out round by round until the foes break, the party is down, or the rounds run out, which is a draw. The ends are made whole, out of 10,000: each result a whole percent, exactly what is shown, its hurts shared out in hundredths. One roll picks the row; the party's hurt is then dealt front first, and those it fells take the encounter's falls.
  - **A fight is an effect** (your call: inside events), `fight`, in a choice's effects or anywhere else a list of effects sits, with a stance (hit, dodge, damage and armour added) for a Charge or a Run in close beside it. Who fights (your call): the fight's fighters, else the encounter's, else the roster's members in the order they joined, those at 0 health left out. E035 for a combat whose hit bounds cross, or a fighter named twice; E054 for a fight that names nobody, nor does its encounter, without the roster; W137 for a falls' `leave` without the roster.
  - **Odds on the button:** where the event shows odds, a fight's choice says each result with what it costs and does, from the fight's own preview: "Win 67%, 0–10 Hull lost, Credits 12 → 24 · Draw 16%, …". Buttons gain `detail`, a second line left out while its bindings are empty, which the stock event and talk screens fill with `{.odds}`, and the designer offers.
  - **In the editor:** the kit box, the two records' forms and the effect's; rows in words ("fight thraex with spartacus: hit +15, dodge −15"); and above an encounter's form, the fight worked out as the sim works it, against its fighters, the roster's start or one actor picked, at full health, each result with its chance, hurt, falls and effects, changing as a stat or a foe is typed.
  - **The gate** (your call): the drift, brought in without its combat, its raider and their encounter, and with the hostile ship's choices fighting nothing, has each made with forms, landing as the fixture holds it; the raiders' table above their form reads as the sim works it out; Fight and Run in close are given their fights with forms. Flown from the Play panel's seed to its first hostile ship, both choices' odds read as the table, and the fight is fought. In the pit, a slice of Ludus, a thraex bout is made with forms and Spartacus sent from the roster, its table read for him alone. Both projects' bots in the Logic panel read as `eb sim` prints them. On phones at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390, the drift is flown by touch through its first fight to its exit, the event in reach with no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds, and the raiders' table is read in the inspector's sheet, its party picked by touch. `fights.test.ts` checks over 300 seeds of each that every fight the bots fight lands in a row of the table its choice showed, and that the wins sit inside the Wilson interval of the chances shown.
  - [`docs/specs/encounters.md`](docs/specs/encounters.md) is the design, as built; [`docs/research/encounters.md`](docs/research/encounters.md) the survey.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - the drift's first raiders were won 99.95% of the time: their guns and hull went up, and the close stance's armour went down, until a fight was worth a choice;
    - `roster.place` was first worded as its label alone, naming no actor, where every other roster fact names its own: it says "ana’s place on the roster is 1" now, with a test;
    - a choice's effects before its fight aren't in the fight's preview: Run in close adds 5 heat, then fights, so its draw shows heat 15 → 20 where it will be 20 → 25. The chances and the hurt are right. Open question 4 in the spec;
    - the e2e's Play helper, shared with M4.6's specs, left the drift's scripts unrun on a phone held sideways under a full run's load: Run its scripts is drawn once trust is read, which came after Play, so the helper's one look found nothing to press and Play stayed shut. It looks again until Play is enabled; seen failing twice before, and passing with M4.6's specs beside it after.
  - **Untested for feel:** every number of both fights, the stances, the odds' words on a button, and the table's rows above a form. No real phone has fought.
  - **64 planted bugs:** 57 at the unit level, of which 54 were caught. Of the three survivors, one was code that did nothing, and went: `shares` kept values of 0 out of the units handed out, which a value of 0, having no remainder, never gets. Two got checks and, planted again, were caught: a combat's floor on a hit's chance when it sets none, 5%, and the stock event and talk screens' `{.odds}` line. Two more were planted with the new checks, each caught: the ceiling, 95%, and `roster.place`'s words. 5 through the built page, of which 4 were caught: a button's second line never drawn, or drawn empty; the table not above the encounter's form; and the party picked ignored. Before planting, the pit's spec found the picked line inside the line as written ("Crixus, Spartacus, …"), so the last would have passed; its match is exact now. The survivor drops the form's value from the keys the table is worked out again on: it is still worked out again a moment later, as the edit reaches the record index, which no check measures.
- **M4.8 Audio** (D58, your calls). Done:
  - **Sounds as layered ZzFX voices** (your call, [specs/audio.md](docs/specs/audio.md)): `sound` records of one to three voices, each ZzFX 1.4.0's parameters by name and a start, rendered by the engine's own port of ZzFX's `buildSamples` (MIT, its notice kept) with its one `Math.random` a roll the caller passes, so a sound is the same samples in the editor, the player and Node. The port is held byte for byte to the `zzfx` package over 200 random sounds. A sound plays at ZzFX's level, its pitch moved per play by its first voice's randomness.
  - **Ambience made by the engine, music from files** (your call): `ambience` records of noise (white, pink or brown through a filter) and drone layers, each an 8-second loop with no seam, its level wandering by its sway, and sounds now and then; `music` records of stems, audio files in `assets/` with their provenance, started together and looped. Each layer, sound now and then and stem plays while its condition holds. The topmost screen on the stack that names an ambience or a music plays it (`mixOf`, a plain function); the player follows, crossfading. Pack format 8 carries the stems as base64, so an exported game plays offline.
  - **Cues on the feedback lines** (your call): a line gains `sound` and `buzz` (tap, knock, thud, double, alarm), `say` optional; the first line that matches a beat feels it; the worker sends each step's cues with its words; one buzz a step, the heaviest. The theme names the sounds of a press, a screen opening and Back. Checks: E049 for a screen's ambience or music, a theme's sound or a line's sound that names no record; E047 for a line with nothing to say, sound or buzz, or `to` without `say`.
  - **Buses and volumes:** master, music, ambience, effects and interface, each with headroom; the shell's settings gain Sound, Music, Ambience and Effects, Quieter and Louder in tens, and Vibration where the device can vibrate. Nothing is made until a press, Sound at 0 makes no audio context at all, a hidden page suspends, and `window.__ebAudio` says what the page is doing, for the e2e.
  - **In the editor:** Sound, Ambience and Music in Records after a project's own types; above a sound's form, Play, its samples drawn, its length and peak, Start from a preset (Blip, Pickup, Jump, Hit, Laser, Explosion, Power-up, Step), Vary and Paste ZzFX as a new voice, each one step of undo; above an ambience's or a music's, Play and Stop through the game's own player, each condition ticked to hear it as if it held. The designer's screen form picks an ambience and a music, the theme designer the interface's sounds.
  - **The gate** (your call: the hut, checked by machine): the hut gains five sounds, its wind (with rain from day 4 and a door now and then) and the storm's, a music of one stem the engine's synth rendered (`hut-theme.ts`, logged as the project's own), sounds and buzzes on four feedback lines and the theme's click. The Phase 3 spec makes them in the editor: the stem imported in Assets with its provenance, each sound pasted in voice by voice as ZzFX's designer copies it, the ambiences and the music made with forms, each landing as the fixture holds it; the export is still the fixture file for file, the stem byte for byte. `audio.spec.ts` hears the hut through the hook in the Play panel and at `/play/`: no context before a press, the hut's ambience and music, the storm's while its screen is up, the rain once day 4 comes, quiet once the hut is shut, every line's sound and buzz (`navigator.vibrate` stood in for), Sound at 0 with no context, Music at 0 its bus at 0, a hidden page suspended, and the previews. `audioPhone.spec.ts`, at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390, turns the volumes by touch and plays and varies a sound in the inspector's sheet, in reach. `hutAudio.test.ts` holds every hut sound and layer to a pinned hash, under full scale as played.
  - [`docs/specs/audio.md`](docs/specs/audio.md) is the design, as built; [`docs/research/audio.md`](docs/research/audio.md) the survey of eleven games.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - the player made its audio context as a game opened whenever Sound was above 0, before any press, against its own rule: only a press, or Sound raised from 0, starts it now, and the spec checks the hook reads no context at the title;
    - the three audio types were engine types, which Records hid as it hides flags and sheets, so a sound couldn't be made there: Records lists them after a project's own types, so a project's first type is still the table it opens on;
    - the designer's pickers showed bare ids where the record form's show "Hut (hut)": they share the record form's names now;
    - the provenance form asks for a Tool only of generated art, so the hut's stem names the engine's synth in its source line; open question 4 in the spec;
    - the hut's credits now list its stem, so on a phone held sideways the stock Credits page scrolls with only text in it, which axe flagged: a screen's main region that scrolls takes a tab stop now, with a ring inside its edge;
    - an empty project's Records panel now has a Type list, whose options two older specs counted as the command palette's: they look in the palette alone; and a screen file read mid-write threw in the designer's specs, where it now reads as not there yet.
    - the Play panel and Export game packed a music's stems empty, so the hut's music was silent in the editor and in a game exported from it: what they send the worker carried every asset as its name alone. Found by a planted bug's new check (below); audio assets go whole now.
  - **Untested for feel:** every sound, both ambiences and the stem's mix, the presets' ranges, Vary's tenth, the buses' headroom, the buzzes' lengths, and the crossfades. No one has listened in CI, and no real phone has played the hut's audio.
  - **Untested for speed on a phone:** a layer renders on the page's thread as its ambience starts, 10 to 29 ms each in Node, so the storm's three layers stall a page for about 50 ms there, and likely more on a phone. Not measured on a phone.
  - **70 planted bugs:** 48 at the unit level. My first run of them counted every one caught, which proved nothing: the commands that ran two test folders were joined wrongly and failed on any tree. Run again with each command first seen passing on the clean tree, 38 were caught. Each of the 10 survivors got a check and was then caught: a drone that doesn't fit its loop (the crossfade hides it at the seam, but not the dip in loudness where its two ends cancel), a noise's default filter, ZzFX's defaults written out, a line that only buzzes, the buses' two gains, E049 for a screen's music and a theme's sound, a sound's three voices at most, and only a music's stems packed. One check first caught its bug by a 15-minute timeout, a failed comparison diffing 176,400 samples; renders are compared as yes or no now. 22 through the built page, 15 caught. Of the 7 survivors, one was code that did nothing, and went: Stop above a loop's form disposed of the player its effect already disposes of. Six got checks and, planted again, were caught: each sound's bus and each layer's and stem's gain, which the hook now says; Sound at 0 suspending a running context; the title going quiet; a volume press moving one step; and a preset drawn afresh each time. The gains check found a bug no plant had put there: the Play panel and Export game packed a music's stems empty, since what they send the worker carried every asset as its name alone, so the hut's music was silent in the editor and in a game exported from it, while the mix still named it. Audio assets go whole now, with a unit test and the play spec checking the exported stem byte for byte, each seen failing with the fix taken out.
- **M4.9 Play-in-Editor** (D59, your calls). Done:
  - **Run any effect** (your call, [specs/play-in-editor.md](docs/specs/play-in-editor.md)): the Play panel changes a game only by its own effects, run through the sim as a step of their own, `debug/run`, from the effects form content uses, with Preview from the game's own `preview()`. The engine's `debug` module is built only into the games the editor opens; its action is allowed in every mode and offered in none, a game's own module can't take its name, and its slice counts the debug steps in the state's history. A debugged save stays in the editor: the shell offers no Save as file or Copy code for its slot, and the player page refuses it before replaying it.
  - **Replay after edits** (your call): the run in play is recorded from its start, each step with its state hash, up to 10,000 steps; a loaded save's log is played into it. Step back plays the run again to the step before, held to each hash; Step forward takes the next one again. Replay plays the recording on the project as it is now and stops at the first step refused or that leaves another state, naming it, the first slice and path that changed with both values, and the other slices that changed; the game goes on from there under the edited project, its screens too.
  - **Calendar steps, step back** (your call): +1 of each of the calendar's lengths, by `wait`, so timers, jobs and start-of rows fire as a played day's do; back is Step back.
  - **The rest of the panel:** each quest with why it hasn't moved on (a count's events so far, a condition in words and whether it holds, days and a job's time left), Set stage and Start; the state slice by slice with its facet hash, opened to its JSON; named states kept as saves of their own, restored and deleted; the log with each step's beats, debug steps and refusals marked, steps taken back dimmed. One section opens at a time.
  - **The gate** (your call: the hut, checked by machine): `debug.spec.ts` plays the hut two days in the Play panel and reads them in the log; keeps a state; waits a day, which brings the storm as a played day would; finds the slot can't be saved out; steps back, the storm gone, and forward; reads the loads quest's why ("Carry five loads up: 2 of 5") and sets its stage; runs an effect made with the form, after its preview; reads the debug slice's count; restores the state; then edits the loads quest by its form and Replay stops at step 1, naming it ("in economy, /amounts/coins was nothing, now 40; factions and quests changed too"). `debugPhone.spec.ts` works the same tools by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390, each section in reach with no target under 44 px and nothing axe finds, and Replays the project unchanged. `host.test.ts` drives the worker's debug host on the hut in Node.
  - [`docs/specs/play-in-editor.md`](docs/specs/play-in-editor.md) is the design, as built; [`docs/research/play-in-editor.md`](docs/research/play-in-editor.md) the survey of sixteen of your games.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - Replay first named the first changed slice by its key alone, so editing the loads quest reported economy's coins and said nothing of quests: it names every other slice that changed now;
    - a test helper read the log's lines with their parts run together ("1Carry"): it reads each part apart;
    - a state kept after a step back holds only the steps after it, since the save then starts where the game stood: the recording in the worker keeps the run, the save doesn't (in the spec);
    - the bench's first season stopped at three steps, carrying when only the storm's choices were offered: it takes what the game offers now;
    - CI failed M4.8's audio spec once: its wait for the title's quiet matched `{ levels: {}, gains: {} }`, which any levels match, so it read the ambience before the quiet landed, and this milestone's shell changes moved the timing. The wait matches exactly now (`expectQuiet`), passed 9 of 9, and fails when the title stays loud.
  - **Untested for feel:** the panel's wording, one section open at a time, the log's 200 lines, and the state as JSON on a phone. No real phone has run the debug tools.
  - **22 planted bugs:** 19 at the unit level, 3 through the built page, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; all 22 caught. Four checks were added first, so that a bug in each would fail one: step back's hash check (a tampered recording), Step forward keeping the steps further ahead, a condition's truth in a quest's why, and the kernel's slice named only when nothing else changed.
- **M4.10 Export** (D60, your calls). Done:
  - **All four targets, checked by machine** (your call, [specs/export.md](docs/specs/export.md)): `export.json` beside project.json names each target's edition, the web build's domain and the Android app's package and key fingerprints. The **web** build is a folder: the page, the runtime named by its content, the manifest, five icons, `version.json` and a service worker whose caches are named by its scope and the build's hash, deleting only its own, installing past the HTTP cache, waiting for the player's "Reload to update" on the title, and never answering for `version.json` or `/.well-known/`. The **file** is one HTML page that plays from `file://`, its worker started from a blob URL. The **itch.io** zip has relative paths and no service worker. The **Android** app is a workflow, from Dirtbag's `build-twa.yml`, written only for a web build on its own domain, its `assetlinks.json` written with the fingerprints.
  - **Publish is a repository you push** (your call): Publish to GitHub saves a zip of the web build under `site/`, a Pages workflow that deploys it and then waits for the live `version.json` to name the build, the Android workflow and `twa-manifest.json` with it, and a README with three steps; `eb publish --repo` writes the same into a git working tree, keeps its README, commits and pushes with `--push`. The editor makes no network call.
  - **Editions are leave-out lists with a leak check** (your call): an edition leaves out records, types, scenes and assets, and is compiled from the project's files without them. E055 names each kept record that still names one, in Problems as you edit; E056 and W138, W139 check export.json. The leak check reads every file of the edition's build, the runtime's own text taken out first, for each left-out id as a string by itself and each left-out text of 12 characters or more, in every form the page holds them; the full build is its control, each thing looked for found there or the export fails.
  - **Package ids are `com.rcjlabs.<game>`** (your call), which closes open question 4.
  - **The runtime** is `apps/editor/src/game`, built by `vite.game.config.ts` into one script and one stylesheet, the worker inside; the editor keeps it at `runtime/` for offline use, and `eb export` reads it from `dist-game/`. The same project gives the same bytes from either: the hut's web build is `3ac04cd77773` from both.
  - **In the editor:** the Project panel's Export section draws export.json as a form, editions offered as targets' choices, with Export web, Export file, Export for itch.io and Publish to GitHub, and says what each wrote and what its leak check found.
  - **The gate** (your call: the hut, exported and played by machine; installing on a phone is a worth-a-look): `export.spec.ts` exports the hut from the editor and plays the single file from `file://`, saving across a reload; serves the web build at a path with the e2e's own server, its service worker controlling the page, reloads it offline and plays on, then serves a new build, whose "Reload to update" the title offers and which then takes over; plays the itch.io zip from a folder of its own with no worker; makes a taster leaving out a map of the summit, sees E055 in Problems while a kept record names it, exports a clean taster and then, with a script naming the map, is refused with the place named and nothing saved; and checks Publish's repository with its workflows and Android files. `exportPhone.spec.ts` does the export by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 and plays the file at that size, in reach with nothing axe finds. `sw.test.ts` runs the service worker in Node against a stand-in global.
  - [`docs/specs/export.md`](docs/specs/export.md) is the design, as built; [`docs/research/export.md`](docs/research/export.md) the survey of how the games we have ship.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - a worker made with `new URL(…, import.meta.url)` anywhere the runtime imports makes Vite emit it as a file of its own, which a single page would leave behind: the player page's worker moved to a file the runtime never imports, and the runtime's build fails if it makes anything but its two files;
    - the exported page had no main landmark and no level-one heading, which axe found in landscape: `#game` is a `main` now, with the game's name as a heading for screen readers;
