    - Paeth's choice between left and above differs only on a tie that forces them equal, so a planted swap of the two was no bug at all; the PNG test now reads back rows of mixed bytes under every filter.
  - **Untested for feel:** the export section's wording, the drawn icon, and the README's three steps. No real phone has installed an exported game, and no Android bundle has been built: the workflow is checked as text, and its first run on GitHub is its real test.
  - **25 planted bugs:** 21 at the unit level, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 19 caught at once. Of the two survivors, the build's hash read without the files' bytes survived because every test that changed a byte also changed a name: a test now changes the description alone, and catches it. The other, Paeth's left and above swapped, was no bug: the two choices differ only on a tie that forces them equal. A real one, the predictor left out, is caught by a new test of mixed bytes under every filter. 4 through the built page, all caught: no update offered on the title, the edition ignored by the Play worker, a leak saved anyway, and a service worker that answers nothing.
- **M4.11 Templates** (D61, your calls). Done:
  - **Two original games** (your call, [specs/templates.md](docs/specs/templates.md)), each an ordinary project folder under `templates/`, listed by `templates/templates.json`:
    - **Salvage coast**, the Expedition run, with seven kits. The harbour (`scripts/harbour.ts`) offers the chandlery, the contract board, the yard and the harbourmaster. A voyage is the coast, a layered map made fresh each time, of wrecks and storms drawn from decks, raiders fought with their odds shown, a cove and open water. It ends free and clear when the debt is paid, seized when the fifth voyage ends with it standing, or lost at sea when the hull runs out.
    - **The last ferry**, the Story/choice: one night's crossing, four passengers, six ink scenes whose choices set flags, a quest for each passenger, and three endings that the landing picks and the ending screen shows.
  - **Make it yours** (your call): `guide.json`, steps shown in the Project panel above Export, each with an Open button that goes where a problem in its file would go: a record's form at the field, a script, a scene, the theme, or a panel. E057 checks its shape and W140 a step whose file the project lacks or the editor can't open. Zips carry it and packs don't. Remove the guide is one step, which undo brings back.
  - **New project's Start from** (your call): Empty, Expedition run, or Story and choices, with a blurb under the choice. Each template is a lazy chunk of the editor, precached, so it works offline. The project takes the author's name in project.json, export.json (with a short name that fits in 12) and the title screen, and its scripts are trusted, since they came with the editor. `eb new <folder> --template <id> [--name <text>]` writes the same files through the compiler's `fromTemplate`.
  - **The gate, all by machine** (your call):
    - `tools/cli/test/templates.test.ts` validates each template with no problems, warnings included;
    - it plays 500 seeds of each with its own bot to its golden file, with no offer refused;
    - it reaches every stage of every quest and every ending over those seeds.
    - `templates.spec.ts` makes a project from each in the editor and follows every step to the place it names. It lowers the coast's debt to 40 by the second step's form, plays to an ending in the Play panel by pressing only what the stock screens draw, and exports one file that plays from `file://` under the new name.
    - `templatesPhone.spec.ts` does the same by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390, with each screen checked for fit, 44 px targets and axe the first time it shows.
    - A third desktop test renames the raider the guide opens, which the encounter and the step follow, then deletes it, and finds W140 in Problems opening the Project panel.
  - [`docs/specs/templates.md`](docs/specs/templates.md) is the design, as built; [`docs/research/templates.md`](docs/research/templates.md) the survey behind it.
  - **Found on the way:**
    - **Renaming a record followed references only in the project's own type files.** Since M3.3, renaming a kit's or the engine's record left its references broken: an encounter's foe, an event's fight, a choice's cost, a quest in an effect. The rename now also rewrites every reference the record index finds to it, at its pointer, read from the working copy as the step runs, and moves guide.json's steps that open the file. Conditions, a screen's text, scenes and scripts that name it are still left (templates.md, open question 4).
    - The exported title screen showed the template's own title: `fromTemplate` names ui/shell.json's title too.
    - A name over 12 characters started with W138: the short name is its first whole words that fit.
    - A weighted bot rarely paid the debt, so the free ending wasn't reached: the coast's bot is a script, `skipper`. Played at random, the coast is lost at sea in 81.0% of runs and free in 0.2%.
    - W108 calls a flag that only a screen reads never read. The ferry's crossing quest reads its ending flag as well (templates.md, open question 3; logged below).
    - On a phone held sideways the inspector is a side zone's tab panel, not a region: the specs find it by its panel.
    - The zip test didn't cover guide.json or export.json at the root; it does now.
    - **CI found a race in the command palette, older than this milestone.** Escape shuts the dialog, but its close event comes a task later. Ctrl+K pressed in between set a flag that was already true, then the close event shut it. In a page's own log that happens about one round in ten. The palette now follows the dialog's `cancel`, which comes as it shuts, and ignores a close that arrives once it's open again. A layout test presses Escape and Ctrl+K back to back thirty times; with either half taken out, it fails.
  - **Logged for later:** W108 should count screens' conditions and text as reading a flag. Rename should offer to change conditions, screens' text, scenes and scripts it can parse.
  - **Untested for feel:** both games' writing and balance, the guide's wording, and the coast's odds. With its own bot it ends free in 23.8% of runs, seized in 50.8% and lost in 25.4%. No real phone has made or played either template.
  - **35 planted bugs:** 31 at the unit level, each command first seen passing on the clean tree.
    - Of the first 23 (guide.json's checks, the zip, `fromTemplate`, the bundles, the step's place, `eb new`, both modules and a guide's file), 20 were caught.
    - The three survivors were guide.json's problems on their way to the Problems panel: where one opens, the list, and the open project's index. The first two got unit tests and the third an e2e (a step's file deleted), and each, planted again, was caught.
    - Of 8 more, in the tab's count of guide problems and the rename's new code, 6 were caught. One didn't apply because Biome had reformatted its line, and was caught once planted on the new line.
    - The last survivor, a filter keeping the pointers to records' files, did nothing: a scene's file isn't JSON, so rewriting leaves it as it is. It went.
    - 4 through the built page, all caught: the template's scripts not trusted, Remove the guide removing nothing, a step's Open doing nothing, and New project ignoring Start from.
- **M4.12 Proof #1: Space-Wrecked acts 1–2.**
  - The hangar job board and shop → launch → the sector run (events, contracts, stations, factions, crew, heat) → the settle choice.
  - Fights auto-resolve, because the builder and boarding tactics wait for Phase 7.

**Done when:**

- An import script maps space-wrecked's events (29), quests (11), contracts (29), factions, traits, encounters, recruits and companions with zero validation errors. Anything that needs an engine change is logged as an engine issue: content needing engine edits means the engine is wrong. [auto]
- Bots finish 500 seeded runs, and every act 1–2 quest step is reachable. [auto]
- It is playable from Pages on your phone in landscape, and it installs. [you]
- A UI-only export stays within a size budget set from a measured reading. [auto]

**Where it stands** (D62, your calls): the three [auto] gates pass and it is deployed (#47, 2026-10-23); the [you] gate waits on your phone.

- **The game** is `games/space-wrecked/`, an ordinary project folder ([specs/space-wrecked.md](docs/specs/space-wrecked.md)), live at [`engine.rcjlabs.com/games/space-wrecked/`](https://engine.rcjlabs.com/games/space-wrecked/). Its text is all rights reserved, RCJLabs (your call). `pnpm build` exports it beside the editor, whose service worker leaves `/games/` alone, and CI's confirm job loads it live.
- **The import** (`pnpm import:space-wrecked`) writes all 455 of its records from a copy of the original's data in `source/`, among them 79 parts, 40 hull slots, 3 shops, 7 crew jobs, 20 traits, 40 recruits, 4 companions, 15 enemies, 35 encounters, 40 events, 5 decks, 13 loot tables, 4 factions, 12 stations, 11 quests, 29 contracts, 3 backgrounds, 3 pursuers, the two maps and the balance. `eb validate` finds no errors and two warnings, both on flags only scripts touch. A test runs the import again and holds it to the committed records, byte for byte.
- **The scripts** are five modules (campaign, refit, crew, board, run) and the bot. The job board is a made event whose jobs are made encounters, so each job's odds on its button are the fight's (your call: fights by the encounters kit). Parts are fitted to the hull as gear, and Launch waits on the flight checklist (your call).
- **The gate** (`tools/cli/test/spaceWrecked.test.ts`): 500 seeds of the captain all settle, none refused, every act 1–2 stage of every quest reached, each run matching its golden run. The captain's runs for 4 seeds are played again through the worker's host, its every action and every other action on offer a button its screens draw. `spaceWrecked.spec.ts` plays the opening by touch sideways and upright, opens the game beside an installed editor, and holds the export to 540,000 bytes.
- **Engine issues:** fixed are a gear slot taking items by tag, a recruit's traits shuffled, a hall's offers as facts, a map's name as a fact, and an `offered` list's `takes`, so a screen can list part of what's on offer. Logged below are 3 to 8 of [the spec's list](docs/specs/space-wrecked.md#engine-issues).
- **Found on the way:**
  - The same fight table was worked out several times a turn: a choice's odds, the bot's look at them and the fight. Working them out was 54% of a run, so `endsOf` keeps its last 64, keyed by every number that shapes them, and a test changes each of those numbers to see the table worked out again.
  - With the encounters kit's default of fighting to the end, the start crew's odds were far from the original's. Each fight's `breaks` now brings them within a few points (Readings).
  - The captain never bought a part: a shop offers only what the purse covers, and it was looking for the wrong slots. It now buys the cheapest part a failing line of the checklist needs.
  - W108 and W109 count records only, so a flag that only scripts read or set is warned about. That is the same gap M4.11 logged for screens.
  - **The refit screen listed everything on offer**, Launch and Undock among its parts, since a list of `offered` couldn't be narrowed. That also meant the screens test couldn't fail: any action was a button on the refit screen. A planted bug, the docked screen's Patch button removed, survived and showed it. Lists of `offered` now take `takes` (E049 checks it; the designer shows it as Only these actions). The screens test now holds every action on offer at every step to a button, not only the captain's, and the e2e checks the refit dialog lists only Fit and Strip.
  - **CI found a type error in the importer** (a balance number possibly missing) that a local `pnpm -s typecheck | tail` hid: silent mode drops the child scripts' errors, and the pipe hides the exit code. Checks are now read by their exit codes.
- **Logged for later:** a fee by rank on recruits; a kit effect on a random member (`wound`); restock on demand; a resource's capacity that moves with gear; a squad of N for encounters; a map node made known from afar; W108 and W109 counting scripts.
- **One e2e failure not explained:** the upright opening failed once in 15 local runs, while the 500-seed test ran beside it. It passed 14 reruns, with and without that load, and its error wasn't kept. The one read in it that didn't wait for the page now waits.
- **Untested for feel:** everything. The tuning is a stand-in matched only to the start crews' odds, the approach phase between ships isn't carried, the screens' words and layout are a first pass, and no real phone has played or installed it.
- **27 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree.
  - Of the first 22, 20 were caught:
    - in the scripts, the board's pay, breaks and won event, the hunters, quest placement, the room aboard and the sale price, each by the golden runs;
    - the docked screen's Patch button;
    - the offer facts' hired slot, missing slot and fee;
    - the fight cache's key without `breaks`, without armour, and with the party alone;
    - the importer's three tunings;
    - `maps.name`, gear by tag and shuffled traits;
    - the size budget, and the editor's service worker answering `/games/`, through the built page.
  - The two survivors:
    - The Patch button removed survived because the refit screen listed every action (see above).
    - The fee read in credits, whatever the resource asked, survived because the test asked only for credits. A test now asks for a resource the fee doesn't name.

    Both were caught when planted again.
  - 5 more in `takes`, all caught: the check for an unknown action, the check for a list that isn't `offered`, the module pattern's prefix, and through the built page, the renderer's filter and the refit screen's `takes`.
  - Not checked: the fight cache dropping its oldest table past 64. Without that, memory would grow but no result would change.
- **Your check:** on your phone held sideways, open [`engine.rcjlabs.com/games/space-wrecked/`](https://engine.rcjlabs.com/games/space-wrecked/), start a game and play through the hangar to Launch and a few jumps. Then add it to your home screen, open it from there in flight mode, and Continue. Tell me what felt wrong, and M4.12 and Phase 4 are done.

## Phase 5: 3D world building

**Goal:** Creation Kit-style world building in the browser.

- **M5.1 Viewport.**
  - three.js, WebGPU first with WebGL2 fallback.
  - Editor camera: orbit and fly; on touch, one finger orbits and two pan or pinch.
  - Gizmos with touch handles, snapping, multi-select, outliner, prefabs.
  - **Gate** (D63, your call): an e2e builds a space in the editor, by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch on phones. It places prefabs, moves, turns and scales them by gizmo with snapping, groups two into a new prefab, renames it in the outliner, and finds it all after a reload ([specs/viewport.md](docs/specs/viewport.md)).
  - **Where it stands:** done; its gate passes by machine (#48, 2026-10-24).
    - **Files:** `spaces/<id>.json` and `prefabs/<id>.json`, a name and objects one to a line, each a shape or a placed prefab with its place, turn (degrees, YXZ) and scale, defaults left out. The compiler reads and checks them for `eb validate` (E058 to E061, W141); the editor's Problems panel shows the same problems, each opening its file with its object chosen. Nothing packs them yet: M5.8 draws spaces in a game.
    - **The Scene panel** lists the spaces and prefabs and opens one in a 3D view: three's WebGPURenderer, drawn only when the camera, the file, the choice or the size changes. three loads with the first file opened, never with the editor's start. The camera orbits, pans, zooms, frames and flies (right button and W, A, S, D, Q, E), and orbits by one finger and pans and pinches by two.
    - **The gizmo** moves along an axis or a plane, turns about a ring and scales along an object's own axis or all three, snapped to 0.5 m, 15° and 0.1 (Ctrl or ⌘ the other way), several objects about their centre. Its handles are hit on screen, 22 px either side of each line or point. Each drag is one step of undo, written as the pointer lifts.
    - **The toolbar** has Move, Turn, Scale, Snap, Add (a shape, or a prefab that doesn't loop), Select more, Group into prefab, Delete and Frame. The Outliner chooses and renames. The inspector edits one object's name, place, turn, scale, shape and colour, or opens its prefab; for several it counts them.
    - **The gate:** `spaces.spec.ts` on desktop by mouse and keys, `spacesPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390: a prefab with a box, three placements of it, one moved, turned and scaled by the gizmo onto the steps (each one step of undo; Ctrl lands off them), two picked in the view and grouped into a new prefab, renamed in the outliner, the camera flown, zoomed and pinched, nothing drawn while still, and all of it after a reload; three not loaded before the first file opens; every screen in reach, axe clean.
    - **Found on the way:**
      - three's OrbitControls imports plain `three`, which would put three's WebGL renderer in the chunk beside the WebGPU one. The camera is the editor's own plain functions.
      - Held sideways, the view was taller than its zone, and a gizmo handle sat under the tab bar, where a finger landed on the tabs. The view is held to what a short screen has room for, and the e2e's hook offers only spots the canvas shows.
      - Add put every new object on the last, so a second was hidden in the first. It steps 1.5 m along x to a free spot.
      - A ring's angle was added up move by move, so a drag could pass 180°. A planted bug showed that changed nothing: 225° and −135° are one turn. It is the angle from the grip now.
      - The check that three doesn't load with the panel's list looked for a chunk's name. A planted static import folded three into the list's own chunk, under another name, and it passed. It reads `window.__THREE__`, which three's core sets as it runs.
    - **Logged for later:** a placed prefab edited in place; a space's own snap step; fly by touch before M5.8's stick; the toolbar beside the view on a phone held sideways; a grid that follows the space; the outliner reordered by drag; duplicate, copy and paste; several objects edited in the form at once; moves along an object's own axes.
    - **One e2e failure not this milestone's:** a designer test at 390×844 timed out on a field once, while the unit tests and the whole e2e suite ran at once; it passed alone.
    - **Untested for feel:** everything. No real phone has used the view, the WebGPU path has run nowhere (CI has no adapter), and the gizmo's sizes, the drag slop and the camera's speeds are first guesses.
    - **64 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 55 caught the first time.
      - Of 14 in the files and checks, 13 were caught; the survivor, the files read in any order, passed because the test's files were listed in order already. The test now lists them out of order.
      - Of 37 in the plain functions, the catalog, the Problems panel and the layout, 31 were caught. Five survived weak tests: a turn's order (the only test turned about the axis the object was already turned on), the 44 px reach (the test read the constant it guarded), a ring's closing segment (hidden by the reach), the grip farthest from other handles, and a hand-laid file kept byte for byte (the test's file was in the writer's layout already). Each test now fails without its code. The sixth showed the summed ring angle above was dead code.
      - Of 13 through the built editor, 11 were caught: drawing every frame, a step per move, Shift, Ctrl, flying, Add's choice, the Outliner as a tab, Select more, the pinch, the sheet's outliner and `touch-action`. The survivors, three loaded with the list and the view too tall sideways, each got the check above.
      - Every survivor was caught when planted again.
- **M5.2 Scenes.**
  - Components on a space's objects, each kind with a schema and a form: the engine's, and a project's own.
  - Placed records, whose fields a placement overrides; prefab placements that override the objects inside them.
  - **Gate** (D64, your call): an e2e by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways adds components and edits them with their forms, places a record and overrides one of its fields, overrides a field inside a placed prefab and reverts it, and finds it all after a reload. A fixture project holds every new check to `eb validate` ([specs/components.md](docs/specs/components.md)).
  - **Where it stands:** done; its gate passes by machine (2026-10-24).
    - **Files:** an object is a shape, a placed prefab or a placed record (`record`, as `type:id`). It can carry `components`, by kind, and `overrides`: a placed record's fields changed here, or a placed prefab's objects changed here by id, nested for prefabs inside prefabs. The engine's kinds are light, collider, trigger zone, spawn point and sound, each a type file; a project's own are `components/<kind>.json`. The compiler checks them as E058 and E062 to E066, W142 and W143; the editor's Problems panel shows the same problems.
    - **The editor:** Component kinds in the Scene panel's list, each made and changed in the type designer, its changes reaching every component of it. An object's form has its components' forms, Remove and Add a component. Add's Record… places any record. A placed record's form shows its fields as they stand here; a placed prefab's lists the objects inside it, each opening a form whose every change is an override. Overridden fields say Changed here, with Revert. A record's form lists In spaces. Renaming a record, a type or a field rewrites the placements and overrides that name it.
    - **The view:** a placed record is an octahedron with its name over it; each component a mark of its kind, a light a point light too; overrides drawn as the merge makes them.
    - **The gate:** `components.spec.ts` on desktop and `componentsPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 build the lodge from an empty project: a keeper type and Greta; Hold, a kind of the project's own, in the type designer; a lamp prefab with a light and a hold; a yard placing both; Greta's greeting changed where she stands, her record untouched; inside the lamp's placement, the box's light turned up and its colour changed, then the colour reverted (and undone and redone on desktop); her label over her marker; her record's In spaces opening the yard at her; and all of it after a reload, every screen in reach and axe clean. `tools/cli/test/spaces.test.ts` validates the lodge fixture clean and finds each check in a copy broken once for each.
    - **Found on the way:**
      - spaces.ts and spaceParts.ts imported each other, so the override's schema was built before its fields existed and every override was "Unknown field". The object's shape is spaceShape.ts now.
      - A kind an override added to an object was checked only for the fields it set, so a trigger zone added without effects passed. A kind the object lacks is checked whole.
      - The editor's start carried the checks that read records, 25.9 kB, because the catalog called a function that also called them. They are apart now (Readings).
      - The view's test hook found no spot on a small chosen marker, since the gizmo covers it. It can now give a spot under the handles, for the label check.
      - architecture.md put ink scenes in `dialogue/`; they have been `scenes/<id>.ink` since M3.11.
    - **Logged for later:** an override that takes a component away; picking and moving an object inside a placement in the view; a placed record's override reaching inside a field; a project's own kinds drawn with a mark; the flag lint counting a trigger zone's reads and sets (M5.8, when triggers run: now a flag only a trigger names is W110).
    - **One e2e failure not this milestone's:** in the whole suite, `input.spec.ts`'s keyboard run found a toast's text below the contrast line once, as axe read it mid-fade; it passed alone. Logged: the axe check waiting for toasts to settle.
    - **Untested for feel:** everything. No real phone has used the forms inside the sheet or the marker's label, and the marks' sizes and colours are first guesses.
    - **33 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 32 caught the first time.
      - Of 24 in the checks, the overrides' merge and the editor's edits, 23 were caught. The survivor, a colour override let onto an object inside a prefab that isn't a shape, passed because no test gave one a colour. A test now does.
      - Of 9 through the built editor, all were caught: a label never made, labels never placed over their markers, Changed here hidden, Revert of the colour aimed at the wrong field, In spaces gone, Add a component adding the wrong kind, a placed record's field written outside its overrides, the users of each record dropped, and an inner object's change written to the placement itself.
      - The survivor was caught when planted again.
- **M5.3 Terrain.**
  - Heightmap sculpting and splat painting with touch brushes, chunked LOD, water (D65).
  - **Gate** (D65, your call): an e2e by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways makes a terrain, sculpts with each brush, paints each layer, adds water, shows finer chunks near the camera than far off with no crack at a seam, and finds it all after a reload. A fixture project holds the new checks to `eb validate`. Frame times are readings in software rendering only.
  - **Where it stands:** done; its gate passes by machine (2026-10-24).
    - **Files:**
      - A space's `terrain` sits between its name and its objects: size 65, 129, 257 or 513, spacing, low, high, and one to four layers of a name and a colour.
      - Beside the space, `spaces/<id>.heights.png` holds the heights as 16-bit grey, and `spaces/<id>.paint.png` the layers' weights, one channel each.
      - Water is the engine's sixth component kind, a colour and a clarity, on a plane.
      - The compiler checks them as E058, E067, W144, W145 and W141, reading only the PNGs' headers. The Problems panel shows the same problems and opens the space for a PNG's.
    - **The editor:**
      - In a space, the toolbar's Terrain row holds a size and Add terrain, and then the brushes, the layer, Radius, Strength and Terrain settings.
      - A stroke dabs every half spacing along the ground. It is one step of undo, written as the pointer lifts; a second finger puts it back.
      - With nothing chosen, the inspector holds the terrain's form. Low and high are written as their field is left, so typing never clamps.
      - Add stands a new object on the ground.
    - **The view:**
      - The chunks are drawn at four levels by distance, each built once at its level as a terrain opens. After that a chunk is built again only where a stroke, an undo or a level changes.
      - Each edge lies on a coarser neighbour's line. Normals come from the full heightmap.
      - Water is see-through, and the brush's ring shows on the ground.
    - **The gate:**
      - `terrain.spec.ts` on desktop and `terrainPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 start from an empty project and sculpt a ridge, a trench, a smoothing and a flattening near the origin, each read back through the view's hook and each a step of undo.
      - On desktop an undo of Flatten is drawn back, then redone.
      - They paint each of the four layers at a spot of its own, then use the form: on desktop High typed a key at a time and a colour changed, by touch a layer renamed.
      - They add water on the ground, drawn see-through, then zoom out by keys or a pinch until the sculpted seams join two levels. The levels must rise with distance, with no gap at a seam, read from the meshes' buffers, and the drawn vertices must lie on the grid.
      - After a reload, the file, both PNGs' bytes and the readings are as they were, every screen in reach and axe clean.
      - `tools/cli/test/terrain.test.ts` validates the fell fixture clean and finds each check in a copy broken once for each.
    - **Found on the way:**
      - The spec said a height between vertices was bilinear, but the mesh splits each square in two, so a brush would land off the drawn ground. Heights are read on the triangles drawn, as the spike's were.
      - WebGPURenderer draws no `LineLoop`: the ring is a line closed on its first point.
      - A frame asked for just before the view closed drew over freed meshes. Seen on phones, where switching panels closes the view; a closed view now draws nothing.
      - Smooth copied the whole heightmap each dab, 1 MB at 513. It copies the brush's area and a vertex around it.
      - Opening a 513 terrain built every chunk at full detail, then again at its level. Building each once at its level takes 18 to 29 ms against 138 to 168 ms, in Node (Readings).
      - Add put objects at 0 m, under any raised ground.
      - W145 read "Water sits on a plane" for water that doesn't sit on one; it says "Water off a plane" now.
      - CI's whole suite failed once on M5.1's sideways spec. A handle was found just after Frame was pressed but before the view framed, as Frame ran after a paint; on a loaded runner the drag missed the handle. Frame now runs as the toolbar's press lands, as the mode and the pick have since M5.1. Reproduced first: Frame planted 150 ms late fails that spec.
    - **Logged for later:** the brush's ring before a finger lands ([specs/terrain.md](docs/specs/terrain.md#open-questions), question 4); a height typed for Flatten (question 3); layers as textures with M5.5 (question 1); terrains beyond 513 with M5.9 (question 2); a pinch in brush mode has no check of its own that the stroke it cut short went back.
    - **Untested for feel:** everything. No real finger has stroked a terrain. The brushes' rates, the radius's range and the ring's colour are first guesses.
    - **54 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 48 caught the first time.
      - Of 43 in the grid, the brushes, the stroke, the chunks, the ray, the edits, the PNG code and the checks, 37 were caught. Six survived, each for a missing case, and each case is now a test:
        - a heights file of the right size one way only, refused by the grid and by E067 (two plants);
        - paint of the right depth in the wrong colours;
        - a part misspelt, `fell.height.png`;
        - the mesh split on the other diagonal, which the crack test's nearly planar terrain hid;
        - a ray passing under the terrain.
      - Of 11 through the built editor, all were caught:
        - the terrain's files never read;
        - an undo not drawn;
        - neighbours not built again as a level changed;
        - one level for all;
        - a stroke never written;
        - the layer ignored;
        - water drawn solid;
        - the form not shown;
        - High written on each key;
        - a touch stroke lost;
        - Add at 0 m.
      - Each survivor was caught when planted again.
- **M5.4 Environments.**
  - Sky, sun and time of day, fog, weather.
  - Presets: desert/wasteland, coast/fjord, forest, snow, cave/dungeon, sci-fi interior, town.
  - **As decided** (D66, your calls): all of it now, rain and snow included. An environment is a record of the engine's `environment` type that a space names, the presets records Start from writes. The sky is physical (three's Preetham sky) outdoors, a gradient for stylised looks, or a flat colour for caves and interiors. The hour is set by the author and previewed in the view; the game's clock drives it from M5.8 ([specs/environments.md](docs/specs/environments.md)).
  - **Gate** (D66, your call): an e2e by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways makes an environment from each preset, sets the hour, fog and weather, and reads the view back through its hook and the canvas's pixels, then finds it all after a reload. A fixture holds the new checks to `eb validate`. Frame times are readings in software rendering only.
  - **Done** (2026-10-24; [specs/environments.md](docs/specs/environments.md)):
    - **The record and its checks:**
      - `environment` is the engine's seventh record type, beside flag, sheet, sound, ambience and music: hour, sky, haze, three sky colours, sunlight and sky light with their strengths, fog, and weather with its amount. Every field may be left out, for noon under a clear physical sky.
      - A space names one after its name. E068 is a fog whose near isn't below its far; E069 a space naming an environment there isn't; E058 a prefab naming one at all. The space is a use of the record: its form lists it under In spaces, and renaming it rewrites the space.
      - W142 and W145 moved to `spaceLints.ts`, so `spaceParts.ts` stays under 300 lines.
    - **The editor:**
      - Records lists Environment after the audio types, with Start from and the seven presets above its form.
      - With nothing chosen in a space, the inspector picks its environment or none, makes a new one from a preset in one step of undo (`dale-coast`, "Dale, coast"), opens it, and sets its hour, fog and weather, which are the record's own fields.
    - **The view:**
      - The sun and the sky's light are plain arithmetic of the hour (`look.ts`), with tests. The sky is three's SkyMesh with its clouds off and its light through ACES inside its own material, a gradient sphere with the sun's disc, or the background in one colour, always behind everything.
      - Fog is three's. Rain is up to 3,000 lines and snow up to 2,000 flakes from one mesh, each drop's place a function of its number and the moment, wrapped through a 60 m box around where the camera looks; the view draws every frame while they fall, and once with reduced motion asked for.
      - The hook's `environment()` reads all of it back from three, not from the record.
    - **The gate:**
      - `environments.spec.ts` on desktop and `environmentsPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 start from an empty project and make a space, give it a new environment from each preset in turn, and see each in the view: the hook's sky, fog and weather as the preset has them, and the colour of a band near the top of the canvas with the camera level (a colour sky's within 6, a gradient's nearer its zenith than its horizon, a physical day's blue over red).
      - They pick the coast again and move its hour from noon to dawn: the sun lower, dimmer and warmer, the sky darker above by more than 10 in luma. They turn its fog off, on and nearer, and set rain, snow and their amounts; on desktop the snow draws frames by itself, and stands still, drawn once, with reduced motion; Start from Desert over it in its own form keeps its name, and is undone.
      - After a reload the space's file, the record and the hook's whole reading are as they were, every screen in reach and axe clean.
      - `tools/cli/test/environment.test.ts` validates the dale fixture clean and finds each check in a copy broken once for each.
    - **Changed on the way:**
      - Town's hour is 17.5, not the spec's first 18.5, which put the sun 7.8° below the horizon and gave a preset with no sunlight at all.
      - The spec said the sky light falls "to a fifth at night"; it now says how: whole from 17.5° up, a fifth from 11.5° below.
      - An environment that isn't of its type isn't drawn: the view keeps the editor's light and says so.

      - a physical sky below the horizon is as pale as at it, which a terrain hides but an empty space shows;

If phones find the sky's shader or the chunk dear, M5.7 can work Preetham's sky out per vertex on the gradient's sphere instead, with neither.

- **M5.5 Assets.**
  - glTF/GLB import with Draco and Meshopt, KTX2 textures, material presets, LOD, generated colliders.
  - A CC0 starter kit with provenance and licence checks.
  - **As decided** (D67, your calls): the editor reads Draco and Meshopt geometry and KTX2 textures with three's own decoders, offline, and packs a model's geometry with Meshopt as it is imported; KTX2 textures come in ready-made. A model and a material are records of the engine's `model` and `material` types: a model names its file and holds its scale, which material each of the file's materials is drawn with, its LOD levels (ratios made by meshoptimizer's simplifier as it is drawn) and its collider (a box, sphere, capsule or convex hull, fitted and kept as numbers); a material is a colour, roughness, metalness, glow and opacity, with presets. A space places a model record and draws it, and a record whose type names a model is drawn as that model. The starter kit is about 20 low-poly pieces a script here writes, dedicated by RCJLabs to CC0 ([specs/models.md](docs/specs/models.md)).
  - **Gate** (D67, your call): an e2e by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways imports a Draco GLB, a Meshopt GLB and one with KTX2 textures, sets a material preset, makes LOD and a collider, places the model in a space, reads it back through the view's hook (triangles drawn near and far, the collider's shape), adds the starter kit, and finds it all after a reload. A fixture holds the new checks to `eb validate`. Decode times and sizes are readings only.
  - **Done** (2026-10-24; [specs/models.md](docs/specs/models.md)):
    - **The records and their checks:**
      - `material` and `model` are the engine's eighth and ninth record types. A material is a colour, roughness, metalness, glow and opacity, every field optional, with eight presets. A model names its `.glb` or `.gltf` under `assets/` and holds its scale, which material record each of the file's materials is drawn with, up to three LOD levels, and its collider: a box, sphere, capsule or hull of 4 to 64 points.
      - E070 is a file the engine can't draw (not glTF 2.0, an extension it needs that the engine doesn't read, a buffer or image not beside it), read from the glTF's JSON alone; E071 LOD levels that don't each keep fewer triangles from farther away; E072 a collider without its shape's numbers, or with others; W146 a slot the file has no material of. `eb validate` and the Problems panel give each at its field.
      - The glTF header reader moved from the editor to the compiler (`gltf.ts`), so both read a file the same way.
    - **Import:**
      - `@eb/models` packs each model in a worker: gltf-transform reads it, Draco through three's own decoder; meshoptimizer reorders it for the vertex cache; positions are quantised to 16 bits inside each mesh's bounds and texture coordinates to 16 bits; every buffer view is Meshopt-compressed, normals at 8 bits. It writes one GLB at `assets/models/<name>.glb`, the same bytes for the same file; a `.gltf` and the files it names become one GLB.
      - With Make a model ticked, each gets a record with a box collider, and LOD levels at a half, a quarter and a tenth if it has over 1,000 triangles. The import is one step of undo; each packing's sizes and time go to the console.
    - **The view:**
      - A placed model record, and a placed record of a type whose first top-level field naming `model` alone is set, draw as the model, through prefabs and their overrides. Add → Model… places one.
      - GLTFLoader and its Draco, Meshopt and KTX2 decoders load with the first model, in a chunk of their own. Each file is decoded once for its hash and cloned for each placement, which draws M5.2's marker until its file is ready.
      - Slots draw with their material records. LOD levels are cut by meshoptimizer's simplifier as a model is first drawn and picked by three's `LOD`. The collider is a wire while the object is chosen.
      - The hook's `models()` reads it all back from three: the level drawn, its triangles, the collider, each slot's colour, the textures and the wire.
    - **The forms:** above a model's form, the file's facts, a material picker for each of its materials, and Fit collider's Box, Sphere, Capsule and Hull, fitted in the worker; above a material's, Start from and the presets.
    - **The starter kit:** `tools/starter-kit/` writes twenty pieces, floor to chest, as a project folder, `starter-kit/`, dedicated by RCJLabs to CC0: GLBs packed as import packs them, model records with the kit's six materials and fitted colliders, and its log. `eb validate` finds it clean, and the script's output matches the committed files byte for byte. Add starter kit in the Assets panel copies it in, one step of undo, skipping files the project already has, from a chunk loaded only when it is pressed.
    - **The gate:**
      - `models.spec.ts` on desktop and `modelsPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 start from an empty project and import, in one pick, a Draco GLB (hills, 2,048 triangles), a Meshopt GLB (a crate) and a GLB whose texture is a KTX2 (a sign), each packed with its record.
      - They make Bark from the Wood preset and draw the crate's wood with it, give the crate a LOD level and fit it a sphere, then place all three in a space. The hook reads the hills' box and four levels, the crate's sphere in Bark's colour, and the sign's texture. The hills draw all 2,048 triangles near and at most 246, a tenth within a fifth, past 60 m. The collider's wire is drawn on the chosen model and no other.
      - They add the starter kit and place its pine, drawn in the kit's wood and leaves. After a reload every model is drawn with the collider and materials it had. Every screen in reach is checked for overflow, 44 px targets and axe.
      - `tools/cli/test/models.test.ts` validates the workshop fixture clean and finds E070, E071, E072 and W146 in a copy broken once for each.
      - Each of the four e2e tests ran three times, 12 of 12 passing, and once more with M5.1's and M5.4's specs after the wire check and `lastShown.ts` went in, 12 of 12 again.
    - **Changed on the way:**
      - Meshopt packs bigger than Draco until both are gzipped (D67's As built; Readings).
      - Packing reorders for the vertex cache, as gltfpack does.
      - The kit's log is `assets/assets.json`, not the spec's first `provenance.json`, so the kit is a project folder `eb validate` can hold.
      - three's DRACOLoader names its own decoders, so the build shipped and precached an asm.js fallback and a second wasm, 948 KiB the editor never fetches. A build plugin blanks those names and fails the build if three names others.
      - A race from M5.1: what the view was shown before it existed never reached it, so a space whose props all arrived while three's view was being made stayed blank. Found while drawing models; `spaces/lastShown.ts` now keeps a key only once there is a view.
    - **Logged for later:**
      - questions 1 to 8 in [specs/models.md](docs/specs/models.md#open-questions): size budgets, LOD baked into exports, textured materials, thumbnails, animation, a Basis-compressed KTX2 fixture, keeping Draco as it came, and New material from in the slot pickers;
      - what GLTFLoader uses of three's core, 26 kB, now loads with every space, since the core is one module in the view's chunk (Readings). M5.7 can weigh a chunk of its own for it against one more request.
    - **Untested for feel:** everything. No phone has decoded a model, cut a LOD level or drawn the kit. The kit's shapes and colours, the default LOD levels and their distances are first guesses.
    - **26 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 25 caught.
      - Of 15 in the checks, the packer, the import, the placements, the form and the kit, all were caught: E071's order, E072's needs, W146's slot and E070's version each ignored; quantising off, reordering off, Draco kept; LOD at exactly 1,000 triangles; a `.gltf`'s files not looked for beside it; the import's log line dropped; a kit file overwritten; an override of the field naming the model ignored; a field naming models among others taken as the model field; a slot set to none kept; a kit piece changed.
      - Of 8 through the built editor, 7 were caught: slots drawn as the file has them, LOD levels cut whole, KTX2 off, no wire, Draco not read in the worker, Start from writing nothing, and Fit collider fitting a box whatever was pressed.
      - The view's reset survived `models.spec.ts` and every space spec: the race it guards never happened in them. The rule moved to `lastShown.ts`, whose two plants, a key kept with no view and every key shown, its unit test catches.
      - The build plugin's count, planted wrong, fails the build.
- **M5.6 Foliage** (D65: after Assets, so there are models to scatter).
  - Scatter and foliage painted with touch brushes, drawn instanced.
  - **As decided** (D68, your calls): a terrain's foliage is density, one 8-bit channel per type of `spaces/<id>.foliage.png`, painted as its layers are; plants are scattered from it by a fixed seed whenever the space is drawn, so painting more adds plants and moves none. A foliage type is a record of the engine's `foliage` type: the model, plants per 100 m², a size range, a random turn, lean with the ground, slope and height limits and how far it is drawn, with presets for trees, bushes, grass and rocks; a terrain lists up to four. Plant and Clear brushes, and Fill from a paint layer, everywhere or nowhere. Drawn instanced in the terrain's chunks at the model's LOD level for their distance. No wind yet ([specs/foliage.md](docs/specs/foliage.md)).
  - **Gate** (D68, your call): an e2e by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways makes a foliage type from a starter-kit model, plants, clears and fills it, reads the counts near and far through the view's hook, and finds it all after a reload. A fixture holds the new checks to `eb validate`. Frame times are readings only.
  - **Done** (2026-10-24; [specs/foliage.md](docs/specs/foliage.md)):
    - **The record and its checks:**
      - `foliage` is the engine's tenth record type: the model it scatters, plants per 100 m², a size range, a random turn, lean with the ground, the steepest, lowest and highest ground it grows on, and how far it is drawn, every field but the model left out for a default. Start from above its form writes Trees, Bushes, Grass or Rocks, keeping its name and model.
      - A terrain's settings list up to four, and `spaces/<id>.foliage.png` holds their density, a channel each. E058 is more than four or one twice; E067 the density file missing or not an 8-bit RGBA of the terrain's size; E073 a foliage record there isn't; E074 a size or height range backwards; W144 a density file with no foliage to read it. `eb validate` and the Problems panel give each at its file and field; a record's form lists the spaces it grows in, and renaming it rewrites their lists.
    - **The scatter** (`foliage/scatter.ts`): cells `10 / √density` metres a side, five draws from a hash of the record's id and the cell, a plant standing where the first is below the density there, on ground neither too steep nor too high or low, kept by the chunk it stands in. Painting more only adds plants; nothing standing moves. At most 200,000 of a type in a space.
    - **The tools:**
      - The terrain's row gains Plant and Clear once the terrain lists a foliage type, with the type they paint and Fill: Everywhere, Nowhere, or Where a paint layer is. Each stroke and each Fill is one step of undo, "Plant Glade, trees".
      - Under the terrain's form in the inspector, Foliage lists its types with Open and Remove, adds one of the project's records, or makes one new from a preset, named for the space ("Glade, trees") with the kit's model when the project has it; each one step of undo with the list and the density file.
    - **The view:** each type scattered over the terrain and drawn as instanced meshes a chunk, one for each mesh of its model, at the model's level for the chunk's distance and not at all past the type's reach. A stroke scatters again only the chunks its dabs reach, as they land; a change to the list, a record or the terrain, on the next frame; a change to how the model is drawn, its plants again. The model is read and decoded as a placed one is. The hook's `foliage()` and `plantsNear()` read it back.
    - **The starter kit** gains a tuft of grass for the Grass preset: 21 pieces.
    - **The gate:**
      - `foliage.spec.ts` on desktop and `foliagePhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 start from an empty project: they add the starter kit, give a glade a 129-vertex terrain, paint Rock at a spot of its own, and make Trees and Grass new from the terrain's form, the kit's pine and grass.
      - They plant trees along a 32 m row with a 10 m brush (15 on desktop), clear across its middle (fewer, more than none), undo the clearing (every one back), and fill the grass Where Grass is: grass on open ground, none at the Rock.
      - They give the pine a LOD level in its form, half its triangles from 40 m, after its trees are drawn: every tree chunk then draws the level its distance gives, some whole. Zoomed out until the camera is 150 m off, no grass is drawn, though all of it is still scattered (24,552 plants), and every tree is drawn at the pine's second level.

    - **Changed on the way:**

      - The view scatters on the next frame rather than at once, so a terrain and its list arriving in one change scatter once.

      - The viewport's hook moved to `spaces/viewportHook.ts`, so `viewport.ts` stays near its size.

The scatter reading the first channel for every type survived until a test painted two channels differently.

A view that never drew a type's model again survived, since the models were decoded before the scatter ran; the gate's LOD step now catches it.
- **M5.7 Performance.**
  - Quality tiers (auto-detected, overridable), a perf HUD, and budgets enforced in CI.
  - **As decided** (D69, your calls): Low, Medium and High are built now and gated by machine; each tier's frame rate on a phone is set from your M1.10 reading when you take it, and D8 stays PROPOSED until then. A tier starts from the device's hints (the GPU's name, memory, cores, screen and pointer) and steps down one when frames drawn back to back run long for a few seconds, never up by itself; Auto, Low, Medium or High picked by hand always wins, kept per device. A tier changes the pixel ratio, antialiasing, the sun's shadows, the physical sky or a gradient, and how far and how thick foliage and weather are drawn, a lower tier drawing a subset of the same plants and drops; never what the sim sees. A perf overlay in the Scene panel shows frames, draw calls, triangles and the tier with why. CI fails a build over its size budgets (the editor's start, the 3D view's chunks, the precache, the exports) or its count budgets (draw calls and triangles for fixture scenes at each tier); frame times stay readings ([specs/performance.md](docs/specs/performance.md)).
  - **Gate** (D69, your call): an e2e by mouse and keys on desktop and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways opens the grove's glade, finds Auto's tier and its reason, picks each tier and reads its settings, its foliage and its counts back from three through the hook and the overlay, within budget, and finds the tier picked after a reload; `pnpm budgets` fails a build over a size budget. Frame times on a phone are readings, and the targets wait for your reading.
  - **Done** (2026-10-24; [specs/performance.md](docs/specs/performance.md)):
    - **The tiers** (`perf/tiers.ts`): Low draws at a pixel ratio of 1, no antialiasing, no shadows, a physical sky as its gradient, foliage to half its reach and half its plants, and a quarter of the drops; Medium at up to 1.5, a 1,024 shadow map, three quarters of the foliage and half the drops; High at up to 2, 4× MSAA, a 2,048 map that foliage casts into too, and everything. A lower tier keeps the plants whose first draw is lowest and the first of the same seeded drops, so it draws a subset and moves nothing. The sun's shadow camera is fitted round the terrain and the objects as either changes or the sun moves.
    - **Choosing:** the Scene toolbar's Quality opens a row with the list (Auto, Low, Medium, High) and Stats, both kept per device. Auto starts from the device's hints (`perf/hints.ts`): a software renderer is Low; a coarse pointer Medium, or Low for a GPU the table names; a fine pointer High with 8 cores and 8 GB where the browser says, else Medium. While frames are drawn back to back, past a view's first 2 seconds, a 95th percentile over 50 ms across 3 seconds steps Auto down one; it never steps up, and a tier picked by hand never steps. A new tier makes a new view on a new canvas, the camera and the objects chosen kept.
    - **Stats:** over the view's corner, the tier and why ("Auto: Low (a software renderer)", "Auto: Medium, stepped down from High", "Chosen: High"), the backend and pixel ratio, frames per second with the median and 95th-percentile frame, and the last frame's draw calls and triangles. The hook's `perf()` reads the same with each setting as three holds it.
    - **Budgets:** `budgets.json` holds each budget with its reading, date and what it measures; `pnpm budgets` (`tools/budgets`) measures the editor's start, what the 3D view and its models add, the precache and Space-Wrecked's export after the build, in `pnpm check` and in CI, and fails over any. `spaceWrecked.spec.ts` reads its budget from the same file. The count budgets, the glade's draw calls and triangles at each tier, are held by the gate.
    - **The gate:**
      - `perf.spec.ts` imports the grove, its glade now under a shower, adds a box and shows Stats: Auto is Low, "a software renderer", in CI's software WebGL. Orbited off its framing, it picks Low, Medium and High in turn and reads each back from three: the pixel ratio, antialiasing, the shadow map, whether the box and foliage cast and the ground takes shadows, the shadow camera's reach over the glade, the sky drawn and the drops falling; the camera as it was; each lower tier's plants a subset of the higher's, no circle on a 2 m grid holding more; and the draw calls and triangles within budget. After a reload, High and Stats are as they were.
      - The same spec gives the page a fast computer's hints (16 cores, 8 GB, a named GPU, no WebGPU): the glade opens at High, and the software renderer's long frames step Auto down, the overlay saying from what. Picked by hand, High holds through 7 seconds of the same frames.
      - `perfPhone.spec.ts` does it by touch at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 at three device pixels a point, where each tier's pixel ratio is held to its most (1, 1.5, 2). Every screen in reach is checked for overflow, 44 px targets and axe.
      - Unit tests hold the tiers, the hints table, the frame times, the step down with its warm-up, the editor's tier state, the scatter's share and the budgets' reader on a made-up build and against budgets.json's margins. `pnpm budgets` was seen failing with scene-view's budget set to 1,000 bytes.
    - **Changed on the way:**
      - **Models' LOD:** with shadows on, three updated each model's LOD for the sun's camera too, 50 m off, so the far level was read back and cast the shadow; the models specs at High found the hills never at their nearest. The view now picks each level for its own camera before a frame, where each stands now.
      - **Quality's own row:** in the toolbar, the list and Stats cost a phone at 360 px a fifth row of view, and the components spec's placed record sank under the inspector's sheet.
      - **The warm-up:** a new view's first frames, while three compiles its shaders, ran 350 to 480 ms at any tier, enough to step a tier down by themselves.
      - **The specs before M5.7 draw at High,** set in `playwright.config.ts`'s storage, since they hold the view to every plant, drop and the physical sky as authored.
      - **`viewport.ts` grows from 417 lines to 486:** the tier, the frame clock and the models' LODs. The shadow fit moved to `shadows.ts` and the reading to `perfSummary.ts` to hold it there.
    - **Logged for later:** questions 1 to 8 in [specs/performance.md](docs/specs/performance.md#open-questions): the targets, the hints table, a game's Quality, post-processing, baked foliage and LOD, the view's chunk, an import's budget, and Low's sphere.
    - **Untested for feel and on a phone:** everything. No phone has drawn a tier, stepped down or shown Stats; each tier's settings and the 50 ms line are first guesses until your reading.
    - **45 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 44 caught.
      - Of 21 in the tiers, the hints, the frame times, the step down, the editor's tier state, the scatter, the budgets' reader, budgets.json and the shadow fit, all were caught: the pixel ratio uncapped, Low stepping to itself; SwiftShader not software, memory unknown taken as none, the pointer ignored; frames drawn apart counted, the maximum for the 95th percentile, no fresh count after a step, the warm-up gone; the nearest rank floored, a median unrounded; the scatter's share ignored; the 3D chunk counting the start's chunks, the start without its CSS, over judged at twice the budget; a budget past its 5%; a pick not forgetting a step; and the fit ignoring where objects stand, the terrain's corners or the sun's distance. A step recorded while a tier was picked survived, unseen since a pick ignores it, until the test asked for none.
      - Of 24 through the built editor and `pnpm budgets`, 21 were caught: antialiasing, shadows, the tier, the camera, the drops, the sky, the plants' share, foliage's shadows, the pixel ratio (at 360 px, three device pixels a point), the WebGL hint, the step down, frames back to back, the overlay's reason, Stats and Quality kept, objects casting, the ground receiving, the shadow camera's reach, foliage casting at Medium and the canvas kept across tiers; and `pnpm budgets` passing over a budget.
      - Three survived it. A step while a tier is picked, the same plant the state test catches; a fit ignoring where objects stand, since the glade's terrain reaches farther than its box, the same plant the shadow test catches; and a view made before the hints are read, which three throws away before it draws a frame, so the gate's record of every tier drawn from the page's first frame finds only Low: what is lost is a renderer started for nothing.

  - Rapier physics; 1st- and 3rd-person controllers and camera rigs; a touch stick, keyboard and gamepad.

A pack of format 9 carries the project's spaces, prefabs, their trigger zones resolved, and the terrain PNGs and model GLBs they draw from; a screen's Space widget draws one, the game's HUD over it, in the Play panel, on the player page and in exports (a second runtime file, `walk.js`, for a game with spaces). Rapier's compat build runs on the page beside three: the terrain a heightfield, collider components and models' colliders solid, `moves` bodies pushed, the character a kinematic capsule. The sim stays pure: a trigger zone entered is a `world/trigger` action it checks and runs. A spawn point says first- or third-person; the third-person camera pulls in where something solid is in the way. Keys and mouse, a pad's two sticks, and by touch a stick where the left thumb lands, a drag on the right half to look and a jump button ([specs/movement.md](docs/specs/movement.md)).

It is never offered; the play session lets the stage's trigger through to the sim's checks, where every other action must be on offer.

When the Scene view was built anew while a batch of model files decoded, that batch built nothing when it ended, and the next batch found nothing new to decode. A batch that decodes a file now builds the view again; `viewModels.test.ts` holds it, seen failing without the fix. The pine went missing on CI once more after the fix, and no run here repeats it, loaded or not; the models specs now wait for the yard, the pine's record and its file to be stored before they reload, so a failure says whether the save or the view lost it.
      - **Strafes let go by the page:** on a loaded CI box the keys spec let go of D a metre late, walked past the boulder and the stones, and failed. A strafe now ends on the first frame the feet pass its line (`holdUntilPast`, its key-up sent in the page), and the run to the cairn faces the stones' middle.
    - **Logged for later:** questions 1 to 9 in [specs/movement.md](docs/specs/movement.md#open-questions): positions in saves (M5.9), bots that walk, swimming, ladders and doors, look sensitivity, Rapier's separate `.wasm`, plants that block, the decoders outside walk.js, and the Play panel sideways.
    - **Untested for feel and on a phone:** every speed, the jump, the slopes, the camera and the stick's size, until your phone walks the trail.
    - **43 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 42 caught.
      - Of 33 in the sim, the pack, the checks, the stage's plain functions, the hosts, the exports and the budgets, 32 were caught: a once zone firing twice, a zone's condition skipped, the world's own keys, its order, format 9 always, the world unchecked, a zone's path by dots, screens' flags ignored, the compile without them, the Space widget unchecked, the world not joined, the session refusing the walk's triggers, zones sent again, dropped zones kept, a zone hit only at its middle, the camera not pulled in, steeper slopes climbed, heights' rows swapped, the stick's dead zone gone, a jump not waiting, a zone's size unscaled, yaw flipped, the script check told no spaces, the index's flags stale, models sent by name, a .gltf's neighbours left out, any walk read from boot, exports without walk.js or with it uninlined, walk.js leak-scanned, its budget unmeasured, and the press's retry gone.
      - One survived: the pad's stick moving the screens' focus during a walk. Planted again, the pad spec caught it.
      - Of 10 through the built editor, 8 were caught by the specs: the pad's stick moving focus, pause ignored, zones unsent, the editor's walk.js path, held keys only (a tap lost), the jump button's tap lost, the stick backwards, and the single file's walk.js unread. Held keys only survived `keyboard.press`, which Playwright spreads over frames, until the tap became a keydown and keyup in one task. `pnpm budgets` caught walk.js left out of the precache, and the build fails on its own with Workbox's 10 MB limit put back.
- **M5.9 Worldspaces.**
  - Interiors and load doors; loading screens.
  - Persistent references, saved as changes against the authored content.
  - **As decided** (D71, your calls): a door is `enter-space`, an effect a zone, a use, a talk, an event or a quest stage can run; the player takes and opens things with Interact (E, the pad's X, a touch button), an `interact` component on the object; a save keeps the space, where the player stood and which way they faced, and what was taken, moving bodies going back to their authored places ([specs/worldspaces.md](docs/specs/worldspaces.md)).
  - **Gate:** Phase 5's walkable slice below, by keys, a pad and touch at 360, 390 and sideways.
  - **Done** (2026-10-25; [specs/worldspaces.md](docs/specs/worldspaces.md)):
    - **The sim:** the world module's slice is version 2: the space the player is in (the start in a new game), where they last stood and which way they faced, the spawn point they arrive at, and the objects taken, as "space/object". `world/use` runs an interact's effects (`bad-use`, `no-use`, `taken`, `spent`, `unmet`), raises `world/used` and keeps a take; `world/at` keeps where the player stands, to the centimetre and the tenth of a degree, and refuses a space they aren't in (`elsewhere`); `enter-space` moves them to a space, at a spawn point or its start, clearing where they stood, and raises `world/arrived`. None is ever offered. Facts: `world.in`, `world.taken`, `world.fired`. A version 1 save migrates to the start with nothing taken: `trail-world-v1.json`, saved by M5.8's build of the trail, loads and plays on.
    - **Packs:** format 10's world adds each space's uses, resolved through prefabs and overrides, each space's spawn points and the start; a world format 9 holds stays format 9, its hash kept. project.json's `start-space` names the start, or the first space by id does.
    - **Checks:** the `interact` component kind (label, when, effects, once, takes); E075, a start-space that names no space, from `readProjectFiles`, so the editor's catalog has it; E076, an `enter-space` naming a space or a spawn point there isn't, in records, zones and uses, by `eb validate` and the editor; and the kits' checks now hear what zones and uses hold, so a pickup's `start-quest` starts its quest (W125) and a zone's `set-stage` must name a stage the quest has (E043), at the space's line.
    - **The Space widget:** its `space` may be left out, and it draws the space the player is in, starting the stage again as `enter-space` moves them; New screen's Space template leaves it out. The host shows the next space's name and Loading… as a status over the box until the stage's first frame.
    - **The stage:** it starts where a load stood the player, or at the spawn point they arrive at; Interact (E, the pad's X, or the prompt tapped, a 64 px button left of Jump naming its key) sends `world/use` for the nearest use within 2 m of the body's middle and 60° of the way the player faces, after a `world/at`; it says where the player stands as the shell pauses, as the page hides, and every 5 s once they have moved 0.5 m; its sends go one at a time through an outbox that tries a dropped one again by a timer; what the sim says is taken is hidden, with everything inside it, and loses its solids. The hook adds how the walk began, the prompt, the uses and stands sent, and what is taken and hidden in three's scene.
    - **Headless runs:** `eb sim` and the editor's Run play with the pack's own world, so a game's own `enter-space` runs; the trail's going in and out now moves the player between its spaces.
    - **The gate:**
      - The glen (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/glen`), made only of data and a one-mode script: a glen with a rope to take, a bothy whose door is an interact and whose lamp is on a table, a shed whose door is a zone, and a gate's zone on the way that sets the supplies quest's last stage. It validates clean, builds as format 10, and `glen.test.ts` plays it through the session: each take, each door each way, the gate, and a save in the bothy that a load puts back where it stood.
      - `glen.spec.ts` walks it in the Play panel by keys and the mouse: the rope taken by E after a stand, hidden in three's scene, and in the bag on the HUD; the bothy's door opened, its loading screen seen, the bothy in first person at its door; the lamp taken, the quest moving on by itself; paused, quit and continued, back in the bothy where the player stood and facing the same way, the lamp still gone; out by its way out to the glen at its door, the rope still gone; the page hidden saying where the player stood; the gate's zone setting the quest's last stage; the shed's door zone into the shed in third person and out by its interact. By a stand-in pad: the prompt naming X, X taking the rope and opening the bothy's door.
      - `glenPhone.spec.ts` at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390: the prompt beside Jump, at its height, at least 44 px, naming no key; tapped to take the rope; the stick to the bothy's door and the prompt tapped; paused, quit and continued, back in the bothy where the player stood; every screen in reach.
    - **Changed on the way:**

The zones' triggers now go through the same outbox; `acts.test.ts` holds it, seen failing with the old wiring and with triggers let past the queue.

Each has a test now, and planted again was caught. A third, the spawn arrived at ignored, was planted against text Biome had reflowed; planted again, it was caught.

- **M5.10 Streaming and snapping** (split from M5.9, D71).
  - Chunked exteriors that stream around the player.
  - Snapping for modular kits in the Scene panel.
  - **As decided** (D72, your calls): one space, cut by the engine into 64 m cells by its top-level objects' footprints; the 3×3 cells around the player built whole (drawn, solid, zones and uses), kept until they leave the 5×5; an object farther but within the tier's view distance (Low 250 m, Medium 400 m, High 600 m) drawn as its models alone, at their coarsest level and instanced; fog at the view distance; the terrain always whole, up to 513 vertices. In the Scene panel, a moved box latches touching or flush onto another's within 12 px, a grid step of 0.25, 0.5, 1 or 2 m takes the other axes, and Drop stands the chosen on what is under them ([specs/streaming.md](docs/specs/streaming.md)).
  - **Gate:** the moor, 1 km across with the starter kit's pieces spread over it, walked headless across through Rapier and in the Play panel by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, its rings built and taken down as the player crosses cells, its far models instanced, a far zone heard once its cell is built and a far chest taken; a hut snapped together from the starter kit by mouse and by touch, its pieces' places read back to the millimetre, a floor dropped onto its walls. Frame times as cells build are readings; feel waits for your phone.
  - **Done** (2026-10-25; [specs/streaming.md](docs/specs/streaming.md)):
    - **Cells:** each top-level object of a space has a footprint, its place on x and z and how far anything of it reaches: a shape by half its scaled box's diagonal, a placed model by its collider times its scales (half a metre without one), a prefab by the farthest of what is inside it, through overrides. The footprints fill an index of 64 m cells from the origin.
    - **The rings:** as the player crosses into another cell, every object with a cell in the 3×3 around them is near, and one already built stays near until it leaves the 5×5. What comes near is built nearest first; what leaves is taken down. An object not near whose footprint's edge comes within the tier's view distance (Low 250 m, Medium 400 m, High 600 m) is far. A change of tier works the far out again.
    - **Building:** a near object is solid at once, its zones heard and its uses offered; it is drawn a few a frame, each frame building for at most 4 ms and at least one, each top-level object built on its own as the Scene panel builds a space. The ring around where the walk starts is built whole under the loading screen, and a player put more than a cell away has theirs built whole before the next step. A moving body belongs to its authored place. Far objects are drawn as their models alone, each model's coarsest LOD level, one instanced mesh per model, material set and mesh, with far water drawn whole; fog closes at the view distance unless the environment's own closes nearer. A space smaller than a ring walks as before.
    - **The hook:** `window.__ebWalk.cells()`: the cell, the objects near and drawn, how many wait, the far models, their meshes and the level each draws, Rapier's bodies, the zones heard and uses offered, the view distance, the farthest far object, and the slowest spread frame.
    - **Snapping:** a moved box (the chosen objects' boxes joined, as three draws them, marks and lower LOD levels left out) latches touching or flush onto another object's box it is beside, the nearest within 12 px on screen at the gizmo's distance, on each axis the drag moves along; the box latched to is drawn as a wire. The grid takes the axes that didn't latch, at the step the toolbar's Grid picks (0.25, 0.5, 1 or 2 m, kept on the device). Drop stands the chosen on the highest top under them, else the terrain's highest under their corners and middle, else 0 m, as one step of undo. `window.__ebViewport` adds `boxes()`, `latched()` and `screenOf()`.
    - **The gate:**
      - The moor (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/moor`), written by `pnpm make:moor` and held to it byte for byte: 1,024 m of terrain, about 1,350 starter kit pieces over all 256 cells (cottages as a prefab of six, fences, rocks, pines with LOD, lamp posts), a road north from the spawn, a cairn's zone 158 m up it that sets a flag, and a chest on the road past it. It validates clean and builds as format 10.
      - `streaming.test.ts` walks it headless through Rapier: up the road, crossing into cells 0, −1 and −2, the ground under the feet at every step, the cairn's zone heard as the walk crossed into the cell before it, the chest offered once built and not before, and carried corner to corner with every ring held to the spec and bodies only for what is near. `cells.test.ts`, `nearObjects.test.ts` (on a clock that steps 1.5 ms a reading), `streamingStage.test.ts` and `fog.test.ts` hold the parts.
      - `streaming.spec.ts` walks it in the Play panel by keys: the start's ring built whole under the loading screen with the far drawn as instances (fewer meshes than a tenth of the models, a pine at its coarser level, none past the view distance); up the road at a run, two cells crossed, the cairn's cell built first and the south row taken down, the zone sent once and the flag on the HUD; the chest taken by E. `streamingPhone.spec.ts` runs the road by the floating stick at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390 and takes the chest by tapping the prompt.
      - `snapping.spec.ts` builds a hut from the starter kit by mouse in a new project: a floor; a wall and a doorway each dragged until they latch flush to its opposite edges, at ±0.9 m, off the 0.5 m grid; a roof latched flush over the floor and then touching the walls' tops, at 2.5 m; the grid at 1 m, the roof moved up landing at 4 m with nothing latched; Drop putting it back at 2.5 m, an undo and a redo; and a reload, the file and the step as they were. `snappingPhone.spec.ts` builds it by one finger at 360×740, 390×844 and 844×390, the toolbar with its Grid and Drop in reach.
    - **Changed on the way:**
      - **The chest stood off the road,** so a headless walk north passed it out of reach; it stands on the road now, and the walk stops against it.
      - **The far meshes' levels in the hook said how many levels a model had,** not which it drew, so drawing the whole model far would have passed; the hook now reads back the level drawn.
      - **The slowest frame counted the rings built whole** under the loading screen; it counts only spread frames now.
      - **The 1 m grid check stopped at 4 m,** where a 0.5 m step stops too; it stops at the first place at or above 3.5 m.
      - **A drag by a fixed number of pixels missed a latch** by perspective, so the snapping specs drag 2 px at a time until the view says what they want; on phones Frame came so close that an axis pointed at the camera, so they leave the camera where the space opened it.
    - **Logged for later:** questions 1 to 6 in [specs/streaming.md](docs/specs/streaming.md#open-questions): terrain tiles past 513 vertices, far cells merged into one mesh, a light built or taken down recompiling shaders, a model with no collider counting 1 m across, the first guesses, and boxes along the world's axes.
    - **Untested for feel and on a phone:** the view distances, the 4 ms a frame, the 64 m cell and the 12 px latch, until your phone walks the moor and builds the hut.
    - **45 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; 44 caught as first planted, and the other caught once it had a test.
      - Of 36 in the cells, the footprints, the streamer, the stage's streaming, the near objects' queue, the fog, bounds snap, the grid and Drop, and the moor, all 36 were caught: the kept ring gone, a footprint's reach ignored, NEAR at 0, the far past the view distance, distance from the middle, nothing taken down, taken solids built, every near object's solids added again, not nearest first, a new view distance not working the far out again, zones and uses only of what was just built, no drop from Rapier, a model's reach unscaled, a shape's reach fixed, no placements for the far, a jump never built whole, zones and uses not handed on, no ring applied, the 4 ms ignored, the queue's order turned round, nothing taken down from the view, the fog's start, an environment's nearer fog ignored, its colour lost, a latch not needing the boxes beside each other, touching or flush gone, not the nearest, no tolerance, the grid snapping a latched axis, Drop ignoring what is under, standing on what is above, sinking into the terrain, and the moor edited by hand.
      - Of 7 through the built editor, 6 were caught: far models at their whole level, frames never building, the step not kept, Drop ignoring the other boxes, a drag given no boxes, and the toolbar's step ignored. The marks counted in a box survived: the kit's collider wires fit its pieces. `snapView.test.ts` now measures a made-up node with a wire, a hidden mesh and a LOD, and caught it planted again, and two more planted only against it: a coarser LOD level counted, and a hidden mesh counted.
