## Phase 6: 3D life → Proof #2

**Goal:** the world is inhabited. NPCs keep schedules, fight and talk.

- **M6.1 NPCs.** Built from actor records: appearance, stats, inventory, faction, dialogue.
  - **As decided** (D73, your calls): an NPC is an actor record placed in a space, each actor in one place (E077); it draws as its model, else as a figure the engine draws from a height and colours; its talk field gives it a Talk prompt by Interact, and it turns to face the player as it talks; it carries a bag of its own, the inventory kit's slice going to version 2; with the factions kit it belongs to a faction; and the walk pauses under any screen ([specs/npcs.md](docs/specs/npcs.md)).
  - **Gate:** the ford, a hamlet whose smith, ferry-keeper and warden are talked to by keys, a pad and touch at 360, 390 and sideways, a horseshoe passing from the smith's bag to the party's and on, the warden speaking only to friends of the ford, the walk paused under each talk, and a save loaded with the bags kept; and an NPC made in the editor alone, placed, seen as a figure and talked to.
  - **Done** (2026-10-25; [specs/npcs.md](docs/specs/npcs.md)):
    - **An NPC:** an actor record placed in a space or a prefab. Each actor stands in one place across a project's spaces, counted through prefabs: a second is E077, at the space's object that puts it there.
    - **The actor's fields:** with any kit that has actors, `model` and `figure` (a height from 0.5 to 2.5 m, 1.75 left out, and clothes, trim and skin colours); with the dialogue kit `talk`, with the factions kit `faction`, with the inventory kit `carries`.
    - **The look:** an NPC that names a model draws as it, its collider its solid; one that doesn't is the engine's figure, a body, a waist band, a head and a nose that shows the way it faces, on a fixed capsule 0.3 m round and its height tall. The Scene panel draws figures too, with their names.
    - **Talking:** the pack gives each placed actor with a talk and no interact of its own the engine's Talk, which opens it, read through its placement's overrides; W123 counts an actor's talk as opened. Its prompt is measured from the NPC's chest, at two thirds of its height. As the talk opens the NPC turns to face the player over 250 ms, and back over 500 ms once the walk goes on, the short way round; the turn is the stage's alone and no save holds it. A screen under another draws paused, so the walk stands still under a talk, an event or a shop.
    - **Bags:** the inventory kit's slice is at version 2, with `bags`: an actor's bag is its `carries` until anything in it changes. `give-item`, `take-item` and `roll-loot` take an `actor`; `inventory.carried(actor, item)` reads one; an actor's bag raises `actor-gained`, `actor-lost` and `actor-full`. A version 1 save migrates with no bags kept, and `market-inventory-v1.json` keeps loading.
    - **Factions:** `factions.band-of(actor)` is the party's band with the actor's faction, undefined for an actor with none.
    - **The ford** (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/ford`): `ford.test.ts` plays it through the session (each talk, the horseshoe from Ada's bag to the party's and on to Bryn's, the warden's welcome held back until the ford's folk are friendly, a save loaded with the bags kept) and breaks it once for E077. `ford.spec.ts` walks it by keys and the mouse and by a stand-in pad, `fordPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and `npcEditor.spec.ts` makes Ada in the editor alone at 1280 and 390 wide, reading her back as a figure through `window.__ebViewport.figures()` and talking to her in Play.
    - **38 planted bugs**, each command first seen passing on the clean tree; all caught, 29 by unit tests and 9 by the e2e. Before they ran, reading the code showed one would survive: the walk hook said what an NPC drew as from its record, not from three, so it was made to read the scene.
    - The e2e plants ran twice. The first run was void: a half-applied edit had left the hook's `drawnAs` undefined, so every ford spec failed for that alone. On the fixed tree, one plant meant to stop the paused walk drawing an NPC's turn made it draw more instead, and survived; planted again as never drawing, it was caught. That catch leans on timing: the talk opens before the 250 ms turn ends, and a slower round trip to the worker would let it pass.
    - **Untested for feel:** the turns' times, the figure's proportions and the Talk's reach from the chest wait for your phone.
- **M6.2 Navigation.** Navmesh baking (recast-navigation-js) and crowds.
  - **As decided** (D74, your calls): a `walk-to` effect sets an NPC's goal in the sim, the stage's crowd walks it there round solids and other NPCs, and arriving is an event content can wait on; a save keeps each NPC's goal and where it stood (the world module's slice at version 3). The navmesh is baked in the editor's Scene panel and kept as `spaces/<id>.nav`, W147 when it no longer matches the space's solids, and shipped in packs and exports. A tap on the ground walks the player there along it. Solids that move don't carve it ([specs/navigation.md](docs/specs/navigation.md)).
  - **Gate:** the square, a market with stalls, a well and a gate: a carter sent by their talk across it round the stalls, twelve villagers walking between its corners without meeting, all walked headless through recast in Node and in the Play panel by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, the square crossed by a tap, a save while walking loaded with each where it stood; and a stall moved in the Scene panel, W147 shown, the navmesh baked again and the warning gone.
  - **Done** (2026-10-25; [specs/navigation.md](docs/specs/navigation.md)):
    - **The navmesh:** Bake navigation, in the Scene toolbar's Navigation row, bakes the open space in a worker for the player's body (0.35 m round, 1.8 m tall, a 0.35 m step, 40° slopes) on cells of 0.15 m by 0.1 m, one tile up to 128 m across and tiles of 128 cells past it, and writes `spaces/<id>.nav` as one step of undo: `EBNV`, a version, the hash of what it read (the solids through prefabs and overrides, placed models' colliders, the terrain's settings and heights) and Recast's export. Show navigation draws it over the view. `pnpm bake` bakes the same file in Node.
    - **Checks:** W147 when the space's solids or terrain have changed since the bake; E078 for a `.nav` that isn't one or names no space; E079 for a walk-to whose actor stands in no space, whose object the space lacks, or whose space has no `.nav`. The editor gives them as `eb validate` does, which found and fixed an older gap: E076 never fired on records in the editor.
    - **The sim:** `walk-to {actor, to}` sets a placed actor's goal; the stage's `world/npcs-at` says where each NPC stands and which arrived, raising `world/npc-arrived {actor, at}` for an arrival at the current goal only; `world.npc-at(actor)` reads where it last arrived. The world module's slice is at version 3 with `npcs`; a version 2 save migrates with none, and `glen-world-v2.json` keeps loading.
    - **Packs:** format 11 adds the navmeshes and the placed actors, only when a space has a navmesh; every pack without one keeps format 10 and its golden hash.
    - **The stage:** nav.js (recast-navigation 0.43.1 as one classic script, its wasm inside) loads beside walk.js, or from the page in a single-file export, only for a space with a navmesh. A Detour crowd walks the NPCs built near the player on the walk's fixed steps, 1.4 m/s at most, round solids, each other and the player; an NPC is there within 0.5 m of the navmesh's point nearest its goal, and its body follows its agent. Stands go to the sim every 5 s once one has moved 0.5 m and as the shell pauses, arrivals at once, a refused one again a second later.
    - **Tap to move:** a touch lifted within 300 ms and 10 px, not on a button, walks the player along Detour's corners to the navmesh's point under it, with a ring there; the stick, the keys or a pad end it at once. Touch only: on a computer a click locks the pointer.
    - **The square** (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/square`): `square.test.ts` builds it as format 11 and plays it through the session, and breaks it once each for W147, E078 and E079; the editor's `square.test.ts` walks the carter and the twelve headless through Recast with the sim hearing every arrival, never closer than 0.60 m, and bakes it again to the committed `.nav` byte for byte. `square.spec.ts` plays it by keys, `squarePhone.spec.ts` crosses it by a tap at 360, 390 and sideways, and `navEditor.spec.ts` moves a stall, sees W147, bakes and sees it gone, at 1280 and 390.
    - **A bug the gate found:** the session let only `world/trigger`, `use` and `at` past its guard on offered actions, so every stand the page sent was refused before the sim saw it, while the headless tests, which took actions straight, passed. `world/npcs-at` now passes too, and both gate tests send as the page does.
    - **A bug CI found:** NPC bodies were made kinematic so the crowd could move them, and a character that starts inside a kinematic body never gets out: M6.1's forge spawns the player on Ada's spot, so she could no longer turn to face them. NPC bodies are fixed again, set where their agents are each step; a Node test starts a character inside an NPC and walks it out, and fails with the kinematic body.
    - **27 planted bugs**, each run against the clean tree first; 26 caught, 23 by unit tests and 3 by the e2e (arrivals never told, a tap's ray mirrored, the stale row unsaid). Three survived the first run. Two were real gaps, closed with new tests: the editor's index rechecks records when only where the actors stand changes, and a corrupt `.nav` for a space someone walks in is E078 alone in the editor as in the compiler. The third removed a line that `step` made redundant, keeping an away NPC's place; no check could see it, so the line is gone.
    - **Untested for feel:** the NPCs' 1.4 m/s, how they part round each other, the tap's 300 ms and 10 px, and the ring wait for your phone.
- **M6.3 AI.** Packages and schedules (sandbox, patrol, travel, follow, sleep, work), data-driven behaviour trees, and perception by sight and hearing.
  - **As decided** (D75, your calls): the game clock runs while the player walks, at project.json's `clock.rate` (20x, a game day in 72 real minutes, when left out; 0 stops it), stopping under screens and the pause, kept in saves, with Rest and Wait still jumping it. An actor's behaviour is a record, a tree of nodes (`first`, `in-order`, guards with a condition, hours, days and a cooldown, and leaves for the six packages, wait, face and effects) that the ai kit's module re-checks after every event and that sets goals the stage walks, as D74's; a far NPC is wherever its schedule says. NPCs see (a range, a cone, a ray) and hear the player's noises, the stage saying what each sensed once a second, and an awareness from 0 to 100 climbs and decays through unaware, suspicious and alerted. Edited as an outline with forms in the inspector; the graph editor waits for Phase 8 ([specs/ai.md](docs/specs/ai.md)).
  - **Gate:** the village, a smith who works by day, sleeps at night and wanders between, and a guard who walks the walls by night and stands at the gate by day, grows suspicious of a player heard or glimpsed and halts one seen up close: played over a whole game day headless through the session and through Recast and Rapier, in the Play panel by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways with the clock running and Wait jumping it, and the guard's behaviour made in the editor's outline alone at 1280 and 390 wide.
  - **Done** (2026-10-25; [specs/ai.md](docs/specs/ai.md)):
    - **The clock:** project.json's `clock.rate`, game seconds a real second from 0 to 3,600, 20 when left out. While a space is walked with no screen or pause over it, the walk sends the time kit's `time/elapse` once a real second with the milliseconds walked, at most 2 s a step, and the kit turns them into ticks at 86,400,000 ÷ (ticks a day × rate) ms a tick. A `clock` whose rate can't split the calendar's ticks into whole milliseconds is E080; one left out that can't run at 20 stays still, W149 saying why. Packs carry it from format 12, only when it runs, so every older pack keeps its format and golden hash. With hours and minutes units the time kit gives `time.hour` and `time.clock` ("22:05").
    - **Behaviours:** the ai kit, which needs the stats and time kits, adds the `behaviour` type and an actor's `behaviour`, `sight` (15 m), `cone` (120°) and `hearing` (10 m). A behaviour's `do` is a tree: `first`, `in-order`, a guard (`if`, `hours`, `days`, `cooldown` and its `do`) and the leaves `travel`, `sleep`, `work`, `patrol`, `sandbox` (each with a `dwell`), `follow`, `wait`, `face` and `effects`; `@self` is the NPC running it. The kit's module re-checks each actor's tree after every event and keeps what each is doing in its slice (`ai.doing`, raising `ai/doing`); a leaf's goal goes to the world module. A sandbox draws its next point from the NPC's own stream.
    - **The world module at version 4:** `follow {actor, distance?}` and `stand {actor, facing?}` beside `walk-to`. A version 3 save migrates, and `square-world-v3.json` keeps loading. The crowd keeps a follower within its distance (0.5 m of slack) and turns one standing with `facing` to the player; an NPC built after the walk began starts at its goal, its arrival said at once, or beside the player it follows.
    - **Perception:** the stage looks five times a second, for the player's chest (1.2 m up) within an NPC's sight, within half its cone and with a clear ray through Rapier, and for the player's noises within their reach and its hearing (running 6 m, landing 5 m, Interact 4 m). Once a second it says what each sensed as `ai/sensed`. Awareness runs from 0 to 100: a sighting adds 2 to 8 by distance, a noise 20, and quiet takes 1 every 200 ms. An NPC is suspicious from 30 and alerted from 70 (`ai.awareness`, `ai.level`, raising `ai/level`).
    - **Checks:** E081 for a node its calendar or space can't read (a unit, an hour or a weekday the calendar lacks, a point the actor's space lacks, walking in a space with no navmesh, an actor with a behaviour that stands in no space); W148 for a behaviour no actor has; E044 for a slot other than `@self`. The editor gives them as `eb validate` does.
    - **The editor:** the AI kit's box; Clock rate under the Project panel's calendar; a behaviour's form is an outline of its steps (Add inside, Add after, Up, Down, Wrap in, Unwrap, Remove) with the chosen step's form beneath, a kind changed by a button, and each problem at its step; follow and stand in the effects' forms and words.
    - **The village** (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/village`): `village.test.ts` plays it through the session (the clock at 20x and Wait, a whole day, the guard's patrol in order, a noise and then a sighting to the halt, a save mid-patrol loaded to the same state) and breaks it once each for E080, W149, E081 and W148. The editor's `village.test.ts` walks the smith's and the guard's day through Recast and senses the guard through Rapier. `village.spec.ts` plays it by keys and the mouse, `villagePhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and `aiEditor.spec.ts` makes the guard a watch in the outline alone at 1280 and 390 wide and plays it to the walls.
    - **A bug the gate found:** a press that came while the clock's step was on its way to the worker was dropped, and Wait did nothing. The stage's steps and the player's presses now share one line (D75): a stage step that comes while another is on its way is dropped, its outbox trying it again, and a press waits behind it, the latest kept. The full e2e then found focus lost as the hut's store closed by keys and by a pad: the buttons came back a render after the view, so the one focus went back to was still disabled. They now come back in the same render, which a unit test holds.
    - **A bug main's CI found:** after M6.2 merged, `npcEditor.spec.ts` at 390 failed once because M6.1's facing stopped drawing a turn the moment its time ran out, so a stage paused under a talk could stop a frame short of facing the player. The turn now reports one frame more; a unit test holds it. M6.2's deploy waited on it and goes out with this milestone.
    - **58 planted bugs:** 55 against unit tests and 3 against the e2e (the stage never running the clock, never sensing, and a toast drawn faint), each command passing on the clean tree. 52 were caught on the first run. The 6 survivors were real gaps, each closed with a test and seen caught: the hour rounded rather than cut at the half hour; an in-order left keeping its place; `@self` unbound in a step's effects; an hour equal to the day's length let through; W149 shown away from Clock rate; and follow's words.
    - **A check made steadier:** the hut's season by keys failed once in four runs on a toast's contrast. Toasts queue, and one fading in over its 160 ms can start while axe looks, after the wait for animations. Axe now looks with motion reduced, as a player who asks for that sees every toast; the faint toast above shows it still sees them.
    - **A cost the budgets found:** every export's runtime carries every kit, so Space-Wrecked's web export grew 9,992 bytes with M6.3, past its budget by 6.3 KiB, in a game that turns no ai on. Its budget is re-read at 546,460 bytes with the same 4% room.
    - **Untested for feel:** 20x, awareness's gains and decay, the guard's cone and reach, the follow distance and the noises' reach wait for your phone.
    - **Logged for later:** an export carrying only the kits its project turns on, each kit a chunk of the runtime; and questions 1 to 5 in [specs/ai.md](docs/specs/ai.md#open-questions): NPCs travelling between spaces, combat on the same trees, a sleeping or working NPC's look before animation, following an actor or turning to a noise, and whether 20x suits a game.
- **M6.4 Combat.** Real-time melee and ranged; hits are detected by physics and damage is resolved in the sim; projectiles; death and loot.
  - **As decided** (D76, your calls): a swing or an arrow that Rapier finds reaching its target always hits, and the sim takes the weapon's damage less the target's armour off its health, with no roll; the player aims softly, turned toward the nearest foe within 30° in front, by an Attack button beside Jump, a click, F or a pad; a fallen NPC lies as a body Interact searches, its bag and a loot table rolled as it fell, and a fallen player is offered their last save; a behaviour's `attack` step closes, winds up where it can be seen, strikes and recovers, archers shooting from where they stand, at most two foes engaged at once while the rest circle ([specs/combat.md](docs/specs/combat.md)).
  - **Gate:** the camp, a clearing where three bandits (two swords, a bow) wander until alerted and then fight a hero with a sword, a bow and arrows: fought headless through the session and through Rapier and Recast in Node, the third bandit waiting at 5 m while two strike (it holds rather than circles, D76), each falling and searched, and the hero falling once and the save loaded; in the Play panel by keys and the mouse, by a pad and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways; and a bandit armed and given an attack behaviour in the editor alone at 1280 and 390 wide, then fought.
  - **Done** (2026-10-26; [specs/combat.md](docs/specs/combat.md)):
    - **The kit:** the fighting kit needs stats, gear and encounters. project.json's `fighting` names the player's actor and the `combat` record whose health, damage and armour a fight reads; E082 when it is missing once a project has a space, is given with the kit off, has the wrong shape, names no actor or no combat record, or names an actor placed in a space as an NPC. Packs carry it from format 13. An item's `weapon` group (melee: damage, reach, arc, wind-up, strike, recover; ranged: damage, range, speed, draw, recover, ammo) is worn through the gear kit; one wearing none fights with fists. W151 for a field the weapon's kind doesn't read.
    - **Hits:** the stage times every swing on the walk's fixed steps (wind-up, strike, recover) and finds what it reaches, within the reach of the wielder's chest, half the arc and a clear Rapier ray; an arrow flies under gravity, aimed up for the drop, a ray a step. `fighting/hit` is refused when either has fallen, is elsewhere, doesn't wield the weapon, or is an NPC not engaged; accepted, it takes `max(1, damage + the wielder's damage − the target's armour)` off health, with no roll. A player's shot takes its ammo first (`fighting/loosed`). A hit alerts the NPC struck (awareness to 100).
    - **The player's side:** soft aim turns the player over the wind-up toward the nearest standing foe within 30° and the weapon's reach plus 0.5 m, or range. Attack is F, a click with the pointer locked, a pad's right trigger (X is Interact) or a touch button above Jump, 64 px; held, it swings again as the last recovers. The player's capsule leans with the swing.
    - **NPCs fighting:** a behaviour's `attack` step (the ai kit, with the fighting kit on) sends the world module's `attack`; at version 5 each NPC keeps its turn (`fight`) and `down`, and `world.npc-engaged` is the first two standing by turn. An engaged melee NPC closes to its reach less 0.3 m, leans back 15° to wind up and forward 20° to strike; an archer keeps within 0.8 of its range and shoots while it has a clear line; one waiting its turn holds at 5 m and backs away. Leaving an attack stands the NPC. A version 4 save migrates; `village-world-v4.json` keeps loading.
    - **Falls:** health run out by a hit, an effect or a status fells the actor: its `drops` rolled into its bag, its `on-fall` effects run, an NPC's goal cleared and its behaviour stopped. A body lies where it fell, its capsule out of the way; Interact on it is Search, its bag moving to the party's, then Nothing left, behind any body not yet searched. The player falling pushes the `fallen` mode, where nothing is on offer and the game is not over: the shell's Fallen screen offers Back to the space it last came into (the arrival checkpoint, `checkpoint/<slot>`, kept as the player arrives, begins or loads, never the fatal step's) or the title.
    - **The editor:** the Fighting kit's box; the Project panel's Fighting form (Player, Combat), each pick a step of undo, its E082 at each field; an item's Weapon group and an actor's Drops and On fall, from the kit's type files; Attack the player in the behaviour outline, only with the kit on (E081 otherwise); W150 for an actor that attacks wearing no weapon.
    - **The camp** (`tools/cli/test/fixtures/camp`): `camp.test.ts` builds it as format 13 and fights it through the session (two strike while Wren waits, each falls, Rook's purse and coin searched, an arrow a shot, the hero falling and the save before it loading), and breaks it for E082 twice and W150. The editor's `camp.test.ts` fights it through Rapier and Recast in Node, and `fight.test.ts` holds a late arrow to nothing. `camp.spec.ts`, `campPhone.spec.ts` and `campEditor.spec.ts` fight it in the Play panel by keys and the mouse, by a pad and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways, and arm a bandit with forms alone at 1280 and 390.
    - **Bugs the gate found:** a searched body hid an unsearched one beside it from Interact, which now prefers a use with something to come of it; an NPC's arrow landing after its archer fell was sent and refused, and now hurts no one; the game's screen said "Nothing to do: the game is over" under the Fallen screen, and now says nothing while the game is not over; a bot fighting by keys won with 2 health of 30, so the hero had 50, and with 10 of 50 under the whole suite's load, so the hero has 80; and the keys test held a blow the sim rightly refused, the hero's second on Rook sent before the stage heard he had fallen, to be a bug, and now allows a refusal only after the hit that felled the striker or the target.
    - **57 planted bugs:** 54 at first, 48 against unit tests and 6 against the e2e (the F key, the pad's trigger, the touch Attack button, a click with the pointer locked, the arrival checkpoint, and the words under the Fallen screen). 43 were caught on the first run. The 11 survivors were real gaps, each closed and seen caught: a swing's reach, arc and ray, soft aim's cone and an arrow's gravity and aim had only the headless fight over them, and now have `strikes.test.ts`; the distance the crowd keeps a fighter at, its backing away and a body laid down have tests of their own; E082's names in a project with no space are held to eb validate's; and the arrival checkpoint's rule, which the camp's one space couldn't tell from the new game's, moved out of the shell's hook into `checkpoint.ts` with a test, and was planted twice there. The 57th, the hero's first blow on each bandit sent with a weapon they don't hold, was planted against the keys test's new rule on refusals: a 30% chance on Rook alone survived, as it often never fired; sent every time, it was caught.
    - **A cost the budgets found:** the editor's start grew 1,583 bytes with the fighting kit's type files and words, past its budget by 0.6 KiB; re-read at 120,893 bytes with the same 5% room.
    - **Untested for feel:** contact always hitting, the 30° cone, the wind-ups and their lean, holding at 5 m, the camp's numbers, and touch's Attack above Jump wait for your phone.
    - **Logged for later:** questions 1 to 7 in [specs/combat.md](docs/specs/combat.md#open-questions): blocking and dodging, NPCs fighting each other, taking item by item, lead on a moving target, the cone and wind-ups, keeping a press made mid-swing, and the camp's numbers on a phone.
- **M6.5 Animation.** State machines from clips, sharing one rig.
  - **As decided** (D77, your calls):
    - **A starter character generated in code:** a mannequin on the engine's rig, with idle, walk, run, the air, a swing's three phases, a bow's, a hit, a fall and a talk. Dedicated CC0 by RCJLabs.
    - **The figure becomes it:** the figure (D73) and the player wear it.
    - **One rig:** VRM's humanoid. Clips made for other skeletons (Mixamo, Unreal, Quaternius, KayKit) are carried onto it as they are imported, normalised once in the model worker and made to play in place.
    - **Two layers:** attacks play on an upper-body layer over the legs.
    - **Animators are records:** an `animator` record holds the state machine, its transitions in the condition text over the stage's `anim.*` facts. The editor edits it as an outline with a 3D preview ([specs/animation.md](docs/specs/animation.md)).
  - **Gate:**
    - **Headless:** the animator's transitions, blends and rates; a foreign rig made in code, normalised with every vertex where it was; its walk played on the starter character with the same bone directions.
    - **The camp in the Play panel,** read through `__ebWalk.anims()`:
      - by keys: the hero and the bandits walk and run at their clips' pace, jump, swing with the upper layer's phases lined up with the hits, and fall;
      - the same by touch at 360, 390 and sideways.
    - **In the editor alone** at 1280 and 390 wide: a foreign walk imported and mapped, and an animator made, previewed and seen in Play.
  - **Done** (2026-10-26; [specs/animation.md](docs/specs/animation.md)):
    - **The rig:** VRM 1.0's humanoid, 22 bones, 15 of them required. `@eb/models` maps a skin's joints to it by name (VRM, Mixamo, Unreal, Quaternius, KayKit, Blender's), held to the hierarchy, and a model's form can say otherwise; it normalises the skeleton once, each rest a T-pose on the world's axes facing −z, the inverse bind matrices and every clip's keys carried with it, so every vertex draws where it did; and takes the hips' drift out of every clip, kept as its pace. The model worker does it before packing, and the record gets its `rig` and `clips`; a skinned model starts with no LOD levels. W152 for a skin off the rig.
    - **The starter character:** `pnpm make:starter-character` writes `starter-character/` from `tools/starter-kit/src/character`, byte for byte: a mannequin of 372 triangles on the rig, 1.76 m, five material slots, 16 clips sampled from formulas at 30 frames a second, each frame lifted out of the ground bone by bone, and the stock animator; dedicated CC0 by RCJLabs. walk.js and the editor carry it; Add starter character copies it into a project.
    - **Animators:** an `animator` record names the model its clips come from and holds a whole-body layer and an upper-body one, each of states (a clip, from another model if it says, looping or not, at a fixed speed, by pace or fit to a phase) and transitions read in order from the state playing, then from any state, on conditions over the stage's `anim.*` facts, with blends. An actor's `animator` picks one; the stock one serves the rest. E083 for a clip its model hasn't or a model off the rig, E084 for states that don't fit together, E085 for an animator on an actor whose model is off the rig, W153 for a state nothing reaches. The pack's world carries each placed actor's animator and the models its clips come from.
    - **In the walk:** the player and every NPC built are the starter character, moved by their animator on three's mixer: the legs from the whole body, the upper body from the upper layer where it plays, stepped by the motion's time every frame within 30 m, every other frame to 60 m and not at all beyond (Low halves both). The fight gives each fighter its swing's phase, its bow's, its weapon, its fall and a hit's 0.4 s; the swing's states are fit to the phases the fight times, so the arm comes down as the hit is found. M6.4's leans are gone. Under a talk's pause the NPC talked to goes on moving. `window.__ebWalk.anims()` reads back each layer, what the mixer plays and six bones.
    - **The editor:** an animator's layers as outlines, as behaviours' are: a row a state with its transitions under it, numbered in the order they are read; Add state, Add transition, Up, Down, Remove, Add and Remove upper body; the chosen row's form, its clip a choice of its model's clips and a state renamed where the layer names it. Its conditions read the `anim.*` facts alone. Above it a preview, a lazy chunk, plays it on the starter character on facts set by a slider, switches and choices, and says what each layer plays.
    - **The gate:** headless, the animator's run, the mixer on the real GLB and the walk's bodies by distance; a foreign rig made in code (Mixamo's names, an A-pose, a centimetre armature facing +z, a walk with root motion) normalised with every vertex where it was to 1e-5, resting in metres where it stood, its walk on the engine's rig with the same bone directions to 1°, its pace read to 1%; the starter character byte for byte, with nothing more than 5 mm under the ground. `anim.spec.ts` and `animPhone.spec.ts` read every frame of the camp by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: idle, the walk and the run at the speed over their paces, the air, the swing's phases with the strike as each hit goes and the hand coming down and forward, and a bandit down. `animEditor.spec.ts` imports a foreign rig's walk into the ford, makes an animator in its outline and tries it in its preview at 1280 and 390, and sees Ada play it as her talk opens.
    - **Bugs the gate found:** clips sank into the ground (the fall-down 9 cm, a walk's toes 4 cm), now lifted bone by bone; a fit state fading out took the next phase's progress, snapping the arm to standing for a frame; the run's line was the walk's own speed; animation stepped by frame time slid against the motion's fixed steps; and a test reading only a skin's first primitive had hidden part of the character. The kits' spec listed every type Records offers and hadn't the animator; and the jump's check waited 1.5 s of wall time, which a loaded machine's frames didn't fill, so it now waits on the frames and was seen failing on a planted dropped Jump. In CI, four more tests read by machine raced a loaded box's frames, each reproduced locally four at once and fixed in the test: a key held to a line the player was already past stayed down for a round trip and walked them out of Ada's reach; W let go from the test walked the trail's player off the far side of the step; the jump button's tap alone took 22 frames, so the jump was over before the height was read, which the page now records each frame; and the smithy's spawn had been left where Add put it, under Ada, so the yaw toward the player turned by degrees as their feet settled. Each new check was seen failing on a planted bug.
    - **41 planted bugs:** 31 against unit tests and 10 against the e2e (the record form's outline, its conditions' facts, the clip choices, a state renamed, the preview's decoded clips and its facts, the import's rig, the talker under the pause, the animation's time, and, against the jump's new wait, a dropped touch Jump). 34 of the first 40 were caught on the first run. The 6 survivors were real gaps, each closed and seen caught: a rig's rest in metres where it stood (the hips' offset left in centimetres had passed, as the walk's keys set the hips); a pace drifting across both axes; E083 for a model off the rig whose record lists the clip; the world packing the model a state takes its clip from; each copy of the character on bones of its own; and the clip choices held to the model a state takes its clip from, which offered the starter character's own walk unnoticed.
    - **What the budgets found:** M6.5's weight left `scene-view` 779 bytes under its budget and the precache and the trail's export under 2%; the six sizes it grew (the start, the Scene view, the models' chunk, walk.js, the precache and the trail) were re-read with the same 5% room.
    - **Untested for feel:** every clip, the blends, the 3.5 m/s run, and the 30 and 60 m distances wait for your phone.
    - **Logged for later:** questions 1 to 9 in [specs/animation.md](docs/specs/animation.md#open-questions): the clips' feel, feet on slopes, faces, a weapon in the hand, previewing on an actor's own model and choosing joints from the skeleton, root motion, non-humanoid rigs, a node graph, and a player with a model of its own.
- **M6.6 Quest markers and maps.** A compass, a marker over the target and a map of each space, pointing the way for the tracked quest.
  - **As decided** (D78, your calls): Quest UX is three milestones, this the first. An objective names its `target` (an actor, or a space and an object in it), or has it read from what completes it; one quest is tracked, the last started unless the journal tracks another, and a save keeps which; the compass strip and a marker over a target on screen point at each open objective of the tracked quest, a target in another space by the door toward it, found over the spaces' doors; a setting hides them; Bake map in the Scene panel draws a space from above once, kept beside it and warned of when the space changes after (W154), and the pack carries it to a Map screen whose pins are the player and the targets ([specs/markers.md](docs/specs/markers.md)).
  - **Gate:** the glen's supplies quest followed by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: the compass and the marker on the rope, then on the bothy's door and, inside, the lamp, then the shed's way, each read through `window.__ebWalk.markers()`; a second quest tracked from the journal moving the compass; the setting hiding it; and the glen's maps baked in the editor and shown with their pins; and an objective's target set with forms in the editor alone, at 1280 and 390.
  - **Done** (2026-10-26; [specs/markers.md](docs/specs/markers.md)):
    - **Targets:** an objective's `target` is `{actor}` (an id or `@slot`) or `{space, object?}`; one left out is read from a count of `world/used` or `fighting/searched`. E086 for a target naming what the project hasn't, W155 for an actor placed nowhere. The quest's form offers the actors and the quest's actor slots, the spaces, and the named space's objects through its prefabs.
    - **Tracking:** the quests kit's slice v3 keeps `tracked`, the last started; when it ends, the first active by id. `quests/track` takes no time and no mode offers it; the journal's entries say which is tracked and offer Track on the rest. `dock-quests-v2.json` keeps loading, migrated to the first active.
    - **The stage:** after each step the session hands the walk the tracked quest's open targets, slots bound. The walk places each through its own `objectPlaces`, an actor where the crowd has it, and a target in another space at the door toward it (`doorToward`, over the uses and zones that enter a space). The compass strip across the top shows a pip and the distance for each, and the objective's text within 10°, only while there is a marker; a diamond stands over each on screen. Quest markers in the shell's settings hides them. `window.__ebWalk.markers()` reads them back.
    - **The map:** Bake map in the Scene toolbar's Map row draws the open space from above on the view's own canvas, 1024 px, lit from the north-west: what a player sees, with the editor's marks, zones, spawn points and characters left out, and indoors each mesh wholly above 2.4 m over the highest spawn point. It writes `spaces/<id>.map.json` with the bounds and the hash of what it drew, beside a WebP, as one step of undo; W154 when the space changes after, E087 for a map that can't be read. The pack carries it as format 14; the Map screen (the `space-map` widget, a stock template, and a Map button on the stock Space screen) draws it, or plain ground, with the player's pin and a 44 px pin for each target or door that says its objective.
    - **The gate:** headless, the glen's quests followed through the session and the walk's own places (`glenMarkers.test.ts`), the doors, the pins, tracking and its migration, the checks and format 14. `markers.spec.ts` by keys: the rope and the gate, the lamp at the bothy's door, Track from the journal turning the compass, the Map screen's pins, the setting, the lamp inside, the way out, the shed's door, and nothing once both quests are done. `markersPhone.spec.ts` by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: the compass clear of Jump and the prompt and taking no touch, Track by a tap, 44 px pins, the setting. `markersEditor.spec.ts` at 1280 and 390: the glen baked, W154 after the rope moved, baked again, the gate's target set with forms alone, and the baked image in play with the pins on it.
    - **Bugs the gate found:** the record index never had the spaces' objects (`withScreens` dropped them), so the target's Object offered nothing and E086 never checked an object; the bake drew the editor's collider wires, spawn arrows and zones; the planned cut at 2.4 m took the top off every wall, so the glen's buildings showed only as shadows; the sun's shadow camera stood inside any space wider than 15.6 m, a line crossing the ground at its near plane, and with no bias broad floors shadowed themselves (both fixed for the editor's view and the walk); and the Map widget at 60% of the height pushed the screen's bar off a 1280 by 800 screen. In CI, `npcEditor.spec.ts` at 390 found the player 2.7 m from Ada: an object's x, y and z, typed faster than the form redrew, each wrote the whole triple from the last render and put back an axis whose edit hadn't drawn, so the spawn stayed where Add put it. Each box now sets its axis against the file as the edit lands (`setAxis`), and the spec checks the spawn's place. A version 2 glen save now starts Find your feet as it replays and tracks it, the last started.

- **M6.7 Radiant aliases.** A quest's slot filled by finding a matching reference placed in the world ("a chest in a cave", "an NPC of the bandits"). Its calls come first (D78).
  - **As decided** (D79, your calls): a slot can hold a space found by its tags and, in a later slot, an object in it or an actor standing there, found by theirs; spaces and objects carry tags, an object's kept through prefabs and overrides; a slot takes today's picks or the nearest by doors, a repeat trying its last run's pick last; a fallen NPC or a taken object is never found, and nothing comes back in M6.7 ([specs/radiant.md](docs/specs/radiant.md)).
  - **Gate:** the hollows, a green with a board and a post, a cave one door away and another two, each with a cache and a bandit. Supplies repeats on "the cache in the nearest cave" and the bounty on "a bandit in the nearest cave", each going to the barrow, then the mine, then waiting once both are taken or felled. Checked:
    - headless through the session, a save mid-run loading to the same slots;
    - in the Play panel by keys: the compass through the right doors and both caches taken;
    - by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: the board read by a tap, the compass, the journal naming the cave;
    - in the editor alone at 1280 and 390: the caves and the cache tagged and Supplies' slots made with forms, then played.
  - **Done** (2026-10-26; [specs/radiant.md](docs/specs/radiant.md)):
    - **Tags:** a space and any object carry `tags`, up to 16 words, each once (E058). An object's are kept through prefabs and replaced whole by an override; a prefab has none of its own (E058). The pack is format 15 once any space or object has a tag: every space's tags, and each tagged object's by its path with the name `{slot}` shows. Every pack without keeps its format and hash.
    - **Slots that find in the world:**
      - *What they find:* a `space` slot finds a space by its tags. An `object` slot finds an object In a space or an earlier space slot. An actor slot with In or Tags finds a placed actor. A taken object or a fallen actor is never found.
      - *Which one:* Nearest takes the fewest doors from the player's space (`world.doors`). Spaces no door reaches are left out, and ties are drawn from the kit's stream.
      - *Repeats and retries:* a repeat tries its last run's pick last. A space slot that a later slot is In tries its next match when that slot can't be filled; when none can, the refusal names the inner slot.
      - *Names and gone:* `{cave}` shows the space's name and `{cache}` the object's. A run's space or object that a later build lacks is gone, as a record is.
      - *Facts:* `factions.faction-of(actor)` answers "an NPC of the bandits". The quests kit is built with the pack's places, and `kitsInWorld` swaps it in for `eb sim` and Run.
    - **Checks:** E088 for a world slot that can't be read. W127 now also for tags nothing in the project carries where the slot can look. E086 takes a target's `@space` and `@object`, the object slot In that space.
    - **The editor:**
      - *Tags in the inspector:* for the open space with nothing chosen, for an object beside its name, and for an object inside a placement as an override with Revert. Each tag is a chip with a 44 px remove button, and the box offers the project's tags.
      - *The quest's form:* a slot's In offers the spaces, then the space slots before it. A target's Space and Object offer the space slots and the object slots In that space.
      - *Checks as you edit:* the index checks the quests again when only the spaces' tags change.
    - **The gate:**
      - *Headless:* `hollows.test.ts` builds format 15 and plays Supplies and the bounty through the session. A save mid-run loads to the same slots, and the fixture breaks once each for E088, W127 and E086.
      - *By keys:* `radiant.spec.ts` plays the board to the barrow's crate, then through the hall to the mine's chest, and a third read is refused.
      - *By touch:* `radiantPhone.spec.ts` at 360, 390 and sideways reads the board by a tap and checks the compass on the barrow's door, the journal and the Map's 44 px pin.
      - *In the editor:* `radiantEditor.spec.ts` at 1280 and 390 starts from the hollows with no tags and no Supplies. It tags the caves (W127 gone), the barrow's crate, the mine's chest through the prefab, and the empty chest by an override. It makes Supplies with forms, E088 shown until In names the cave, and plays it to the barrow's door.
    - **What the gate found:**
      - A quest with no stage fails its type first, so its slots show no E088 until it has one. Left as it is, and said in radiant.md's As built.
      - `worldSlots.test.ts` passed its tests but not Biome: a `forEach` that returned a value. Run before pushing, `pnpm lint` would have caught it; CI went red on it twice (runs 293 and 294) until `a35b368`.
    - **66 planted bugs:**
      - *57 against the unit tests of the sim, the pack, the compiler, the checks and the editor:*
        - 44 of the first 56 were caught on the first run.
        - 11 of the 12 that got past were closed with tests: a run's space or object gone from a later build; Nearest's unreachable spaces; the refusal's slot; a slot of records keeping M3.10's ways; `kitsInWorld`; `hasTags` with only a space's tags; a placement's own name before its prefab's; E058 past 16 tags; the index's tags key; and the last-pick test, made to run over a dozen seeds as one draw passed half the time.
        - The twelfth, any slot In another backtracking, is equivalent: E088 refuses In naming anything but a space slot. Every slot backtracking, the 57th, is caught.
      - *9 against the e2e, all caught:* a target's slots left unbound, by keys and by touch (the touch spec caught it at the compass, before the Map's pin); the taken found again, so a third read starts; a tag not added; a tag not removed; an override's tags lost; In without the space slots; the index's tags key; and a 24 px remove button, caught by the new phone checks.

      - the precache by 31,430;

- **M6.8 Barter and containers.** Trading with an NPC from their bag and purse; chests and other containers taken from and put into, item by item. Its calls come first (D78).
  - **As decided** (D80, your calls): each NPC has a purse, kept once it changes; a shop names its NPC, who sells from their bag, buys into it with their purse, and restocks both every few days; any object's interact can hold items, taken from and put into one line at a time, and a fallen body opens the same screen; no capacity, owners or theft yet ([specs/barter.md](docs/specs/barter.md)).
  - **Gate:** the post, a yard where Hild trades from her bag and purse, with a chest of bread and a candle and an empty barrel. Checked:
    - headless through the session: a lamp bought, the candle sold and bought back, Hild's purse run dry and restocked after three days, the chest emptied line by line and a lamp put in the barrel, a save mid-trade, and old inventory and economy saves loading;
    - the camp's bodies opened and taken from line by line;
    - by keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: the chest opened, a line taken and one put back, and Hild's goods bought and sold;
    - in the editor alone at 1280 and 390: the chest's Holds, Hild's Purse and the shop's Actor set with forms, then played.
  - **Done** (2026-10-26; [specs/barter.md](docs/specs/barter.md)):
    - **Purses:** an actor's `purse` names what it holds of each resource. The economy kit keeps a purse whole once any of it changes, within its resources' bounds (slice v2, `economy.purse`).
    - **Merchants:**
      - *Selling and buying:* a shop's `actor` sells from their bag, at the shop's prices and with the actor's faction when the shop names none. What the party sells goes into the bag and the price comes out of the purse; it refuses `cant-pay`, and `no-room` past an item's `max`.
      - *Restocking:* the first visit, and each one after `stock.every.days`, applies `restock-bag`, an inventory effect. The bag goes back to `carries` with the stock on top as far as each fits, and the purse back to the record's.
    - **Containers:**
      - *What holds:* an interact's `holds` names what the object starts with. Using it opens the inventory kit's `container` mode after the use's effects. Take, Put, Take all and Close move a line at a time, Take only as much as fits (`none`, `full`).
      - *Kept and packed:* the slice keeps what changed by `space/object` (v3). The pack is format 16 once any use holds, every space untagged when none has a tag. The kit is built with the pack's containers, and its mode is only a game's that can open something.
      - *Bodies:* `fighting/search` opens a body's bag in the same mode. The stage offers Search until the bag is empty, then Nothing left.
    - **Checks:** E089 for a container that takes, or one with the inventory kit off. W156 for a shop whose actor carries nothing and whose stock is empty.
    - **Screens:** the stock Container screen (Take and Put tabs, Take all, Close) and a merchant's `{shop.keeper} has {shop.purse}` on the Shop screen. The sources are `held`, `container.name`, `shop.keeper` and `shop.purse`.
    - **The editor:**
      - *Forms:* an interact's Holds, an actor's Purse and a shop's Actor are forms like any other.
      - *Checks as you edit:* the game is checked again when the first `holds` comes or the last goes, the script worker getting a holding space's file whole. A shop and its actor are checked together for W156.
      - *New screen:* it offers Container once the game's modes have it.
    - **The gate:**
      - *Headless:* `post.test.ts` builds format 16 and plays through the session: a lamp bought, the chest's candle sold and bought back, Hild's purse run dry and restocked after three days, the chest emptied line by line and a lamp put in the barrel. A save mid-trade loads to the same bags and purses, and the fixture breaks once each for E089 and W156. `camp.test.ts` opens Rook's body, takes two coins, closes it still searched but not emptied, and takes the rest. A save by the inventory kit's version 2 and the economy kit's version 1 loads.
      - *By keys:* `barter.spec.ts` takes the chest's bread and puts it back, buys Hild's lamp and sells her the candle, her purse on screen, and puts the lamp in the empty barrel.
      - *By touch:* `barterPhone.spec.ts` at 360, 390 and sideways opens the chest by a tap on its prompt, takes the bread and puts it back, and buys and sells at Hild's counter, each screen fitting with every line 44 px. The camp's specs take each body's bag with Take all.
      - *In the editor:* `barterEditor.spec.ts` at 1280 and 390 starts from the post with no holds, no purse, no shop's actor and no Container screen. It runs the scripts first: no Container template. It sets Hild's Purse, the shop's Actor and the chest's Holds with forms, E089 shown while the chest also takes, and the template appears with nothing run by hand. Each file ends as the fixture's, and in Play the chest opens with its bread.
    - **What the gate found:**
      - New screen read only the kits, so Container, which needs the game's modes, was never offered; it reads the modes now.
      - The script worker got every space's file empty, so the editor's game had no container mode (E049 on `ui/container.json`). A holding space's file now goes whole, and the game is checked again as `holds` comes and goes.
      - A format 16 pack with no tags failed `readPack`, which wants format 15's places; such a world is now packed untagged.
      - The container mode in every inventory game asked every fixture's screens to show it (W133), so it joins only a game that can open something.
      - A commit went in with a formatting error: piped through `tail`, `pnpm lint`'s failure was lost. It was fixed before the push.
      - CI on an early head (d136f7d, docs and a save alone) failed `camp.spec.ts`: one of Wren's arrows was refused, as the stage's view, a round trip old, still had her fighting when the sim had taken her out. M6.4's stage did right; its spec had no word for it. Each hit the stage sends now says whether the view had its striker engaged (`fight().sent[].engaged`), and the spec explains such a refusal. Two planted bugs, the flag always set and never set, were each seen failing in `fight.test.ts`.
      - CI on 0dc2ab9 failed two older specs, each a race on a busy runner, neither M6.8's:
        - `designer.spec.ts` ticked the box "hut" by label while the frame's screen, its title not yet the one just typed, bore the same name. `tick` now finds a checkbox by role.
        - `animEditor.spec.ts` read the preview's hand twice 250 ms apart, and no frame was drawn between. It now waits for the hand to move.
      - While reading those, the editor was found reading every space and prefab for `holds` at every refresh, on a record's edit as on a space's. It reads them now only when a space, a prefab or project.json changed; the editor spec catches that rescan taken away, and the check dropped, each planted once.

The 5 that got past were closed with tests: a whole line put, a restock as far as each fits, a purse within its bounds, a purse kept whole, and a body searched but not emptied.

        - The seventh, Container offered before the scripts run, was never exercised, so its code was taken out.

  - **As decided** (D81, your calls): the valley hamlet in third person (a reeve's job against bandits, a smith who trades, a radiant supplies job, a cave a door away) and the lighthouse at night in first person (the logbook, the keeper's chest, the locked store, the lamp), about ten minutes each, checked by machine as D61. Bots play a walked game through a headless stage that offers what a stage could send, each kept only when the sim takes it ([specs/templates.md](docs/specs/templates.md), [specs/runner.md](docs/specs/runner.md#a-walked-games-stage-m69-d81)).

  - **Done** (2026-10-27; [specs/templates.md](docs/specs/templates.md#as-built-m69)):

      - *What it offers:* `stageOptions` joins a run's stages: the world's uses and zones in the player's space, the player's blows at the NPCs there, an engaged NPC's blow back, and the search of a fallen body. Each is kept only when the sim takes it.
      - *Where it runs:* `walkedStage` gives `eb sim` and the editor's Run `walk`, or `walk+fight` when project.json names fighting. A golden file names its stage, and one made with another is a usage error.

      - *Headless:* `templates.test.ts` holds both to no problems, to their golden files (the hamlet 200 seeds, the lighthouse 500), and to every stage and ending reached with nothing refused. `eb new` writes the hamlet byte for byte.
      - *In the editor:* `templates.test.ts` holds every bundled file to the one on disk.
      - *By keys:* `templates3d.spec.ts` makes each from New project, follows every Make it yours step to its place, walks it to its ending, and plays the exported file from `file://`.

    - **What the gate found:**
      - *Mangled models:* the production build bundled the hamlet's GLBs as text, and Play refused it with eight E070s. A bare exclusion glob is read from the build's root; the exclusions name the folder now.

      - *Touch fingers that never lifted:* the e2e's fingers lifted one of two with a touchMove that left it out, which Chrome takes as still down. Under the held Attack, each look drag undid the last, and the hero walked away from Gorm. They lift by a touchEnd naming the finger now, and the camp's, the post's and the animation's phone specs pass with it.
      - *Long turns:* a touch turn wider than the view is now drags of 45° at most.
      - *A fight that began too late:* by touch, the hero fell walking to the woods' edge before the fight helper began. The fight starts west of the well now.

The tenth, the bare exclusion, passed Vitest, whose root differs, and was caught by the built editor's spec when Play refused the project.

The start grew 397, the Scene view 163, Space-Wrecked's export 623 and the trail's 709, as the runtime's quests kit says whether a quest can start. Five townsfolk keep a day's schedule and talk, and all are essential. *The Howe* (main), *Tove's Net* (side) and *The Watch* (radiant) are the quests, with outlaws to fight and Gudrun and Ulf to trade with.       - *The quests:* *The Howe*, *Tove's Net* and *The Watch*, Halvard's radiant job on an outlaw at a camp. Gudrun and Ulf trade from their bags and purses. The silver laid at Ragnhild's seat ends it.
      - *Its bot:* `stranger`, through the walk and fight stage.
      - *Deployed:* `pnpm build` exports it to `games/kaldvik/` beside the editor, and CI's confirm job loads its files live.
    - **The gate:**
      - *Headless:* `tools/cli/test/kaldvik.test.ts` validates it clean and plays 50 seeds to their golden file with nothing refused. The silver comes home in most runs and every stage of every quest is reached in some. Halvard, struck down, has no talk until he is up. `shore.test.ts` holds the terrain files to the generator byte for byte.
      - *Rapier and Recast in Node:* `apps/editor/src/walk/kaldvik.test.ts` walks the five to their day's, evening's and night's places, fights the howe's three until they lie, takes the key and the silver, and lays Halvard down and stands him up on the terrain.
      - *By keys:* `kaldvik.spec.ts` plays the built export from New game to the ending, at 800×600.
      - *By touch:* `kaldvikPhone.spec.ts`, at 390×844 and 844×390: Gudrun's talk and a flatbread bought, Halvard's talk, the hall's door, each screen fitting with 44 px targets and nothing axe finds.
      - *Its size:* `kaldvik-web` in budgets.json, also checked by the keys spec.
    - **What the gate found:**
      - *A ghost click on touch:* main's CI emptied a chest under a thumb held on the prompt, as the click a tap makes landed on the chest it had opened. The prompt cancels its touchstart now, and the e2e holds a prompt tap for 400 ms.
      - *Shops that counted twice:* a merchant's shelf is their `carries` plus the stock, and both listed the same goods, so Gudrun's six flatbreads showed as twelve. Each stock is a top-up now. The shop screen also kept the hamlet's title.
      - *One space walked:* the world module takes arrivals only in the player's space, so the headless day test walks the stranger into each space in turn.
      - *A stranger who barely lived:* at 40 health, the howe's three left them 6. At 60 they leave 26.
      - *An NPC beside their mark:* the crowd steps an NPC aside as the player comes, so the e2e's talks aim at the NPC, not at their mark.
      - *Slow frames:* at 1280×900 the e2e's software renderer drew the shore at 9 fps and the keys spec took 9 minutes. At 800×600 it draws 20.
      - *Ids twice:* two solids shared ids with marks, and the behaviours named the solids. Marks have names of their own now.
    - **33 planted bugs, all caught:**
      - *25 against essential NPCs:* the sim, the save's migration, the checks, the stage and the bots.
      - *8 against the gate:* the generator twice, the golden file, the budget in `pnpm budgets` and in the spec, the ending's words, a flatbread priced past the stranger's purse, and a file of the export gone from the site check.
      - A ninth, a shop heading too long for a phone, changed nothing a player would see, as the screens wrap it. It isn't counted.
    - **What the budgets found:** the essential NPCs' code grew the start 176 bytes, the Scene view 213, the precache 7,002, walk.js 280, Space-Wrecked's export 1,295 and the trail's 2,075. Those six were re-read.
    - **Untested for feel:** everything, the 60 health and the fights above all, until your phone walks it.
    - **Logged for later:** questions 1 to 3 in [specs/kaldvik.md](docs/specs/kaldvik.md#open-questions): townsfolk who walk between spaces, a bot that heads for the main quest sooner, and fighting the howe's three at once.

**Done when:**

- Bots complete every quest headless; Rapier runs in Node. [auto] Done 2026-10-27 (M6.10): Kaldvík's `stranger` reaches every stage of every quest across its seeds and brings the silver home in most, and `apps/editor/src/walk/kaldvik.test.ts` walks and fights it through Rapier and Recast.
- It holds ≥30 fps on your phone at Low and Med. [you]

## Phase 7: 2D worlds and genre kits → Proof #3

**Goal:** the rest of the portfolio's styles and genres.

- **M7.1 2D scenes.** Orthographic, on the same viewport: sprites and sprite-sheet animation, parallax, pixel-perfect integer scaling.
  - **As decided** (D83, your calls): a keep at night in afterglass's pixel-art style is the demo; a starter sprite kit made in code (CC0, by RCJLabs) is the art; a 2D space is shown, animated and tapped in play, with no player avatar yet; sheets are a grid with named clips. A 2D space is a space with `view: "2d"`, measured in art pixels and drawn at a whole scale; parallax is a component; figures walk in straight lines to the marks the sim sends them to; stage2d.js draws it in play and exports; the pack is format 17 ([specs/2d-scenes.md](docs/specs/2d-scenes.md)).
  - **Gate:**
    - `eb validate` finds the keep clean and breaks once for each new check (E091, E058's 2D rules, a 2D walker needing no navmesh);
    - the starter sprites held to their generator byte for byte, validating clean;
    - the keep's guards walk the wall through a night headless, the sim hearing each arrival;
    - the keep played in the Play panel by mouse and keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: drawn at a whole scale with only the palette's colours, parallax at its factors, clips advancing, the bell rung by a tap, a click and the keyboard; and exported as one file played from `file://`;
    - a 2D space built from the starter sprites in the Scene panel at 1280 and 390, then played;
    - stage2d.js, the precache and the editor's start within their budgets.
  - **Done** (2026-10-27; [specs/2d-scenes.md](docs/specs/2d-scenes.md#as-built-m71)):
    - **Records and checks:** `sprite`, an engine record type (a sheet under assets/, a frame size, an origin, clips as frame ranges at a rate); the `sprite` and `parallax` component kinds; an actor's `sprite` and `speed`; a space's `view`, `fit`, `size` and `backdrop`. E091 is a sprite that can't be drawn: its record's rules, its sheet read to the PNG header, and a component's clip it lacks. E058 holds a 2D space to boxes and spheres at whole scales with no turn, terrain, environment, water or model, and a 3D space to no sprite or parallax. A walker in a 2D space needs no navmesh (no E079). The pack is format 17 once a space is 2D, and an older format refuses one.
    - **The starter sprites** at `starter-sprites/`: 16 sheets in a palette of 16 (three figures with idle and walk, a torch, a bell, a banner, the keep's wall, battlement, tower, gate and window, the night sky, stars, moon and two ridges), 5,086 bytes of PNG, written by `pnpm make:starter-sprites` and held to it byte for byte, dedicated by RCJLabs to CC0.
    - **The stage:** stage2d.js, 13,932 bytes (5,885 gzipped), built beside walk.js and picked by the hosts by the space's view; an export carries it only for a 2D space, inside a single file or beside a web build's page. It draws with Canvas 2D at whole pixels, smoothing off, not three.js (D83's As built). Clips play on the page's clock and hold still under reduced motion; figures walk to the sim's marks and their arrivals are told back; each use is a 44 px button of the page's own; the clock runs as it does in 3D. Its hook is `window.__ebStage2d`.
    - **The editor:** New 2D space; a 2D view in the Scene panel at whole zooms from 1 to 16, choosing by a frame's box, a drag on the chosen moving them and elsewhere panning, the arrow keys a pixel or 8, a pinch and the wheel zooming about the pointer, and Frame; Add → Sprite…, Mark, a prefab or a record; the place in art pixels in the inspector; above a sprite's form, its sheet with the frame grid and each clip playing; Add starter sprites. Its hook is `window.__ebView2d`.
    - **The keep** at `tools/cli/test/fixtures/keep/`: 960 by 180 art pixels at a 320 by 180 fit, five layers in parallax, the keep's wall, battlements, towers and gate, four torches, two guards walking the wall walk all night, and a bell whose use rings it.
    - **The gate's checks:** `keep.test.ts` validates the keep clean, builds it as format 17 and breaks it once each for E091, E058's 2D rules and the walker's navmesh; the keep's guards walk a night headless with the sim hearing each arrival; `keep.spec.ts`, `keepPhone.spec.ts` (360, 390, sideways) and `keepExport.spec.ts` (one file from `file://`, a web build offline) play it, every pixel of its canvas one of the palette's; `keepEditor.spec.ts` builds a 2D space in the editor alone at 1280 and 390 and plays it.
    - **31 planted bugs,** 26 at the unit level and 5 through the built page, each caught. One survived at first: `frameAt`'s wrap for a moment before a clip began, which validated content never reaches; a test line now holds it. The editor spec had no check that arrow presses in a row are one step of undo; it got one before the plants ran, and caught the plant that split them.
    - **Main's red Kaldvík e2e,** carried here: a use waited behind the walk's bookkeeping. On CI's software renderer each step to the worker takes a second or more, and the clock's elapses and the senses' reports queued up behind each other, eleven deep, with a use behind them all; the howe door's waited past 15 s. Now a use goes ahead of the stage's waiting letters, and the clock and the senses each keep one letter waiting, merging what comes meanwhile ([specs/worldspaces.md](docs/specs/worldspaces.md), [specs/ai.md](docs/specs/ai.md)). 13 planted bugs, all caught. A slow phone pressing Interact gained the same.
    - **Main's run on the merge failed once more in the howe,** with "Nothing fights in the space walked" just after the door: the walk was gone. A fall takes the walk with the game (the fallen mode has no screen, so its fallback is drawn alone), and nothing else in the howe can. Not reproduced: three local runs with Chromium's CPU slowed 4×, 6× beside the phone spec and 8× each won the fight with 51 to 60 of 60 health, every space's stage started once, and PR #69's CI passed the same tree. The failed job was run again once. `fought()` now says when the player fell (camp.spec holds it; one planted bug, caught), so a repeat names its cause.
    - **Untested for feel** until you see the keep on your phone.
- **M7.2 Tilemaps.** Layers, autotiling, ASCII import, and walk/cover/sight per tile.
  - **As decided** (D84, your calls): Space-Wrecked's example ship as a boarding deck is the demo, its battle map imported from Space-Wrecked's own text; walls stand on tiles and on the edges between them; a starter tileset made in code in the starter sprites' palette; in play the map is drawn, the walkers the sim sends route round its walls, and sight and cover are facts the sim reads. A tileset is an engine record type (tiles with their rules, a legend, edge frames); a tilemap is a component of an object in a 2D space, its layers rows of characters and its edges Space-Wrecked's map text; autotiling is a 4-bit mask; sight, cover and four-way routes are Space-Wrecked's, ported to the pure core; the pack is format 18 ([specs/tilemaps.md](docs/specs/tilemaps.md)).
  - **Gate:**
    - `eb validate` finds the deck clean and breaks once each for E092, E093, E058 (a tilemap in a 3D space) and W157;
    - the starter sprites, with the tileset, held to their generator byte for byte, validating clean;
    - unit tests for the map text, layers, the autotile mask, sight, cover, routes, the pack and the facts;
    - the deck's crew walk their shift headless, every step one tile across an open edge, the sim hearing each arrival, and both reach the airlock after the alarm;
    - the deck played in the Play panel by mouse and keys and by touch at 360, 390 and sideways: drawn at a whole scale with only the palette's colours and the joins the mask gives, the crew routed through doors and never across a wall, the alarm by a click, a tap and the keyboard, the HUD's line on the hatch following them; and exported as one file played from `file://`;
    - a deck built from the starter tileset in the Scene panel at 1280 and 390, Space-Wrecked's text imported, then played;
    - stage2d.js, the precache and the editor's start within their budgets.
  - **Done** (2026-10-27; [specs/tilemaps.md](docs/specs/tilemaps.md#as-built-m72)):
    - **Records and checks:** `tileset`, an engine record type (a sheet, a tile size, tiles each with its character, frame and rules, and the frames edges and posts draw with); the `tilemap` component (its tileset, size, layers of rows and edges in Space-Wrecked's map text), whose rows and edges the inspector shows as text boxes a line each (a type file's list can now say `lines`). E092 is a tileset that can't be drawn: its sheet read to the PNG header, every frame a tile, its joins, an edge or the post draws on the sheet, and each tile its own one character. E093 is a tilemap that doesn't fit its tileset or its size, or a second in one space; W157 an actor on a cell it can't stand on, or off the map; E058 a tilemap in a 3D space. The legend moved onto the tiles as `char` while building, as a type file's map keys are names (D84).
    - **The core:** Space-Wrecked's map text read and written; sight, cover and four-way routes ported in whole numbers; the facts `world.sees` and `world.cover`. The pack is format 18 once a space has a tilemap: its cells' rules as hexadecimal digits, its edges and where each object stands, worked out by the compiler.
    - **The starter tileset** `deck` in the starter sprites: one 256 by 64 sheet of 1,228 bytes (floor, an airlock, a crate, a console and a locker; machinery and the hull with 16 joins each; a wall, the hull, a door and a hatch, across and down; a post), held to its generator byte for byte.
    - **The stage:** stage2d.js draws a tilemap's layers, autotiled by the 4-bit mask, then its edges and posts, culled to the view. Walkers route four ways round the walls and through the doors, tile centre to tile centre, carrying a frame's step past each; with no route they stay and never arrive. 19,061 bytes, 7,788 gzipped. Its hook gives the tilemap's frames as drawn and each walker's route.
    - **The editor:** Add → Tilemap… (a tileset and a size, at the view's centre); with a tilemap chosen, a Tiles row with the layer, the tiles as buttons drawn with their frames, Paint, Erase, Edges with a kind, Show rules and Import ASCII… (Space-Wrecked's map text or plain rows, the size grown, nothing changed while a character is no tile). A stroke is one step of undo, filled between points, and an edge stroke keeps to its first side's line. Above a tileset's form, its sheet with the tile grid. The hook gives the chosen tilemap.
    - **The deck** at `tools/cli/test/fixtures/deck/`: Space-Wrecked's example ship imported from its own text, 11 by 5 tiles of the starter tileset; stars in parallax; Ines and Oren on their rounds; an alarm panel whose use sends both to the airlock; the HUD's lines on the hatch by `world.sees`.
    - **The gate's checks:** `tools/cli/test/deck.test.ts` validates the deck clean, builds it as format 18 and breaks it once each for E092, E093, E058 and W157; `apps/editor/src/stage2d/deck.test.ts` walks the shift and the alarm headless, every step one tile across an open edge; `deck.spec.ts`, `deckPhone.spec.ts` (360, 390, sideways) and `deckExport.spec.ts` (one file from `file://`) play it, the deck's plating counted on the canvas; `deckEditor.spec.ts` builds a deck in the editor alone at 1280 and 390, Space-Wrecked's text imported, and plays it.
    - **A budget the Tiles row broke:** its buttons read the sheet through the asset browser's reader, whose edits brought the record index and the ink compiler into the Scene panel's chunk, 417 KiB of its 383. They now decode the bytes the 2D view already read: 371 KiB. stage2d.js is re-read at 7.6 KiB for the tile drawing and the routes, with 5% room.
    - **58 planted bugs,** 53 at the unit level and 5 through the built page; 12 survived at first, each now caught by a check written for it: E093's edge rows, edges with a tileset that has none and too many rows; the topmost layer's rule; E092 at the sheet's last frame, on edges and for a space as a character; sight across a north or south edge; cover from an attacker on the same row; a route to a tile that can't be walked, from that tile; a walker's step carried past a tile centre; and a stage started mid-walk, as after a load, sending its walkers straight through walls. That last one is why the walkers' start is now `walkersOf`, tested headless.
    - **Untested for feel** until you see the deck on your phone.
- **M7.3 Side-view rooms.** Floors with room slots, stairs and route-finding.
- **M7.4 Custom stages.** An API for renderers drawn in code (Canvas2D, SVG), so code-drawn styles keep working inside the engine.
- **M7.5 Kits.** Grid tactics (cover, overwatch, sight), ranked squad, tower defense (paths, waves, towers, fixed tick), base/colony builder.
- **M7.6 Templates.** Tactics, Squad roguelite, Tower defense, Side-view builder, Manager sim.
- **M7.7 Proof #3: Space-Wrecked's ship builder and a boarding fight,** rebuilt on the engine.

**Done when:** the proof passes its bots and goldens [auto] and plays on your phone [you].

## Phase 8: Advanced authoring

- Node-graph scripting, compiled to the same actions and effects as trigger sheets.
- A cutscene sequencer: a timeline for camera, animation, dialogue and audio.
- A behaviour-tree graph editor.
- Localization: ICU string tables, string extraction, pseudo-localization, a missing-key marker.
- Balance Lab dashboards: seed sweeps, A/B content overrides, confidence intervals.
- Achievements and platform services (Steam, Play).
- Electron/Steam export (from dirtbag's `desktop/`); store-asset capture; crash replay.

## Phase 9: Ecosystem

- Content packs with a load order, for DLC and mods (Bethesda-style overrides).
- A plugin API for third-party kits, with sandboxed scripts.
- A docs site, tutorials and a template gallery.
- Optional AI drafting of records, quests and dialogue with your own API key.
- Capacitor builds for iOS and Android, if a native build is needed.
- An Unreal hand-off: content as JSON for DataTables, plus RNG golden vectors.

## Phase 10: Art tools and a game made only in the editor → Proof #4

**Goal:** a whole game made in the editor alone, by following a tutorial, with nothing from outside it but the engine's own fonts (D40, your calls).

- **What "made here" allows** (D40):
  - art and sound from the editor's tools, or drawn by code written in its script editor;
  - the few OFL fonts the engine ships, which count as its own;
  - store builds in a GitHub workflow the editor writes (M4.10), since Bubblewrap, the Android SDK and Electron can't run in a browser.
- **Why these tools.** Dirtbag and Space-Wrecked already ship with no outside art or sound, but their art is code: 10.4k lines of Canvas2D painters in Dirtbag, and 6.6k lines for Space-Wrecked's 3D, portraits and icons. These tools are what drawing without code needs. Until they land, proofs #1 to #3 draw their art in code written in the script editor.
- **M10.1 Painter.**
  - Vector shapes and paths, gradients and noise fills, layers, palettes, and palette swaps for the time of day.
  - Parallax backgrounds, and pixel art on a grid.
  - Drawn at runtime or baked to images.
  - The styles to reach: Dirtbag's flat poster landscapes and Space-Wrecked's blueprint parts.
- **M10.2 Icons and type.**
  - An icon painter for app and UI icons, as PNG and ICO for M4.10's generated icons.
  - A glyph drawer for marks the fonts lack.
  - The engine's bundled OFL fonts, with their provenance.
- **M10.3 Figures and portraits.**
  - A 2D rig of jointed parts with automatic posing, like Dirtbag's people.
  - A layered portrait maker, like Space-Wrecked's crew, seeded so a roster can make faces.
- **M10.4 Music.** Music generated by mood and section beside M4.8's synth, like Dirtbag's six moods, with stems on the buses.
- **M10.5 Mesh kit.** Low-poly primitives, extrude and lathe, kitbashing, vertex colours and procedural materials, so 3D needs no glTF from outside.
- **M10.6 Proof #4: a new small game, made only in the editor.**
  - An original short game designed around a tutorial; its theme and genre are open question 7.
  - Built from an empty project by following the tutorial, which joins Phase 9's docs site.

**Done when:**

- CI follows the tutorial through the editor's own screens, from an empty project to the exported game. The tutorial's steps and the test are one list, so neither can drift from the other. [auto]
- No file in the project came from outside: each asset's provenance names the editor tool that made it, or it is one of the engine's fonts. [auto]
- Bots finish 500 seeded runs, and every quest step is reachable. [auto]
- It plays and installs from Pages on your phone. [you]
- Someone new to the editor follows the tutorial to the end, and the time it took is a reading. [you]

**Out of scope unless you revisit it:** multiplayer, console exports, photoreal rendering (that stays an Unreal job).

## Open questions

1. **Editor domain.** Settled 2026-10-17: `engine.rcjlabs.com` (D28).
2. **Engine licence.** chooser-of-the-dead is GPL code with its story and art reserved. Pick before any outside contributions; until then the repo has no licence, which means all rights reserved.
3. **Theme of proof #2.** Settled 2026-10-27: Kaldvík, a steading on Landnam's coast (D82).
4. **TWA package ids.** Settled 2026-10-23: `com.rcjlabs.<game>`; Dirtbag keeps `io.github.rcjlabs.dirtbag` (D60).
5. **Orientation on large screens.** A third-party report says SDK 36 apps can no longer lock orientation on screens ≥600dp. Verify before shipping a landscape-only game such as Space-Wrecked as a TWA.
6. **Reference phone for 3D.** D8 is judged on a mid-range phone, and the 3D frame-rate targets come from its reading. Which phone is it? Decide with the first spike reading.
7. **Proof #4's game.** A new small game made around the tutorial (D40). Its theme and genre are yours to pick before Phase 10, from what the art tools and kits by then can make.
